# Recommend answers for five blocking owner decisions

These five `OWNER_DECISION_REQUIRED` rows in `ADR-0013` all block
`IMPLEMENTATION_START`, all default to BLOCK, and all require an accepted ADR
with predeclared acceptance tests. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-001` is handled
separately — do not cover it.

**Premise: none of this is novel.** Established systems and literature already
answer each. Bring the proven answer with citations; do not design from scratch.
The owner is non-technical and needs a recommendation to accept or amend, not an
essay of options.

## The five

1. **`HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-003`** — exact transaction ownership across
   execution, evidence, permit, and work projections. *Which writes share one
   atomic boundary, and which are separate?*
2. **`HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-013`** — is Kanban a projection of canonical state,
   or are selected tables adapted behind work management?
3. **`HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-017`** — do Electron/desktop bootstrap applications
   remain after choosing the local web or TUI product path? Note `DEC-RANEX-020`
   (ADR-0006) already excludes desktop; check whether that settles it.
4. **`HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-019`** — are execution, gate, permit, and effect
   intent submodules of one strong-consistency Governed Execution context, or
   independently persistent contexts?
5. **`HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-020`** — activate event sourcing for the Execution
   aggregate, or not? Note the aggregate's replay and crash tests were rewritten
   on 2026-07-30 and now replay persisted journal bytes and kill a real process;
   read them before answering.

## Required per decision

- **Recommendation:** one concrete answer, stated plainly.
- **Why, from evidence:** cite prior art with URLs (Temporal, Axon, Marten,
  EventStoreDB, Argo, Airflow, DDD bounded-context guidance, CQRS projection
  patterns) and cite `path:line` for every repository claim.
- **What it costs:** what becomes harder or impossible. No free lunches.
- **Predeclared acceptance tests:** how the decision is proven satisfied.
- **Conflicts:** cite the accepted obligations it touches — `ADR-0003`,
  `ADR-0006` fixed decisions, `ADR-0007` module boundaries, `ADR-0012` readiness,
  `HERMES-PROMOTION-058` to `-065`, and §8.3 of
  `HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md`. A conflict is a finding.
- **What genuinely remains the owner's call**, phrased as one specific question.

## Context you must read

- `docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-...md` — the five rows verbatim.
- `docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §8.3 and
  surrounding state/journal sections.
- `docs/architecture/reviews/2026-07-30-kernel-tracer-adversarial-audit.md` — the
  verified defects and the four production controls for snapshot/journal
  agreement.
- `docs/architecture/reviews/artifacts/2026-07-30/agent-reports/priorart-B.md` —
  verified prior art; build on it, do not redo it.
- The kernel tracer in worktree `.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer` (branch
  `feature/kernel-tracer`, untracked `src/` and `tests/`).

## Non-negotiables

- Verify externally; cite a URL with version or date. Mark anything unverifiable
  as `UNVERIFIED` rather than asserting it. Recalled knowledge stated as fact
  fails this task.
- Read-only. Do not modify, create, or delete any repository file.
- Report every inference separately, marked as inference.
- Prefer the smallest choice that satisfies the accepted obligations. Do not
  propose a new context, artifact type, or dependency without showing why an
  existing one is insufficient.

## Output

One section per decision, in the order above, each with the six required
headings. Then `Inferences` and `Unverified`.

61:  1.3.0)** — `HERMES-PROMOTION-058` renamed
62-  `SHARED_IDENTITY_AND_CANONICAL_SERIALIZATION_DEFINE_KERNEL_RECORDS` to
63-  `CLEAN_KERNEL_PROVIDES_SHARED_IDENTITY_AND_CANONICAL_SERIALIZATION` and
64-  removed the unsupported `kernel records` scope.
65:  `HERMES-PROMOTION-059` replaced “whose state evolution is computed by a pure
66-  reducer without observable side effects” with “every Execution state
67-  transition is computed by its pure reducer.”
68-- **DEFECT 1** — `HERMES-PROMOTION-011` again requires removal of the Nous
69-  commercial subsystem, and `HERMES-PROMOTION-041` again requires removal of
--
84-  `HERMES-PROMOTION-012`, and `HERMES-PROMOTION-018` remove the unsupported
85-  `governed`, `accepted`, generalized domain-package, and `authority`
86-  qualifiers and restore their exact cited scopes.
87-- **DEFECT 5** — Phase 1 lines 1899–1912 remain represented by
88:  `HERMES-PROMOTION-058` through `HERMES-PROMOTION-065`, rather than by an
89-  exclusion-equivalence claim. Phase 0/0A activity and Phase 2–6 sequencing
90-  and exit material remain research-only and are not represented by promoted
91-  gates.
92-
--
97-1899–1912 define the clean kernel's binding structural inventory and behavior,
98-not a delivery schedule.
99-
100-This revision adds eight promoted provisions,
101:`HERMES-PROMOTION-058` through `HERMES-PROMOTION-065`:
102-
103-1. shared identity and canonical serialization;
104-2. the `Execution` aggregate and pure reducer;
105-3. canonical relational execution state and version;
--
110-   deterministic policy adapter;
111-7. architecture import tests before feature code; and
112-8. the replay, crash-boundary, and no-Hermes-import exit gate.
113-
114:It also adds `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-020` as an
115:`OWNER_DECISION_REQUIRED` row for whether qualified `Execution` event sourcing
116-is activated, narrows `HERMES-RESEARCH-ONLY-008` to Phases 0/0A, and
117-adds `HERMES-RESEARCH-ONLY-013` for Phases 2–6.
118-Phase 1 is no longer included in a research-only sequencing disposition.

138-context, but lines 1901–1911 define required parts, required behavior,
139-construction precedence, and a falsifiable gate. They are not treated as a
140-calendar, delivery estimate, or optional schedule.
141-
142:`OWNER_DECISION_REQUIRED` has one precise meaning here: the choice is registered
143-but not selected by this ADR. Its `owner_decision_ref` and `default` remain
144-null, absence yields `BLOCK`, and activation without the exact accepted
145-decision is `DENIED`. The documentation-contract validator passes when that
146-fail-closed definition is intact; it does not convert an unresolved owner

docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md
4-|---|---|
5-| ADR ID | `ADR-0003` |
6-| Version | `1.0.0` |
7:| Status | `ACCEPTED` |
8-| Decision owner | Human owner |
9-| Decision date | 2026-07-28 |
10-| Effective revision | Working tree based on `4baad4a758f70d39af6a21e73488c61db5f82f32` |
11-| Content binding | Exact digest is recorded externally in each immutable review/release source manifest |
12:| Affected contexts | All Ranex bounded contexts, authority records, evidence records, repository layout, adapters, and migration work |
13-| RFC | Not required; closes the owner-directed architecture contract |
14-| Supersedes | None |
15-| Review/expiry date | First target-mode tracer, then on any authority-boundary change |
--
20-
21-The
22-[full-system architecture](../HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md)
23:is accepted as Ranex's normative target architecture. Acceptance means that the
24-paper contract is the construction destination. It does **not** mean that the
25-current branch has passed fork preflight, executable-contract validation,
26-security qualification, recovery drills, performance objectives, or a
27-target-mode runtime tracer.
28-
29:Ranex is one release-pinned modular monolith with explicit bounded-context
30:packages. The only canonical context source path is:
31-
32-```text
33-src/ranex/<context>/
--
42-
43-| Authoritative record or decision | Sole owner | Explicit non-owners |
44-|---|---|---|
45:| `WorkItemStatus` and legal work-item transition | `work_management` | Runs, boards, models, agent assignments, review, assurance |
46:| `RunStatus`, run reducer, workflow node, gate binding, authority unit of work | `governed_execution` | Worker fleet, policy, assurance, models |
47:| `ConsumableAuthorityGrant`, `Permit`, permit consumption, effect intent/outcome/reconciliation | `governed_execution` | Policy, identity, adapters, workers |
48-| Rules, risk derivation, authorization snapshot, `HumanDecisionRecord` policy requirements | `policy` | Models, workers, delivery channels |
49-| Principal authentication, challenges, session/device binding, secret handles | `identity_access` | Policy, workers, delivery channels |
50:| Claims, `EvidenceEnvelope`, qualified `CheckerResult`, `EvidenceSnapshot`, `GateEvaluation` | `assurance` | Reviewers, governed execution, process assurance |
51:| `ReviewSpecification`, `ReviewRequest`, `AnalysisAttempt`, `ReviewObservation`, `ReviewVerdict`, `IndependenceEvaluation` | `analytical_review` | Assurance, governed execution, process assurance |
52-| SDLC conformance audits, capability assessments, corrective actions, fleet experiments | `process_assurance` | Runtime gate evaluator, workers |
53-
54-`analytical_review` may publish immutable references to observations.
55:`assurance` may ingest those references as candidate evidence, qualify them,
56:and create an exact-subject evidence snapshot and `GateEvaluation`; it may not
57:rewrite or co-own the underlying `ReviewObservation`. `governed_execution` may
58-atomically bind a fresh passing `GateEvaluation` to a run and consume a valid
59:permit; it may not author the evaluation.
60-
61-An authenticated human decision is an input to policy and assurance where the
62-governing rule requires judgment. It is never represented as a machine
63-`GateOutcome=PASS`. Policy may establish eligibility for an authority grant,
64:but only `governed_execution` issues and consumes the grant/permit inside the
65:authority transaction.
66-
67-## Architectural standing
68-
69-The target has three independently reported maturity states:
70-
71:1. **Paper contract:** accepted by this ADR.
72:2. **Executable contract:** pending until canonical registries, schemas,
73-   generators, and drift checks pass `AI-G2`.
74-3. **Runtime qualification:** pending until fork preflight and the applicable
75-   structural, behavioral, security, recovery, and operating gates pass against
--
82-## Alternatives considered
83-
84-1. **Keep the architecture conditionally selected until runtime exists.**
85:   Rejected because it leaves constructors without one accepted destination;
86-   implementation maturity is tracked separately instead.
87-2. **Use `src/ranex/contexts/<context>`.** Rejected because it conflicts with
88-   the complete target repository map and adds a redundant physical layer.
89:3. **Let each workflow component own its local gate or permit.** Rejected
90:   because duplicated authority permits disagreement and bypass.
91:4. **Let assurance own review observations.** Rejected because evidence
92-   qualification and independent analytical judgment are distinct lifecycles.
93-5. **Make every context a service.** Rejected because the local-first product
94-   does not justify distributed failure, consistency, deployment, and
--
96-6. **Use one shared domain package.** Rejected because it erases ownership and
97-   makes authority writes difficult to constrain mechanically.
98-
99:## Fitness functions and acceptance evidence
100-
101-| ID | Required result |
102-|---|---|
103-| `FF-PATH-001` | The path registry, architecture tree, generated packages, import rules, and source tree contain only `src/ranex/<context>/` for context packages. |
104-| `FF-AUTH-001` | The lifecycle/data-ownership registries assign every authority-bearing type to exactly one owner and reject duplicate or missing owners. |
105:| `FF-AUTH-002` | Static imports plus integration tests prove that only `governed_execution` writes its authority tables and only `work_management` transitions work items. |
106-| `FF-EVID-001` | A review observation cannot directly produce `PASS`; only an `assurance` evaluator can create a subject-bound `GateEvaluation`. |
107:| `FF-PERMIT-001` | Stale, reused, wrong-subject, revoked, expired, or concurrently consumed grants/permits fail atomically. |
108-| `FF-LIFE-001` | Model/property tests cover every legal and illegal work/run transition, including block/resume, terminality, retry, and cancellation. |
109-| `FF-FORK-001` | `SDLC-FORK-000` binds ancestry, license, provenance, selected adoption strategy, and immutable baseline before runtime implementation. |
110-| `FF-BOUNDARY-001` | Dependency-graph checks report no forbidden edge, cycle, compatibility leak, or adapter-to-domain authority mutation. |
--
129-## Consequences
130-
131-- Construction has one target path, context granularity, and record owner.
132:- The architecture can be accepted without overstating current runtime
133-  maturity.
134-- Cross-context writes require commands/events or the named atomic authority
135:  transaction, not shared-table convenience.
136-- Contract registries and tests are mandatory before implementation readiness
137-  can be claimed.
138-
--
146-
147-Rollback of an implementation restores the last qualified implementation; it
148-does not silently revoke this architecture decision. Reversal requires a
149:superseding owner-accepted ADR.
150-
151-## Human approval
152-
153-The human owner directed the team to close architectural ambiguity, use mature
154-engineering practice, and establish an enterprise-build-ready paper contract.
155:This accepted ADR records that architectural closure. It is not a
156:cryptographic signature, runtime permit, fork-preflight result, or production
157-approval.

276-| ID | Required result |
277-|---|---|
278:| `FF-SDLC-001` | Every execution/activity maps to one Core-SDLC work item/state and no subordinate lifecycle can transition it. |
279-| `FF-REF-001` | Each reference/profile has an exact registered source, applicability, limitation, required behavior, deviation, and verification mapping. |
280-| `FF-PACKET-001` | A sealed packet is deterministic from pinned inputs and invalidates when a dependency changes. |
281-| `FF-GUIDE-001` | No active normative link, packet, generator, or route treats the retired guide as construction authority. |
--
283-| `FF-EFFECT-001` | Each effect declares attempt semantics, idempotency key, durable intent, outcome query, and reconciliation path. |
284-| `FF-SUPPLY-001` | Release manifest pins dependencies/providers and passes provenance, license, vulnerability, support, and exit checks. |
285-| `FF-EXCL-001` | Source, routes, packages, runtime, network, and SBOM contain no excluded surface. |
286:| `FF-DELIVERY-001` | Every channel uses the same command, challenge, decision, permit, authentication, replay, and audit contracts. |
287-| `FF-PRIV-001` | Consent, minimization, classification, retention, deletion, and egress tests pass for personal/media data. |
288-| `FF-LANDING-001` | Only the authenticated human landing path can integrate the exact reviewed/verified head, with rollback prepared. |
289-
290:The compiler rejects a fixed decision if its referenced fitness evidence is
291-missing, stale, for another subject, or reports a blocking non-pass outcome.
292:The decision remains accepted while implementation readiness remains pending.
293-
294-## Alternatives considered
295-
docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md
4-|---|---|
5-| ADR ID | `ADR-0007` |
6-| Version | `1.0.0` |
7:| Status | `ACCEPTED` |
8-| Decision owner | Human owner |
9-| Decision date | 2026-07-28 |
10:| Effective revision | Working tree based on `4baad4a67843b02d5970f442fb54aed8d6525dda`; executable projection and enactment pending |
11-| Content binding | Exact digest is recorded externally in each immutable review/release source manifest |
12:| Affected contexts | Entire repository, every bounded context, adapters, tests, schemas, migrations, deployment, tooling, documentation, and compatibility code |
13-| Supersedes | No ADR; narrows and completes architecture §§11–13 |
14:| Review/expiry date | First contract-registry projection, first target context tracer, then on topology/ownership change |
15-| Compatibility/migration class | Target organization with strangler quarantine for inherited Hermes layout |
16-| Security/data class | Public architecture decision |
17-
18-## Decision
19-
20-Ranex uses a modular monolith whose primary organizing unit is the owned
21:bounded context, not a framework, adapter technology, horizontal “services”
22:folder, or model/tool vendor. The canonical context path remains:
23-
24-```text
25-src/ranex/<context>/
26-```
27-
28-The package name makes product/domain intent visible. The architecture
29:registries, not directory inference, decide which names are bounded contexts.
30-Folder count does not prove modularity, and every context does not need every
31-optional folder.
32-
33:## Canonical repository topology
34-
35-```text
36-ranex/
37-├── src/ranex/
38-│   ├── foundation/                 # semantics-light shared primitives
39:│   ├── <context>/                  # one registered bounded context
40:│   ├── modules/                    # first-party capability modules
41-│   ├── adapters/                   # multi-context/host-edge adapters only
42-│   ├── compatibility/              # anti-corruption boundary only
43-│   ├── bootstrap/                  # composition root and startup
44-│   └── migration/                  # cross-context migration ordering
45-├── apps/                           # delivery applications; no authority rules
46-├── architecture/contracts/         # owner-authored semantic registries
47:├── architecture/generated/         # generated projections; never hand edited
48:├── schemas/<owning-area>/           # canonical wire/artifact schemas
49-├── packages/generated-contracts/   # generated language bindings
50-├── tests/                           # ADR-0008 taxonomy, mirroring owners
51-├── config/                          # release-pinned declarative profiles
--
53-├── docs/
54-│   ├── architecture/               # normative architecture and specifications
55-│   │   ├── decisions/              # ADRs
56:│   │   └── rfcs/                   # non-accepted proposals
57:│   ├── research/                   # evidence/advice, never authority
58-│   └── operations/                 # tested runbooks
59-├── scripts/                        # thin public-API operator/CI clients
60-├── tools/                          # build, contract generation, validation
--
67-Configuration that changes behavior remains versioned, schema-validated, owned,
68-and tested.
69-
70:## Bounded-context internal contract
71-
72-```text
73-src/ranex/<context>/
74-├── __init__.py
75-├── README.md                 # vocabulary, owner, invariants, navigation
76:├── contract.yaml             # generated/validated context-registry projection
77-├── api/                      # sole cross-context import surface
78-├── domain/                   # aggregates, value objects, domain events/rules
79-├── application/              # use cases, command/query handlers
--
82-```
83-
84-Only folders required by enacted behavior are created. `contract.yaml` is a
85:projection of the canonical registries, not a second hand-maintained source.
86-A context that has no local adapter does not receive an empty `adapters/`;
87-shared host mechanics do not justify identical boilerplate in every context.
88-
89-### Placement rules
90-
91:| Concept | Canonical placement | Rule |
92-|---|---|---|
93:| Aggregate/root/entity | `<context>/domain/<domain_concept>.py` or a cohesive domain subpackage | Aggregate root owns invariants and legal mutation; one aggregate transaction does not imply cross-context atomicity |
94-| Value object | Co-located with its sole aggregate or `<context>/domain/<concept>.py` when reused inside that context | Immutable and validated at construction; never a database/wire model |
95-| Domain service | `<context>/domain/<named_domain_operation>.py` | Pure domain decision spanning concepts; no I/O, repository, clock read, policy client, or orchestration |
96-| Application use case | `<context>/application/<verb_noun>.py` or a cohesive use-case subpackage | Orchestrates domain objects and ports; owns no adapter/framework rule |
97-| Repository/capability port | `<context>/application/ports/<owned_capability>.py` | Protocol expressed in owner language; application owns it, adapter implements it |
98:| Public command/query/event/view | `<context>/api/` | Stable, versioned, immutable boundary type; the only source import permitted from another context |
99-| Context-exclusive adapter | `<context>/adapters/<technology>/` | Translates external/ORM/wire types and implements one or more named local ports |
100-| Multi-context or host-edge adapter | `src/ranex/adapters/<boundary>/<technology>/` | Allowed only when the integration is genuinely shared or is a delivery/composition edge; contains no domain rule |
101-| Persistence row/ORM model | Owning persistence adapter | Never imported by domain/API consumers |
102:| Integration event/outbox mapping | Owner API plus owner persistence adapter | Event schema is public; durable mapping/outbox mechanics remain adapter-owned |
103-
104-A “domain service” is not a home for miscellaneous logic. A use case is not a
105:god orchestrator. Split a module when it has multiple owners/reasons to change,
106-mixes public/private responsibilities, creates a forbidden dependency, or
107-cannot be tested through a narrow contract. Ranex adopts no arbitrary
108-lines-per-file, classes-per-folder, or one-type-per-file gate.
--
130-2. A context's `domain` imports only its own domain and `foundation`.
131-3. Its `application` imports its own `domain`, `application/ports`,
132-   `foundation`, and another context's public `api` only when the registered
133:   dependency permits it.
134-4. Its `api` may expose owner-defined DTOs/enums and immutable views; it does
135-   not export domain mutability, ORM objects, adapter types, or service
136-   locators.
--
149-   factories may assemble internal objects but cannot select global policy,
150-   routes, credentials, or alternate business behavior.
151-
152:No test, script, module, extension, or compatibility path receives a broader
153-dependency rule than production code.
154-
155-## Cross-context state and messaging
--
157-- A context owns its aggregates, tables, migrations, public contracts, and
158-  integration events even when SQLite is physically shared.
159-- Synchronous calls use the owning context's public API and remain outside the
160:  callee's transaction.
161:- State-change propagation uses versioned integration events, a transactional
162:  owner outbox, idempotent consumers, correlation/causation IDs, and explicit
163-  duplicate/out-of-order/reconciliation behavior.
164-- Cross-context database reads/writes, foreign repository use, shared mutable
165:  models, and distributed-transaction assumptions are prohibited.
166:- `governed_execution` remains the specifically documented authority-cell
167-  consistency boundary; its centrality does not authorize knowledge or writes
168-  owned by another context.
169-
--
173-  `tests/unit/<context>/domain/` and
174-  `tests/unit/<context>/application/`; contract/integration paths name the
175-  owning context and port/adapter.
176:- Canonical semantic registries live in `architecture/contracts`; JSON Schema
177-  lives once under `schemas/<owning-area>`; generated bindings live under
178-  `packages/generated-contracts`; generated documentation lives under
179-  `architecture/generated` or an explicitly generated docs subtree.
--
185-  `src/ranex/<context>/adapters/persistence/sqlite/migrations/`.
186-  `src/ranex/migration` owns only ordering, compatibility, application,
187-  verification, and rollback across those manifests.
188:- Shared SQLite connection/transaction mechanics may live in a named platform
189-  adapter; they cannot own context table definitions or silently coordinate
190-  cross-context writes.
191-
--
194-- `deploy/` contains declarative package/install/service/host-profile assets.
195-  Runtime behavior remains in production adapters and release management.
196-- `docs/operations/` contains versioned runbooks linked to tested commands and
197:  recovery evidence.
198-- `scripts/` contains thin authenticated clients of public APIs; no repository,
199:  adapter, secret, migration, or authority shortcut is permitted.
200-- `tools/` contains developer/build/codegen/validation programs and fixtures;
201-  it is not installed as product authority unless explicitly packaged behind a
202-  production port.
203:- Accepted decisions live only in `docs/architecture/decisions/`. RFCs live in
204-  `docs/architecture/rfcs/` and remain non-normative until an ADR accepts them.
205-
206-## Ownership, discovery, naming, and navigation
--
210-- one semantic owner/context;
211-- one accountable human owner role and required reviewer role;
212-- allowed dependency edges and data classification;
213:- generated/manual/legacy status; and
214-- applicable topology/test rules.
215-
216:That registry generates a CODEOWNERS-ready projection. CODEOWNERS may request
217-review but never becomes semantic ownership or transition authority.
218-Overlapping patterns with different owners are `CONFLICT`.
219-
--
221-docs, tools, `legacy`, worktrees, and generated TypeScript are excluded.
222-Context packages use `snake_case` registry IDs and explicit `__init__.py`
223-files. Import-time scanning/registration is prohibited; the release catalog
224:discovers modules/adapters from manifests at the composition root.
225-
226-Names use domain or use-case language. Unqualified dumping grounds such as
227-`utils.py`, `common.py`, `helpers.py`, `manager.py`, and `service.py` fail
228-review unless the registry records one narrow responsibility. Every context
229-README links owner, vocabulary, public API, invariants, dependency diagram,
230:data/migrations, operations, and tests; it does not duplicate canonical
231-contracts.
232-
233-## Compatibility and legacy quarantine
234-
235-Inherited Hermes source remains in its upstream layout during the strangler.
236-Only `ranex.compatibility.hermes_legacy` may import it. After parity and
237:upstream-sync-cost evidence, a frozen subset may move to `legacy/hermes/`.
238-Neither location is part of package discovery for new Ranex code. New domain
239-rules, schemas, migrations, or shared helpers may not be added to legacy.
240-
--
248-topology_rule_set: "RANEX-TOPOLOGY-1.0"
249-rules:
250-  - {id: "ORG-PATH-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Each registered context root is exactly src/ranex/<context>/."}
251:  - {id: "ORG-CONTEXT-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Every context has one owner, public API, contract projection, and no second semantic home."}
252-  - {id: "ORG-LAYER-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Domain/application/port/adapter files match the placement and inward-dependency rules."}
253-  - {id: "ORG-PUBLIC-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Cross-context source imports resolve only through the target context api package."}
254-  - {id: "ORG-DEPENDENCY-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Actual imports are a subset of registered allowed edges."}
255-  - {id: "ORG-CYCLE-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "The context graph and source import graph are acyclic."}
256-  - {id: "ORG-IMPORT-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Imports are effect-free and do not perform runtime registration or configuration decisions."}
257-  - {id: "ORG-COMPOSE-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Only bootstrap/composition.py selects and wires concrete product implementations."}
258:  - {id: "ORG-MESSAGE-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Cross-context state propagation uses public APIs or registered integration events/outboxes, never table/repository sharing."}
259-  - {id: "ORG-PERSIST-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Each table and migration has one context owner; shared persistence mechanics own no domain schema."}
260-  - {id: "ORG-TEST-MIRROR-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Every production owner has tests in an ADR-0008 allowed root with matching context/layer metadata."}
261-  - {id: "ORG-GENERATED-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Schemas and generated artifacts have one semantic source, generator/input digests, and zero hand-edit drift."}
262-  - {id: "ORG-MIGRATION-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Context migrations are owner-local and cross-context ordering is explicit, tested, and reversible."}
263-  - {id: "ORG-LEGACY-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Only the compatibility boundary imports inherited/legacy roots; new Ranex packages never do."}
264-  - {id: "ORG-OWNERSHIP-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Every governed path resolves to one CODEOWNERS-ready semantic owner and required reviewer."}
265:  - {id: "ORG-DISCOVERY-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Package/module discovery is manifest-driven, side-effect-free, and excludes tests/docs/tools/legacy."}
266-  - {id: "ORG-NAV-001", enforcement: "REQUIRED", invariant: "Context README and registry links make owner, API, invariants, data, dependencies, operations, and tests findable."}
267-  - {id: "ORG-EXEMPTION-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Every topology exception is exact-path, exact-rule, owned, justified, time-bounded, and non-transitive."}
268-```

303:## Fitness evidence
304-
305:| ID | Required evidence |
306-|---|---|
307-| `FF-ORG-001` | Registered and actual paths agree; unknown/duplicate owners and misplaced packages fail. |
308-| `FF-ORG-002` | Static import graph proves allowed inward/public edges, no cycles/private imports, and no legacy leak. |
--
310-| `FF-ORG-004` | Composition tests build each release profile from the sole root and reject missing/extra/ambiguous bindings. |
311-| `FF-ORG-005` | Contract and failure tests prove public API/event translation, duplicate/out-of-order handling, and no shared-table shortcut. |
312-| `FF-ORG-006` | Migration inventory maps every table/version to one owner and passes ordering, replay, rollback, and restore checks. |
313:| `FF-ORG-007` | Registry-to-CODEOWNERS/package-discovery/generated-doc projections are reproducible and drift-free. |
314-| `FF-ORG-008` | Representative changes remain local to the declared owner or expose an explicit architecture decision instead of hidden coupling. |

316-These are obligations. No current source tree or build is declared conformant.

350-orthogonality, trade-off analysis, and continuous fitness checks. They do not
351-prove one folder taxonomy, DDD correctness, the selected context count, or a
352-microservice boundary. APOSD depth is a consumer-relative qualitative lens,
353:not permission for god modules or a scalar score, and information hiding
354-cannot conceal authority, security, failure, recovery, or operational facts.
355-Clean Architecture needs explicit quality-attribute
356-trade-offs; FSA ratings are not Ranex scores; the retained Code Complete source
357-is Ch.5 only; Clean Code and Pragmatic examples are contextual and
358-edition-sensitive. Ownership, dependency, consistency, change-locality,
359:failure, security, data, and runtime evidence remain decisive.

docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md
4-|---|---|
5-| ADR ID | `ADR-0012` |
6-| Version | `1.1.0` |
7:| Status | `ACCEPTED` |
8-| Decision owner | Human owner |
9-| Decision date | 2026-07-29 |
10-| Effective revision | Definition-only working tree; no readiness assessment or authorization is claimed |
11-| Content binding | Exact digest is recorded externally in each immutable review/release source manifest |
12:| Affected contexts | `process_assurance`, `assurance`, `policy`, `work_management`, `governed_execution`, `configuration_management`, `provenance_compliance`, `migration`, `release_management`, `operations`, and every context supplying readiness evidence |
13-| RFC | Not required; resolves the human owner's readiness-language and bootstrap-authority decision |
14:| Supersedes | Ambiguous uses of “build ready,” “enterprise build ready,” and “runtime ready” in earlier documents; it does not weaken any evidence, authority, security, recovery, or production gate |
15:| Review/expiry date | Review on any readiness tier, gate, evidence role, reviewer route, architecture-rule denominator, production-admission, or authority-boundary change |
16-| Compatibility/migration class | New typed readiness namespace; historical unqualified readiness statements become nonauthoritative prose until mapped to this contract |
17:| Security/data class | Public decision and gate metadata; referenced source, review, security, operational, and production evidence retains its own classification |
18-
19-## Revision history
20-
21-| Version | Date | Change and rationale |
22-|---|---|---|
23-| `1.1.0` | 2026-07-29 | Reconciled the readiness lifecycle with the shared state/transition conventions: registered `READINESS-STATE-1.0` in the main state catalog, changed guard identifiers to underscore form, and bound transition facts to the standard `architecture/contracts/states.json` catalog. This corrects the prior cross-contract incompatibility without changing or versioning `TransitionEventV1` and declares no readiness tier. |
24:| `1.0.0` | 2026-07-29 | Initial accepted definition-only readiness contract. |
25-
26-## Decision
27-
28-Ranex has two noncompensating readiness tiers with different authority and
29:evidence boundaries:
30-
31:| Canonical machine state | Documentation label | What it permits | What it does not claim |
32-|---|---|---|---|
33:| `IMPLEMENTATION_START_READY` | `DESIGN_DEFINITION_READY` | Admission of staged product implementation under the normal per-work-item packet, gate, grant, permit, TDD, review, and landing controls | Enacted product runtime, production safety, operational effectiveness, mature capability scores, release, deployment, or user impact |
34:| `PRODUCTION_READY` | `ENTERPRISE_RUNTIME_READY` | Eligibility to request the normal exact-subject release/deployment authority chain after runtime and operational readiness passes | A deployment effect, release permit, waiver, permanent qualification, or proof of healthy operation after change |
35-
36-The documentation labels are explanatory labels, not additional states or
37:accepted aliases. Machine records use only the canonical states. A document
38-that says only “build ready,” “enterprise ready,” or “runtime ready” grants no
39-authority.
40-
41:`IMPLEMENTATION_START_READY` is deliberately attainable before full runtime
42-enactment. Runtime rule results and capability effectiveness may remain
43-`NOT_ASSESSED`, but that fact must be explicit, cannot be represented as
44-`PASS`, and cannot be omitted from the assessment. This prevents absent runtime
45:evidence from circularly blocking the start of staged implementation while
46-also preventing a paper contract from masquerading as a production system.
47-
48:`PRODUCTION_READY` requires enacted runtime producers, complete current rule
49:results, operational and recovery evidence, applicable evidence-bound
50-capability assessments, and the normal human authority chain. An
51-implementation-start result cannot compensate for any missing production
52:evidence.
53-
54-Neither tier is currently declared. This ADR accepts the vocabulary,
55-state machine, gate contracts, and authority boundary only.
56-
57-## Bootstrap without circular authority
58-
59:Before `IMPLEMENTATION_START_READY`, one bounded
60:`PRE_READINESS_TOOLING_TRACER` lane may create only the evidence needed to
61-evaluate that tier:
62-
63-- compiler, generator, validator, schema, fixture, manifest, and deterministic
64-  review-harness changes on registered architecture/tooling paths;
65-- fork-preservation, provenance, clean-worktree, and source-manifest work;
66-- one real current-subject ADR-0008 cycle and its separately produced
67:  `SUCCEEDED` `LandingRecord` and sealing evidence; and
68-- read-only review and finding-reconciliation artifacts.
69-
70-Every tracer change still needs an exact work item, bounded task packet,
--

   174	## 2. Root architecture decision
   175	
   176	The product is a local-first, one-host, batteries-included modular monolith.
   177	Its differentiator is governed execution of probabilistic workers, not the
   178	number of models or tools it can call.
   179	
   180	```text
   181	Hermes inheritance:
   182	  mutable agent loop
   183	    -> tools, providers, approvals, memory, plugins, jobs, gateways, state
   184	
   185	Ranex target:
   186	  deterministic governed-execution authority
   187	    -> policy and exact-subject authorization
   188	    -> durable state, journal, evidence bindings, permit, and outbox
   189	    -> typed activity/effect dispatch
   190	    -> replaceable reasoning workers, reviewers, tools, and delivery surfaces
   191	```
   192	
   193	The controlling assertion is:
   194	
   195	> **Ranex owns control, cross-worker orchestration, authority, canonical state,
   196	> and proof. Official typed runtimes supply bounded leaf execution. Hermes
   197	> supplies attributed provenance, characterization, translation, and temporary
   198	> non-inference compatibility only.**
   199	
   200	This assertion is an owner requirement and architecture target. Clean extraction
   201	remains a P0 implementation proof obligation.
   202	
   203	## 3. Fixed decisions
   204	
   205	The stable IDs, alternatives, owners, governing ADRs, and fitness functions for
   206	all 29 rows are machine-checkably registered in
   207	[ADR-0006](./decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md).
   208	This prose table is the readable projection; a mismatch is `CONFLICT`.
   209	
   210	| Decision | Canonical position |
   211	|---|---|
   212	| Product form | One release-pinned modular monolith, not a microservice fleet. |
   213	| Development process | The accepted Core SDLC is the governing process; AI-agent L0–L12 is a subordinate worker protocol. |
   214	| Engineering references | The frozen SWEBOK/book corpus is a major practice base under the Core SDLC hierarchy; it closes unclear work but cannot override policy or become authority. |
   215	| Engineering-practice application | Every architecture and implementation packet binds a machine-registered applicability profile, required behavior, deviations, and verification evidence; citation without demonstrated application is insufficient. |
   216	| Legacy implementation guide | Deleted and retired by `ADR-0002`; historical references grant no construction authority and new route plans derive from this architecture. |
   217	| Upstream relationship | Ranex is a Hermes-derived software fork; lineage/history/license proof is a blocking preflight, and GitHub network-fork status is tracked separately. |
   218	| New core | Authority, domain, and application code outside the named compatibility adapter has no dependency on inherited Hermes internals. |
   219	| Migration | Strangler migration inside the fork; no big-bang rewrite. |
   220	| Work lifecycle authority | `work_management` owns `WorkItemStatus`; boards, runs, models, and Hermes sessions do not. |
   221	| Canonical authority | One `governed_execution` consistency cell owns run transitions, gate bindings, permit consumption, and effect intent. |
   222	| State storage | One physical SQLite authority database for the local target, with logical context ownership, an append-only journal, and a transactional outbox. |
   223	| Event sourcing | Selective replay journal for governed execution; the whole product is not event sourced. |
   224	| Workflow runtime | A runtime port with a local durable runner as the product default; another engine may implement the same port after an ADR and parity tests. |
   225	| Effects | At-least-once or at-most-once attempt semantics with idempotency and reconciliation; never claim exactly once. |
   226	| Policy failure | Deny visibly. Missing, stale, malformed, unavailable, or conflicting blocking proof cannot pass. |
   227	| Model authority | Models produce proposals and observations. They cannot emit an accepted transition, gate decision, permit, waiver, or human decision. |
   228	| AI-worker fleet | Ranex control services alone orchestrate deterministic bounded fan-out/join; every model/harness is a leaf worker; each assignment receives a task-minimal proper subset of its role ceiling; writes are isolated and landing is human-controlled. |
   229	| First-party capabilities | Shipped with Ranex behind stable internal interfaces; not user-installed prerequisites. |
   230	| External extensions | Lower-trust, out-of-process, capability-scoped, and permanently outside the authority path. |
   231	| Desktop app | Excluded from the Ranex target. No Electron desktop, desktop bootstrap, or desktop updater. |
   232	| Local UX | CLI, TUI, loopback-only web dashboard, GitHub edge, and a text-phone delivery port. |
   233	| Phone implementation | Telegram is the first mapped text-phone adapter; other channels implement the same delivery/auth contracts. |
   234	| Voice | Mapped as an optional media/transcription adapter, inactive unless a future accepted decision requires it. It never enters the kernel. |
   235	| Public dashboard | Excluded. The web dashboard binds to loopback; private tailnet publication is an explicit delivery adapter and policy decision. |
   236	| Providers | One explicit provider/model/official-runtime route per assignment; eligible local individual subscription and product API/BYOK/cloud routes are distinct. No adapter/provider/model fallback or auxiliary model call. |
   237	| Nous commercial product | Hermes/Nous is provenance, reference, and non-inference compatibility only: no live inference, parent-agent loop, Portal/model route, credential/entitlement, billing, credits, subscriptions, managed tool pool, purchase, promotion, or fallback. |
   238	| Remote model catalog | Cannot activate or mutate routes. The qualified catalog is release-pinned and cannot introduce provider subagents, fallback, or auxiliary calls. |
   239	| Risk | Deterministic policy derives risk; worker-supplied risk can only be an untrusted observation. |
   240	| Merge | Human-controlled landing until a later accepted decision changes the policy. |
   241	
   242	## 4. Non-negotiable invariants
   243	
   244	1. Only `governed_execution` chooses and commits a legal canonical run state.
   245	2. Only the nonreplaceable application policy-enforcement point authorizes an
   246	   effect.
   247	3. Every target-mode effect crosses `CapabilityBus`; no special tool or agent
   248	   path bypasses it.
   249	4. Transitional inherited harnesses are described honestly as activity-boundary
   250	   plus OS-sandbox mediated until their real tool paths pass the bypass matrix.
   251	5. One transaction commits the run version/current row, ordered journal record,
   252	   consumed permit or decision, and outbound effect intent.
   253	6. Kanban, dashboards, Hermes session state, and external systems are projections
   254	   or adapter state, never alternative completion authorities.
   255	7. Every authority and evidence record binds to the exact project, work item,
   256	   run, activity, workspace, base commit, candidate commit, packet digest,
   257	   workflow version, policy snapshot, module profile, and aggregate version
   258	   required by its contract.
   259	8. A maker cannot approve its own subject.
   260	9. A human waiver remains a waiver and never becomes machine `PASS`.
   261	10. Unknown action, role, capability, route, schema, state, or dependency is
   262	    denied by default.
   263	11. A module cannot qualify, grant, activate, promote, quarantine, or retire
   264	    itself.
   265	12. A route cannot establish its own identity, capability profile, independence,
   266	    or qualification.
   267	13. The reducer performs no I/O and reads no wall clock, randomness, environment,
   268	    process global, database, filesystem, network, provider, or Hermes state.
   269	14. The same workflow/interpreter versions and ordered recorded inputs replay to
   270	    the same state and commands.
   271	15. Every retry preserves the same logical idempotency identity or becomes a new
   272	    explicitly related attempt.
   273	16. `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` is a first-class result and must enter reconciliation.
   274	17. Importing a module causes no registration, environment read, file access,
   275	    network access, thread creation, migration, or other side effect.
   276	18. No external Python plugin runs inside the authority process.
   277	19. Secrets are opaque handles until resolved at the authorized adapter edge.
   278	20. Raw prompts, source, and model output are classified artifacts, not routine
   279	    telemetry.
   280	21. Cross-project data does not enter packets, prompts, workspaces, artifacts,
   281	    logs, learned knowledge, or route-training data without explicit sanitized
   282	    transfer authority.
   283	22. Every upstream synchronization reruns the same de-commercialization,
   284	    architecture, provenance, security, and release gates as a product release.
   285	23. A worker run, merge, deployment, incident, or model observation cannot
   286	    directly advance the canonical Core-SDLC work item; `work_management`
   287	    evaluates the named transition contract and accountable decisions.
   288	24. L0–L12 worker activities, `AI-G*` evidence gates, `MAP-*` map gates, and
   289	    `SDLC-ADOPT-*` process-adoption gates are typed namespaces, not alternative
   290	    work-item states.
   291	25. Assignment claims and lease renewal are compare-and-swap operations with a
   292	    monotonically increasing fencing epoch; an expired or superseded worker
   293	    cannot write, spend, submit an eligible result, or request an effect.
   294	26. Worker liveness, planning, collaboration messages, model consensus, and
   295	    queue completion never prove semantic progress or transfer authority.
   296	27. Prompt instructions may configure a worker but never replace tool-boundary
   297	    permissions, workspace isolation, budget enforcement, gates, or permits.
   298	28. Every Ranex-created descendant assignment and every model/tool operation is
   299	    charged transitively to its reservation ancestry, and verification/human
   300	    capacity applies admission backpressure before assurance is reduced.
   301	29. Ranex control services are the sole cross-worker orchestrator. A leaf
   302	    harness may run a bounded in-role tool loop but cannot create assignments,
   303	    spawn/delegate/coordinate workers, or alter topology.
   304	30. A registered role defines an immutable maximum capability/tool ceiling; an
   305	    assignment starts empty and compiles an exact task-minimal proper subset.
   306	31. Each assignment binds one explicit provider/model/runtime/auth route.
   307	    Adapter, provider, and model fallback plus auxiliary model calls are
   308	    disabled; failure returns to Ranex for a new governed assignment decision.
   309	32. Product worker dispatch uses a release-pinned official typed runtime and
   310	    structured events. A Hermes parent model, Markdown terminal skill, shell,
   311	    PTY, terminal scraper, or tmux keystroke loop is never the hot path.
   312	33. A connected runtime is reusable only for the same assignment and logical
   313	    session under exact route/auth/role/tool/sandbox/workspace affinity. Generic
   314	    cross-task or cross-project conversation reuse is prohibited.
   315	34. No Hermes/Nous live model, provider, credential, entitlement, monetization,
   316	    or fallback route exists in target mode or compatibility mode.
   317	
   318	## 5. The full system planes
   319	
   320	Ranex uses seven planes. A plane is a responsibility and trust boundary, not a
   405	## 8. Canonical consistency boundary
   406	
   407	### 8.1 The Governed Execution Authority Cell
   408	
   409	`governed_execution` is one bounded consistency cell with four cohesive
   410	subdomains:
   411	
   412	1. run/workflow lifecycle;
   413	2. exact authorization and gate-decision snapshots;
   414	3. permits and human-decision consumption; and
   415	4. activity/effect intent, result, retry, and reconciliation.
   416	
   417	These responsibilities are separate domain files and types, but they participate
   418	in one invariant and one SQLite transaction. They are not persisted as four
   419	independent authorities.
   420	
   421	Policy definitions, evidence artifacts, checker qualification, and module/route
   422	catalogs are owned by other contexts. Before a transition, their immutable
   423	decisions and digests are copied or bound into the authority transaction. A
   424	later change cannot rewrite the historical snapshot, while every new effect
   425	still requires current-policy re-evaluation.
   426	
   427	`work_management` owns the separate `WorkItem` aggregate and Core-SDLC
   428	transition history. It does not join the run transaction. It consumes
   429	`governed_execution` integration events and submits idempotent commands through
   430	public APIs; failure is retried/reconciled and never simulated as
   431	cross-context atomicity.
   432	
   433	`policy` owns the authenticated, append-only `HumanDecisionRecord`.
   434	`governed_execution` owns the exact-subject `ConsumableAuthorityGrant` created
   435	from an eligible decision snapshot. The policy table is never mutated by the
   436	run transaction; grant issuance/consumption and permit issuance/consumption
   437	inside the authority cell are compare-and-swap protected.
   438	
   439	### 8.2 Atomic authority transaction
   440	
   441	One successful authority command atomically:
   442	
   443	```text
   444	compare expected run aggregate version
   445	  -> compare bound policy/module/route/workspace/evidence currency tokens
   446	  -> append ordered run/domain/audit event
   447	  -> update canonical current run row and version
   448	  -> bind exact gate/evidence/policy/module snapshots
   449	  -> issue or consume exact-subject authority grant/permit when required
   450	  -> insert zero or more outbound effect intents in the effect outbox
   451	  -> commit
   452	```
   453	
   454	If any write fails, none of them become visible. No external effect runs inside
   455	the database transaction. The outbox relay dispatches after commit using the
   456	recorded idempotency key.
   457	
   458	Gate evaluation and permit issuance are explicit state-only authority commands
   459	and normally create zero effects. An authorized workflow transition may create
   460	`0..N` effect intents, each with its own identity, arguments, destination,
   461	capabilities, idempotency semantics, and reconciliation policy. “Every
   462	transition creates one effect” is forbidden.
   463	
   464	Immediately before dispatch, the PEP rechecks the current policy activation,
   465	module/profile grant, route lock, workspace/base/candidate identity, evidence
   466	freshness, permit status, deadline, destination, and expected run version. A
   467	changed token denies dispatch or triggers re-authorization; it never inherits
   468	the stale intent's authority.
   469	
   470	### 8.3 State versus journal
   471	
   472	- The current row is the operational read source.
   473	- The ordered journal is the replay and audit oracle.
   474	- Snapshots may accelerate replay but never replace the journal.
   475	- A current-row/journal mismatch is corruption and blocks advancement; code
   476	  does not choose whichever source is convenient.
   477	- Other context projections are rebuilt or reconciled from canonical events.
   478	
   479	### 8.4 Reliable events outside governed execution
   480	
   481	Every stateful context commits its aggregate update, local audit event, and
   482	integration-event outbox row in its own unit of work. The governed-execution
   483	effect outbox is not the event-publication mechanism for `policy`,
   484	`work_management`, `module_governance`, `routing`, `identity_access`,
   485	`release_management`, or any other owner.
   486	
   487	Consumers are idempotent and record source context, aggregate ID/version,
   488	event ID/schema, correlation/causation IDs, and processed outcome. Cross-context
   489	delivery is at least once; duplicates and out-of-order messages are tested.
   490	
   491	## 9. Complete bounded-context ownership map
   492	
   493	### 9.1 Nonreplaceable authority contexts
   494	
   495	| Context | Owns | Public API | Persistence authority |
   496	|---|---|---|---|
   497	| `governed_execution` | Run, pinned workflow, activities, gate bindings, consumable authority grants, permit issuance/consumption, effect intents/outcomes, reconciliation | Commands, queries, integration events, immutable views | Sole run/execution-transition authority |
   498	| `policy` | Roles, authorization-eligibility rules, risk-lane derivation, policy packages, activation, authorization snapshots, waivers and authenticated human-decision records | Authorization request/decision, active-policy and human-decision snapshots | Policy definitions and append-only decision history; never issues or consumes execution grants/permits |
   499	| `assurance` | Claims, evidence envelopes, qualified checker results, exact-subject evidence snapshots, and `GateEvaluation` | Evidence ingestion/query, checker result, snapshot and gate-evaluation APIs | Sole evidence/gate-evaluation record owner; does not own review observations, qualify components, bind a run gate, or commit run state |
   500	| `module_governance` | Module catalog, descriptors, capability vocabulary, grants, compatibility, activation lifecycle | Module/grant/profile snapshots | Module and grant authority |
   501	| `identity_access` | Human/service identities, authentication, sessions, nonces, remote decision authentication, data classification, destination facts, secret references | Principal/session/secret-handle and destination-fact APIs | Identity and access authority; policy decides and the egress adapter enforces |
   502

   504	
   505	| Context | Owns | Attachment points |
   506	|---|---|---|
   507	| `product_definition` | Actors, problems/needs, hypotheses, product capabilities, requirements, acceptance examples, outcome measures, validation decisions, `CapabilityStatus` | Discovery/user research, product decisions, work intake, outcome review |
   508	| `work_management` | Projects, canonical `WorkItemStatus`, work class, portfolio/queues/WIP, dependencies/risks/issues, technical-debt records, accountable work roles, external issue mapping, Kanban projections | Core-SDLC transition API, GitHub intake, product/requirement links, governed-run/evidence integration |
   509	| `service_management` | Service catalog, service/capability ownership, supported versions, SLIs/SLOs/error budgets, support/escalation, maintenance and retirement triggers | Operations evidence, release catalog, product capability lifecycle |
   510	| `configuration_management` | Configuration-item registry, content-addressed baselines, status accounting, bidirectional traceability graph, functional/physical configuration audits | Product requirements, source/build/test/docs, release manifests, assurance evidence |
   511	| `supplier_governance` | Supplier/dependency adoption and reuse decisions, shared responsibility, version/support/vulnerability monitoring, concentration/exit plans | Packages, toolchains, providers, APIs, extensions, hosted services, Hermes upstream |
   512	| `resource_governance` | Local capacity, hierarchical cost/token/tool/output/network budgets, parent/child reservations, quotas, transitive usage attribution and provider-limit facts | Policy, routing, scheduling, agent runs, operations; never commercial billing |
   513	| `interaction_history` | User conversation/thread/message identity, continuity, search lifecycle, classification, retention, export and deletion | Delivery channels, context compilation, legacy session import |
   514	| `process_assurance` | SDLC policy conformance, tailoring profiles, human-role competence, process audits/nonconformance/corrective action, process improvement evidence, fleet experiment and calibration records | Core SDLC, work records, metrics, training/qualification and measurement-harness evidence |
   515	| `workspace` | Repository identity, worktree plans, branch/head validation, landing and cleanup | Git adapter, sandbox mounts |
   516	| `instruction_registry` | Atomic versioned instructions, precedence, applicability, checker bindings | Policy and packet compilation |

   519	| `routing` | Provider/model/transport/runtime/auth identities, one-route assignment locks, health, failure and governed-redispatch facts | Official runtime and provider adapters; no fallback chain |
   520	| `qualification` | Checker, module, route, and isolation-profile qualification | Frozen fixtures, canaries, holdouts |
   521	| `effectiveness` | Whole-workflow paired evaluation, causal ablations, owner-facing scorecards | Evaluation runners and artifacts |
   522	| `agent_collaboration` | Typed worker assignments/offers, atomic claims, fenced leases, heartbeats/liveness, mailboxes, Ranex-owned dispatch graphs and fan-out/join, role separation, worker attempts, results and handoffs | Official leaf runtime adapters; workers cannot delegate or coordinate and the context never owns `RunStatus`, work state, gate, permit, effect, merge, or release |
   523	| `repository_intelligence` | Source graph/index, language coverage, freshness, unsupported-analysis states | Atlas/tree-sitter or simpler index strategies |
   524	| `knowledge` | Skills, project memory, learned records, quarantine, sanitization, transfer approvals | Packet sources and explicitly scoped worker reads |
   525	| `scheduling` | Schedules, authenticated triggers, catch-up rules, trigger lifecycle | Cron, webhooks, external timers |
   526	| `delivery` | Channel-neutral messages, commands, decision challenges, rendering, delivery receipts | CLI, TUI, web, phone, GitHub adapters |
   527	| `artifact_management` | Content-addressed blobs, classification, access, retention, legal hold, expiry, purge | Filesystem/object-store adapters |

   566	| Analytical review | `analytical_review` | typed API or official SDK/app-server runtime | review request/attempt/observation state in `analytical_review`; transport/parser qualification evidence in `qualification` | Review observation |
