   585	## 11. Complete target repository map
  1039	### 11.1 Physical coexistence during the strangler
  1040	
  1041	The target tree above is not a command to move 170,000 inherited lines on the
  1042	first implementation day. During migration:
  1043	
  1044	1. upstream paths remain in their inherited root locations to minimize sync
  1045	   churn;
  1046	2. new code lives under `src/ranex`;
  1047	3. only `ranex.compatibility.hermes_legacy` may import inherited root modules;
  1048	4. target drivers import the compatibility facade, never `run_agent` directly;
  1049	5. the upstream-sync worktree compares and classifies new upstream changes;
  1050	6. selected inherited code is extracted behind a Ranex-owned port;
  1051	7. the remaining frozen subset moves under `legacy/hermes` only after parity and
  1052	   sync-cost tests pass;
  1053	8. Git history and upstream attribution remain even when source is later
  1054	   removed; and
  1055	9. the inherited test tree remains byte-bound by ADR-0010: unchanged files may
  1056	   run as nonsealing regression evidence, no legacy scope may expand, an
  1057	   in-place content change requires its registered exception, and each
  1058	   move/rename/removal requires canonical migration proof.
  1059	
  1060	## 12. Standard bounded-context package contract
  1061	
  1062	Every stateful context follows:
  1063	
  1064	```text
  1065	<context>/
  1066	├── __init__.py
  1067	├── README.md                 # vocabulary, owner, invariants, navigation
  1068	├── contract.yaml             # generated/validated registry projection
  1069	├── api/                      # only cross-context source import surface
  1070	├── domain/                   # aggregates, values, events, domain decisions
  1071	├── application/              # use cases, handlers, orchestration
  1072	│   └── ports/                # repository/external-capability protocols
  1073	└── adapters/                 # optional context-exclusive implementations
  1074	```
  1075	
  1076	Rules:
  1077	
  1078	- `api/` exposes commands, queries, integration events, and immutable views.
  1079	- `domain/` contains aggregates, roots, entities, immutable value objects,
  1080	  domain events, invariants, and narrowly named pure domain services. It
  1081	  contains no clients, repositories, SDKs, callbacks, ORM models, environment
  1082	  reads, or framework decorators.
  1083	- `application/` owns use cases and orchestration; repository and external
  1084	  capability protocols live only in `application/ports/`. It imports another
  1085	  context only through that context's `api/`.
  1086	- `adapters/` is optional and contains only technology translation and
  1087	  context-exclusive port implementations. Shared/host-edge placement follows
  1088	  the exact exception rule above.
  1089	- database row models and wire payload models live in adapters, not domain.
  1090	- `contract.yaml` is generated or validated from the canonical registries; it
  1091	  is not a second hand-maintained owner of dependencies or public contracts.
  1092	- no generic `utils.py`, `common.py`, `manager.py`, or `service.py` dumping
  1093	  ground is allowed without a narrow named responsibility.
  1094	- a module descriptor names its factory; import does not register the module.
  1095	- only folders needed by enacted behavior are created; cargo-cult empty layers
  1096	  and copied boilerplate fail review.
  1097	
  1098	### 12.1 Canonical file-responsibility catalog
  1099	
  1100	The table fixes the intended file names and responsibility centers. A slice may
  1160	### 12.2 Web dashboard structure
  1161	
  1162	The web dashboard is a presentation application, not another control plane:
  1163	
  1164	```text
  1165	apps/web-dashboard/src/
  1166	├── app/
  1167	├── features/
  1168	│   ├── runs/
  1169	│   ├── work/
  1170	│   ├── evidence/
  1171	│   ├── decisions/
  1172	│   ├── modules/
  1173	│   ├── routes/
  1174	│   ├── operations/
  1175	│   └── recovery/
  1176	├── generated-contracts/
  1177	├── transport/
  1178	└── security/
  1179	```
  1180	
  1181	It imports generated TypeScript contracts, calls authenticated application
  1182	APIs, and renders returned authority state. It contains no transition table,
  1183	gate rule, permit rule, risk rule, or provider credential.
  1184	
  1185	## 13. Enforced source dependency rules
  1186	
  1187	1. `foundation` imports the Python standard library only and has a strict size
  1188	   and consumer review budget.
  1189	2. Any `domain/` imports only its own context domain and `foundation`.
  1190	3. A context crosses another context only through `<other>.api`.
  1191	4. Application code imports ports, never adapter implementations.
  1192	5. Adapters implement ports and may import public APIs.
  1193	6. No adapter imports another adapter; shared host mechanics become a narrowly
  1194	   named port or platform adapter.
  1195	7. Only `bootstrap/composition.py` constructs concrete implementations, reads
  1196	   runtime environment configuration, and binds factories.
  1197	8. Nonreplaceable contexts never import `modules`, `adapters`,
  1198	   `compatibility`, or `legacy`.
  1199	9. First-party modules submit commands, proposals, and evidence; they cannot
  1200	   write authority tables.
  1201	10. `compatibility` may call Ranex public APIs; no Ranex context imports
  1202	    compatibility.
  1203	11. Only the Hermes compatibility adapter may import the inherited Hermes root
  1204	    during migration.
  1205	12. External extensions cannot import the host package; they communicate
  1206	    through the versioned process protocol.
  1207	13. The declared module graph is acyclic and is checked against actual imports.
  1208	14. `ExecutionContext` is forbidden as a domain-method parameter. Domain methods
  1209	    receive the smallest immutable subject view.
  1210	15. `scripts/` contains thin authenticated clients of public application APIs;
  1211	    no script imports repositories/adapters or performs an unrecorded
  1212	    install/update/migration/restore effect.
  1213	16. Only `compatibility.hermes_legacy` may import inherited Hermes roots during
  1214	    the strangler; no authority, domain, or other application package may.
  1215	17. `governed_execution.application.process_manager` is orchestration-only. It
  1328	## 14. Composition and boot
  1329	
  1330	Boot is explicit and deterministic:
  1331	
  1332	```text
  1333	load release manifest and schema versions
  1334	  -> validate build/provenance/de-commercialization manifest
  1335	  -> load typed configuration without importing inactive modules
  1336	  -> construct identity, authority store, artifact store, and core ports
  1337	  -> run pending approved migrations under migration coordinator
  1338	  -> build the release-pinned module graph
  1339	  -> reject missing, duplicate, cyclic, incompatible, unqualified, or
  1340	     undeclared components
  1341	  -> start outbox/reconciliation/trigger workers
  1342	  -> bind local delivery surfaces
  1343	  -> report readiness
  1344	```
  1345	
  1346	There is no import-time discovery, entry-point override, or remote catalog
  1347	activation in the authority process.
  1348	
  1349	### 14.1 Signed maintenance controller
  1350	
  1351	Install, update, boot migration, offline restore, and rollback sometimes occur
  1352	before the normal authority host is available. They do not become shell-script
  1353	bypasses. A minimal signed `maintenance_controller`:
  1354	
  1355	- accepts only versioned release/migration/restore commands and schemas;
  1356	- verifies operator authentication, target identity, release manifest,
  1357	  provenance, signatures/attestations, compatibility, recovery point, and
  1358	  predeclared rollback;
  1359	- invokes `release_management`, `migration`, and `backup_restore` application
  1360	  APIs under a dedicated maintenance policy/profile;
  1361	- has no model, arbitrary tool, plugin, browser, or general network capability;
  1362	- writes an append-only maintenance journal and content-addressed evidence;
  1363	- uses plan/verify/apply/verify/reconcile phases with idempotent steps; and
  1364	- hands the final state to the normal authority host, which refuses readiness
  1365	  until reconciliation passes.
  1366	
  1367	Signing authority is separated:
  1368	
  1369	- the human release authority approves key generation, activation, rotation,
  1370	  revocation, recovery, and emergency replacement ceremonies;
  1371	- `release_management` owns the accepted signing policy, signer/key IDs,
  1372	  signature requirements, release-manifest binding, and append-only
  1373	  rotation/revocation records;
  1374	- `identity_access` and its secret backend custody private key material behind
  1375	  opaque handles and ceremony-scoped access; `release_management` never reads
  2623	### 16.2 Core-SDLC and execution boundary
  2624	
  2625	- `product_definition` owns capabilities, needs, requirements, outcomes, and
  2626	  product validation.
  2627	- `work_management` alone transitions `WorkItemStatus`.
  2628	- one work item may have many `Run` attempts; `governed_execution` alone
  2629	  transitions each `RunStatus`.
  2630	- `operations` owns incidents; `release_management` owns release/update state;
  2631	  `service_management` owns service commitments and lifecycle triggers.
  2632	- maintenance, retirement, and incident response create linked work items with
  2633	  the applicable `WorkClass`; they do not smuggle new values into
  2634	  `WorkItemStatus`.
  2635	- a run success, merge, release, model verdict, board move, incident mitigation,
  2636	  or capability state change is evidence/input to a work transition, never the
  2637	  transition itself.
  2638	- cross-aggregate mappings and invalidation rules live in
  2639	  `lifecycle-crosswalks.yaml` and `invalidation-graph.yaml`.
  2640	
  2641	`RuleStage` is derived from the owning lifecycle state solely to select
  2642	applicable policy. It is not independently writable.
  2643	
  2644	### 16.3 Gate namespaces
  2645	
  2646	| Namespace | Meaning | Authority |
  2647	|---|---|---|
  2648	| `SDLC-*` | Core-SDLC stage/cross-lifecycle controls, including `SDLC-FORK-000` preflight | Owning SDLC roles plus deterministic requirements |
  2649	| `AI-G0`–`AI-G10` | Exact-subject evidence gates for one agent-assisted execution | `assurance` creates `GateEvaluation`; `governed_execution` only binds a fresh pass |
  2650	| `MAP-*` | Architecture-map completeness assertions over one `ArchitectureSubject` | `assurance` records qualified deterministic evaluation; architecture owner accepts/rejects |
  2651	| `SDLC-ADOPT-*` | Adoption/calibration of the process itself, including `SDLC-ADOPT-FLEET-*` | Process owner/human governor |
  2652	| `GateOutcome` | Runtime exact-subject result | Qualified deterministic gate |
  2653	| Human decision point | Product, architecture, risk, release, destructive or exception authority | Authenticated named human |
  2654	
  2655	IDs never cross namespaces by alias, and no passing namespace implies another
  2656	one passed.
  2657	
  2658	## 17. Command, event, and effect vocabulary
  2659	
  2660	Commands request work; events state facts that already occurred. They are
  2661	different types and cannot share one generic payload. A name without an
  2662	envelope, payload, owner/producer, trigger, consumer, ordering, delivery,
  2663	privacy, compatibility, and replay contract is `DEFINED_NAME_ONLY` and blocks
  2664	`IMPLEMENTATION_START_READY`.
  2665	
  2666	### 17.1 Canonical event envelope and delivery contract
  2667	
  2668	All 40 initially registered events use `DomainEventEnvelopeV1`, projected to
  2669	`schemas/events/domain-event-envelope-v1.schema.json`. Every field below is
  2670	required unless marked nullable:
  2671	
  2672	| Field | Exact type/invariant |
  2673	|---|---|
  2674	| `schema_version` | constant `domain-event-envelope/v1` |
  2675	| `event_id`, `event_name`, `event_version` | exact catalog ID/name and integer `1` |
  2676	| `event_instance_id` | globally unique UUIDv7; immutable and never reused |
  2677	| `owner_context`, `producer_service_id` | exact catalog values; authenticated producer must be owned by that context |
  2678	| `producer_release_digest` | `sha256:<64 lowercase hex>` of the emitting release |
  2679	| `aggregate_type`, `aggregate_id` | exact catalog aggregate and its registered opaque nonempty ID |
  2680	| `source_aggregate_version`, `aggregate_version` | nonnegative integers; resulting version is greater than source version |
  2681	| `aggregate_event_sequence` | positive gap-free integer under `(owner_context, aggregate_type, aggregate_id)` |
  2682	| `subject_ref`, `subject_digest` | governed immutable subject ref and RFC 8785 SHA-256 |
  2683	| `correlation_id` | nonempty trace/workflow ID retained across the whole causal operation |
  2684	| `causation_id` | exact command/event/decision ID that directly caused this event |
  2685	| `idempotency_key` | nonempty stable logical-operation key; same key plus different subject/payload is a conflict |
  2686	| `occurred_at`, `recorded_at` | strict UTC instants with `occurred_at <= recorded_at` |
  2687	| `payload_schema_ref`, `payload_schema_digest` | exact per-row `ranex:event-payload:<EventName>:v1` schema and RFC 8785 SHA-256 |
  2688	| `payload` | closed object matching the row below; no undeclared or nullable-by-omission field |
  2689	| `data_classification`, `retention_policy_id` | maximum classification of subject/payload and exact applicable retention rule |
  2690	| `digest` | RFC 8785 SHA-256 of the complete envelope excluding `digest` |
  2691	
  2692	Catalog payload notation is exact: `Id<T>` is a registered opaque nonempty ID
  2693	of type `T`; `Ref<T>` is the closed object
  2694	`{id: Id<T>, digest: sha256:<64 lowercase hex>}`; `Set<T>` is a bytewise-sorted
  2695	unique nonempty array unless explicitly `[]` is allowed; `Utc` is a strict UTC
  3083	## 18. Workflow semantics
  3084	
  3085	The target interpreter supports:
  3086	
  3087	- sequence;
  3088	- deterministic choice;
  3089	- activity request/result;
  3090	- evidence gate;
  3091	- durable signal wait;
  3092	- durable timer;
  3093	- classified retry;
  3094	- cancellation;
  3095	- compensation request;
  3096	- reconciliation wait; and
  3097	- terminal success/failure.
  3098	
  3099	Parallel/fan-out, map, dynamic graph mutation, and richer authoring remain mapped
  3100	extension points under `governed_execution.domain.workflow`, but cannot gain
  3101	authority until their ordering, cancellation, retry, compensation, and replay
  3102	semantics are specified and tested.
  3103	
  3104	Workflow definitions are immutable release- or project-approved data. Models may
  3105	draft definitions, never activate them.
  3106	
  3107	## 19. Effects, idempotency, and reconciliation
  3108	
  3109	An `Activity` is one logical unit of workflow work. It may be pure, invoke a
  3110	worker, or request `0..N` external `Effect` records. Each effect has its own
  3111	identity, destination, authority, idempotency/retry policy, result, and
  3112	reconciliation history. An activity resolves only when its required effects
  3113	have acceptable terminal facts; optional effects and compensation are declared
  3114	in the workflow definition.
  3115	
  3116	Every `ActivityRequest` declares:
  3117	
  3118	- exact subject;
  3119	- activity/effect type;
  3120	- canonical argument digest;
  3121	- required capabilities and declared effects;
  3122	- route/module/isolation profile;
  3123	- one absolute deadline and remaining budgets;
  3124	- idempotency key;
  3125	- timeout and retry policy;
  3126	- expected result/evidence schemas; and
  3127	- compensation/reconciliation contract.
  3128	
  3129	The outbox relay:
  3130	
  3131	1. leases a committed intent;
  3132	2. dispatches with the same logical idempotency key;
  3133	3. validates the typed result;
  3134	4. records provider/external receipts as artifacts;
  3135	5. marks success, classified failure, denial, or `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`;
  3136	6. retries only when policy permits;
  3137	7. sends ambiguous outcomes to an adapter-specific reconciler; and
  3138	8. records the reconciled fact before the run advances.
  3139	
  3140	Reconciliation is an orthogonal record, not a terminal effect outcome. It
  3141	transitions `PENDING -> RUNNING -> RESOLVED | UNRESOLVED`; `RESOLVED` stores the
  3142	discovered effect disposition and moves `EffectStatus` from `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`
  3143	to the proven `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED_*`, or `DENIED` value. History never erases
  3144	that the original acknowledgement was unknown.
  3145	
  3146	GitHub, Git, messaging, provider, filesystem, and database effects each define
  3147	how to query or prove outcome after a lost acknowledgement. “Probably happened”
  3148	is never a terminal state.
  3149	
  3150	## 20. Evidence, review, gates, and human authority
  3151	
  3152	The data flow is deliberately one-way:
  3153	
  3154	```text
  3155	raw artifact
  3156	  -> EvidenceEnvelope
  3157	  -> immutable ReviewObservation reference or qualified CheckerResult
  3158	  -> validated exact-subject evidence snapshot
  3159	  -> GateEvaluation
  3160	  -> optional authenticated HumanDecision
  3161	  -> exact-subject Permit
  3162	  -> authorized transition/effect
  3163	```
  3164	
  3165	Forbidden flows:
  3166	
  3167	- `ReviewObservation -> PASS` without a qualified gate evaluator;
  3168	- model output -> `HumanDecision`;
  3169	- implementer summary -> proof;
  3170	- telemetry record -> evidence merely because it contains a success message;
  3171	- missing result -> zero, pass, approval, or abstaining vote;
  3172	- waiver -> machine `PASS`.
  3173	
  3174	A blocking gate advances automatically only on a fresh exact-subject `PASS`.
  3175	`FAIL`, `UNKNOWN`, `CONFLICT`, `CHECKER_FAULT`, missing/stale evidence, and
  3176	unproven `NOT_APPLICABLE` block.
  3177	
  3178	`analytical_review` alone owns `ReviewObservation` and related review lifecycle
  3179	records. `assurance` alone owns the qualified evidence snapshot and immutable
  3180	`GateEvaluation`. `governed_execution` may bind that evaluation and consume an
  3181	eligible permit in its authority transaction; it cannot create or edit the
  3182	evaluation. `policy` determines requirements/eligibility but does not issue or
  3183	consume the permit.
  3184	
  3185	## 21. Policy, identity, secrets, and human decisions
  3186	
  3187	`identity_access` owns:
  3188	
  3337	## 25. Delivery surfaces
  3338	
  3339	All surfaces use the same commands, queries, subject views, authentication, and
  3340	human-decision contracts.
  3341	
  3342	| Surface | Target role | Boundary |
  3343	|---|---|---|
  3344	| CLI | Primary administration and scripted operation | Local authenticated process |
  3345	| TUI | Interactive local operation | Same APIs as CLI |
  3346	| Local web | Queue, evidence, approvals, recovery, health | Bind `127.0.0.1`; authenticated; no public listener |
  3347	| Phone | Remote text control and decisions | Channel-neutral port; Telegram first adapter; nonce/expiry/replay controls |
  3348	| GitHub | Issue intake, PR status, permitted completion effects | Signed/authenticated API/webhook adapter |
  3349	| Webhooks/triggers | Start predeclared workflows | Authenticated, idempotent, no direct transition authority |
  3350	
  3351	The desktop application and its packaging/updater/settings state are excluded.
  3352	The local web surface supplies the visual control plane without duplicating the
  3353	product runtime.
  3354	
  3355	## 26. Storage and data lifecycle
  3356	
  3357	### 26.1 Physical storage
  3358	
  3359	The local target uses:
  3360	
  3361	```text
  3362	$RANEX_HOME/
  3363	├── db/ranex.db
  3364	├── artifacts/sha256/
  3365	├── workspaces/
  3366	├── modules/<module-id>/
  3367	├── profiles/
  3368	├── telemetry/
  3369	├── backups/
  3370	└── compatibility/          # read-only/quarantined legacy input
  3371	```
  3372	
  3373	Configuration shipped in the repository is immutable input. Runtime state lives
  3374	under `$RANEX_HOME`; it is never written into the source tree.
  3375	
  3376	`$RANEX_HOME` has one local-owner identity, mode `0700` or platform-equivalent
  3377	ACL, no worker mount, and explicit subdirectory ownership for authority state,
  3378	artifacts, interaction history, indexes, backups, logs, temporary material, and
  3379	compatibility data. Files containing secrets or classified content use `0600`
  3380	or equivalent. Data classified as sensitive requires an accepted at-rest
  3381	encryption/key-recovery profile; encryption keys never live in the same backup
  3382	payload as the protected data.
  3383	
  3384	### 26.2 Ownership
  3385	
  3386	- Core contexts share one physical SQLite file but own distinct logical tables
  3387	  and repositories.
  3388	- Only the governed authority UoW performs the multi-table transition write.
  3389	- Every other context writes only its owned tables through its repository.
  3390	- Artifacts are written by exclusive temporary creation, fsync, atomic rename,
  3391	  and digest verification before a database record references them.
  3392	- Safe unreferenced blobs may be garbage-collected after policy delay.
  3393	- A missing referenced artifact whose `ArtifactStatus` is not `PURGED` is
  3394	  corruption and cannot satisfy a gate; an effective legal hold neither makes
  3395	  missing bytes valid nor changes availability.
  3396	- Authorized purge first writes a durable tombstone containing artifact ID,
  3397	  former digest, classification, retention/legal decision, authorization,
  3398	  purge method and time. `PURGED` artifacts are intentionally unavailable and
  3399	  cannot satisfy replay or a gate; replay yields typed `EVIDENCE_PURGED`, not
  3400	  generic corruption.
  3401	- Availability, quarantine, expiry, purge, and backup inclusion are explicit
  3402	  artifact state/policy; legal hold is the independently authorized append-only
  3403	  fact chain defined in §16.1. Purge locks and rechecks that no effective hold
  3404	  exists and retains the considered hold-fact digests in its tombstone.
  3405	
  3406	## 27. Security and isolation
  3407	
  3408	Threat assumptions:
  3409	
  3410	- model output and repository content are untrusted;
  3411	- a tool-bearing harness may be mistaken or compromised;
  3412	- a third-party extension is hostile until isolated;
  3413	- external providers are egress destinations;
  3414	- local logs and hash chains are rewriteable by an attacker with equivalent host
  3415	  authority;
  3416	- a symlink, subprocess, background job, plugin, MCP server, or alternate tool
  3417	  path can bypass naive wrappers; and
  3418	- upstream changes can reintroduce removed commercial or privileged behavior.
  3419	
  3420	Required controls:
  3421	
  3422	- deny-by-default capability vocabulary;
  3423	- one application PEP;
  3424	- exact realpath, repository, worktree, and commit validation;
  3425	- read-only base repository and exact writable workspace;
  3608	## 31. Explicit product exclusions
  3609	
  3610	The full map includes explicit borders:
  3611	
  3612	- Electron/desktop application and desktop updater;
  3613	- every Hermes/Nous live inference or parent-agent model loop;
  3614	- Nous Portal/model service and every Hermes/Nous credential, OAuth,
  3615	  entitlement, billing, credit, subscription, payment, managed-tool, purchase,
  3616	  sales, and promotional path;
  3617	- a public internet dashboard;
  3618	- automatic merge;
  3619	- a user-installed plugin in the authority path;
  3620	- same-process hostile Python;
  3621	- prompt text as enforcement;
  3622	- model verdict or model consensus as transition authority;
  3623	- remote catalog activation;
  3624	- broad cross-project shared mutable memory;
  3625	- automatic activation of learned policy or skills;
  3626	- indiscriminate MCP catalog exposure;
  3627	- Kubernetes/microservice control plane for the one-host product;
  3628	- claims of exactly-once external effects;
  3629	- event sourcing every module;
  3630	- an unbounded general workflow platform in the kernel;
  3631	- worker-created assignments, provider subagents/teams, nested delegation, or
  3632	  model-controlled fleet coordination;
  3633	- generic full-tool roles or treating provider `allowedTools` as restriction;
  3634	- ambient worker settings, MCP servers, apps/plugins, skills, agents, memory, or
  3635	  background execution;
  3636	- hidden or declared adapter/provider/model fallback, auxiliary model calls, or
  3637	  worker route mutation;
  3638	- terminal-skill, shell, PTY, tmux, or scraped-text worker-runtime protocol;
  3639	- generic cross-task/project reuse of a stateful model conversation;
  3640	- consumer credential-file extraction or provider-identity imitation;
  3641	- raw prompt/model-output logging as routine telemetry; and
  3642	- indefinite compatibility for removed commercial or consumer-product surfaces.
  3643	
  3644	Image/video generation, consumer integrations, computer-use automation, voice,
  3645	learned routing, microVMs, process checkpoint engines, visual workflow editing,
  3646	and multi-host operation are not active target capabilities. Their possible
  3647	attachment class is mapped, but adding any one requires a new product-scope ADR,
  3648	threat model, lifecycle, and qualification evidence.
  3649	
  3650	## 32. Implementation routes through the full map
  3686	## 33. Architecture and completeness gates
  3687	
  3688	### 33.1 Full-map gates
  3689	
  3690	| ID | Requirement |
  3691	|---|---|
  3692	| `MAP-001` | Every capability zone has one owner, public boundary, lifecycle, trust tier, source location, and security boundary. |
  3693	| `MAP-002` | Every explicit exclusion is listed; no omitted area is mislabeled as deferred. |
  3694	| `MAP-003` | The lifecycle registry has one owner per lifecycle and no orphan states. |
  3695	| `MAP-004` | The path/state/data-ownership registries agree with this document and the source tree. |
  3696	| `MAP-005` | The whole fork layout shows new code, inherited code, compatibility, migration, and upstream-sync boundaries. |
  3697	| `MAP-006` | CLI, TUI, web, phone, and GitHub use the same command/decision authority contracts. |
  3698	| `MAP-007` | Security, operations, data lifecycle, and compliance each have a named owner. |
  3699	| `MAP-008` | Knowledge, tools/MCP, routes, extensions, backup, release, sync, and migration have final attachment points even when inactive. |
  3700	
  3701	Each row is evaluated against the same immutable `ArchitectureSubject`
  3702	manifest. A qualified deterministic map checker produces evidence;
  3703	`assurance` records its `GateEvaluation`; the architecture owner records the
  3704	accept/reject decision. The aggregate map passes only when all applicable rows
  3705	are fresh `PASS` and no blocking `UNKNOWN` or `CONFLICT` exists. `AI-G1`
  3706	consumes those exact evaluations for a run. Accepted paper architecture,
  3707	`AI-G1`, `AI-G2`, and runtime qualification remain distinct facts.
  3708	
  3709	### 33.2 Structural gates
  3710	
  3711	- canonical context/path/layer/public placement:
  3712	  `ORG-PATH-001`, `ORG-CONTEXT-001`, `ORG-LAYER-001`, and `ORG-PUBLIC-001`;
  3713	- allowed-edge, no-cycle, effect-free import, and sole-composition checks:
  3714	  `ORG-DEPENDENCY-001`, `ORG-CYCLE-001`, `ORG-IMPORT-001`, and
  3715	  `ORG-COMPOSE-001`;
  3716	- public messaging, single-owner persistence, test mirroring, generated drift,
  3717	  migration, and legacy quarantine: `ORG-MESSAGE-001`, `ORG-PERSIST-001`,
  3718	  `ORG-TEST-MIRROR-001`, `ORG-GENERATED-001`, `ORG-MIGRATION-001`, and
  3719	  `ORG-LEGACY-001`;
  3720	- semantic owner/reviewer resolution, explicit package discovery, navigation,
  3721	  and exact non-transitive exceptions: `ORG-OWNERSHIP-001`,
  3722	  `ORG-DISCOVERY-001`, `ORG-NAV-001`, and `ORG-EXEMPTION-001`;
  3723	- no inactive module registration or migration;
  3724	- no canonical write outside the authority UoW;
  3725	- no `ExecutionContext` in domain method signatures;
  3726	- generated schema/config/example drift check; and
  3727	- architecture graph diff on every change.
  3728	
  3729	All eighteen `ORG-*` evaluations bind the same exact subject. A missing
  3730	applicable evaluation, unknown/duplicate path owner, private cross-context
  3731	import, unregistered central adapter, or expired/broad exception fails
  3732	structural readiness. Only `ORG-NAV-001` and risk-selected diagnostic evidence
  3733	may be nonblocking when policy explicitly says so; an enforcement label never
  3734	permits a higher invariant to be waived.
  3735	
  3736	### 33.3 Behavioral P0 gates
  3737	
  3738	- reducer purity, property tests, replay, upcasters, and snapshot corruption;
  3739	- crash before/after every authority and artifact boundary;
  3740	- policy/checker timeout, exception, malformed response, and disappearance;
  3741	- exact-subject mismatch and permit CAS/reuse;
  3742	- evidence-versus-observation isolation;
  3743	- packet digest stability over resolved inputs;
  3744	- target-host secret/read/write/process/argv/network/output denial;
  3745	- official-runtime Codex/Claude and every later qualified adapter bypass matrix;
  3746	- sole Ranex cross-worker orchestration, provider nested-lineage denial, and
  3747	  absence of Agent/team/delegation/scheduler tools;
  3748	- immutable role ceiling versus exact assignment-minimal proper-subset grant,
  3749	  startup-attested actual tools, catch-all pre-tool/custom-tool mediation, and
  3750	  no ambient setting/MCP/app/plugin/skill/agent/memory/background source;
  3751	- one explicit model/runtime/auth route with no fallback, auxiliary call, or
  3752	  worker route mutation under outage/overload/malformed-result faults;
  3753	- interrupt, correlated drain, SDK disconnect, outer-supervisor cleanup, exact
  3754	  resume, and assignment/session-affinity isolation;
  3755	- cold/warm first-event/content and total latency, process/model/tool
  3756	  amplification, CPU, memory, cancellation, correctness, and leakage evidence;
  3757	- double-claim, lease-expiry, stale-epoch fencing, heartbeat/reclaim, mailbox
  3758	  dead-letter, child-budget escape, and orphan-worker matrix;
  3759	- result-aware loop detection across syntactic variants and unchanged failure
  3760	  results;
  3761	- path/worktree enforcement through symlink, subprocess, helper, alternate Git,
  3762	  generated-path, and new-fixture collision routes;
  3763	- verifier/human-capacity backpressure without assurance reduction;
  3764	- split-source reconciliation with Hermes/Kanban/projections;
  3765	- ambiguous external effect reconciliation;
  3766	- route identity and re-probation;
  3767	- zero Hermes/Nous inference, credential/entitlement, monetization,
  3768	  runtime/package/network/SBOM gate;
  3769	- clean backup and restore with external reconciliation; and
  3770	- upstream-sync anti-recontamination gate.
  3771	
  3772	### 33.4 Default TDD construction and verification gates
  3773	
  3774	[ADR-0008](./decisions/ADR-0008-make-tdd-the-default-development-discipline.md)
  3775	requires acceptance/risk/failure modeling followed by inspectable
     1	# ADR-0007: Establish Modular-DDD Repository Organization
     2	
     3	| Field | Value |
     4	|---|---|
     5	| ADR ID | `ADR-0007` |
     6	| Version | `1.0.0` |
     7	| Status | `ACCEPTED` |
     8	| Decision owner | Human owner |
     9	| Decision date | 2026-07-28 |
    10	| Effective revision | Working tree based on `4baad4a67843b02d5970f442fb54aed8d6525dda`; executable projection and enactment pending |
    11	| Content binding | Exact digest is recorded externally in each immutable review/release source manifest |
    12	| Affected contexts | Entire repository, every bounded context, adapters, tests, schemas, migrations, deployment, tooling, documentation, and compatibility code |
    13	| Supersedes | No ADR; narrows and completes architecture §§11–13 |
    14	| Review/expiry date | First contract-registry projection, first target context tracer, then on topology/ownership change |
    15	| Compatibility/migration class | Target organization with strangler quarantine for inherited Hermes layout |
    16	| Security/data class | Public architecture decision |
    17	
    18	## Decision
    19	
    20	Ranex uses a modular monolith whose primary organizing unit is the owned
    21	bounded context, not a framework, adapter technology, horizontal “services”
    22	folder, or model/tool vendor. The canonical context path remains:
    23	
    24	```text
    25	src/ranex/<context>/
    26	```
    27	
    28	The package name makes product/domain intent visible. The architecture
    29	registries, not directory inference, decide which names are bounded contexts.
    30	Folder count does not prove modularity, and every context does not need every
    31	optional folder.
    32	
    33	## Canonical repository topology
    34	
    35	```text
    36	ranex/
    37	├── src/ranex/
    38	│   ├── foundation/                 # semantics-light shared primitives
    39	│   ├── <context>/                  # one registered bounded context
    40	│   ├── modules/                    # first-party capability modules
    41	│   ├── adapters/                   # multi-context/host-edge adapters only
    42	│   ├── compatibility/              # anti-corruption boundary only
    43	│   ├── bootstrap/                  # composition root and startup
    44	│   └── migration/                  # cross-context migration ordering
    45	├── apps/                           # delivery applications; no authority rules
    46	├── architecture/contracts/         # owner-authored semantic registries
    47	├── architecture/generated/         # generated projections; never hand edited
    48	├── schemas/<owning-area>/           # canonical wire/artifact schemas
    49	├── packages/generated-contracts/   # generated language bindings
    50	├── tests/                           # ADR-0008 taxonomy, mirroring owners
    51	├── config/                          # release-pinned declarative profiles
    52	├── deploy/                          # declarative packaging/install/host assets
    53	├── docs/
    54	│   ├── architecture/               # normative architecture and specifications
    55	│   │   ├── decisions/              # ADRs
    56	│   │   └── rfcs/                   # non-accepted proposals
    57	│   ├── research/                   # evidence/advice, never authority
    58	│   └── operations/                 # tested runbooks
    59	├── scripts/                        # thin public-API operator/CI clients
    60	├── tools/                          # build, contract generation, validation
    61	├── legacy/hermes/                  # optional post-parity frozen source home
    62	└── legal/                          # license, provenance, SBOM policy
    63	```
    64	
    65	Production domain/application behavior lives only below `src/ranex`. `apps`,
    66	`scripts`, `deploy`, and `tools` cannot become alternate business-rule homes.
    67	Configuration that changes behavior remains versioned, schema-validated, owned,
    68	and tested.
    69	
    70	## Bounded-context internal contract
    71	
    72	```text
    73	src/ranex/<context>/
    74	├── __init__.py
    75	├── README.md                 # vocabulary, owner, invariants, navigation
    76	├── contract.yaml             # generated/validated context-registry projection
    77	├── api/                      # sole cross-context import surface
    78	├── domain/                   # aggregates, value objects, domain events/rules
    79	├── application/              # use cases, command/query handlers
    80	│   └── ports/                # repositories and external capability protocols
    81	└── adapters/                 # optional context-exclusive port implementations
    82	```
    83	
    84	Only folders required by enacted behavior are created. `contract.yaml` is a
    85	projection of the canonical registries, not a second hand-maintained source.
    86	A context that has no local adapter does not receive an empty `adapters/`;
    87	shared host mechanics do not justify identical boilerplate in every context.
    88	
    89	### Placement rules
    90	
    91	| Concept | Canonical placement | Rule |
    92	|---|---|---|
    93	| Aggregate/root/entity | `<context>/domain/<domain_concept>.py` or a cohesive domain subpackage | Aggregate root owns invariants and legal mutation; one aggregate transaction does not imply cross-context atomicity |
    94	| Value object | Co-located with its sole aggregate or `<context>/domain/<concept>.py` when reused inside that context | Immutable and validated at construction; never a database/wire model |
    95	| Domain service | `<context>/domain/<named_domain_operation>.py` | Pure domain decision spanning concepts; no I/O, repository, clock read, policy client, or orchestration |
    96	| Application use case | `<context>/application/<verb_noun>.py` or a cohesive use-case subpackage | Orchestrates domain objects and ports; owns no adapter/framework rule |
    97	| Repository/capability port | `<context>/application/ports/<owned_capability>.py` | Protocol expressed in owner language; application owns it, adapter implements it |
    98	| Public command/query/event/view | `<context>/api/` | Stable, versioned, immutable boundary type; the only source import permitted from another context |
    99	| Context-exclusive adapter | `<context>/adapters/<technology>/` | Translates external/ORM/wire types and implements one or more named local ports |
   100	| Multi-context or host-edge adapter | `src/ranex/adapters/<boundary>/<technology>/` | Allowed only when the integration is genuinely shared or is a delivery/composition edge; contains no domain rule |
   101	| Persistence row/ORM model | Owning persistence adapter | Never imported by domain/API consumers |
   102	| Integration event/outbox mapping | Owner API plus owner persistence adapter | Event schema is public; durable mapping/outbox mechanics remain adapter-owned |
   103	
   104	A “domain service” is not a home for miscellaneous logic. A use case is not a
   105	god orchestrator. Split a module when it has multiple owners/reasons to change,
   106	mixes public/private responsibilities, creates a forbidden dependency, or
   107	cannot be tested through a narrow contract. Ranex adopts no arbitrary
   108	lines-per-file, classes-per-folder, or one-type-per-file gate.
   109	
   110	## Dependency and import rules
   111	
   112	Allowed source dependencies point inward:
   113	
   114	```text
   115	foundation
   116	    ^
   117	domain
   118	    ^
   119	application + application/ports
   120	    ^
   121	api and adapters
   122	    ^
   123	bootstrap/apps
   124	```
   125	
   126	More precisely:
   127	
   128	1. `foundation` uses the standard library and semantics-light registered
   129	   primitives only. Moving knowledge there requires consumer/ownership review.
   130	2. A context's `domain` imports only its own domain and `foundation`.
   131	3. Its `application` imports its own `domain`, `application/ports`,
   132	   `foundation`, and another context's public `api` only when the registered
   133	   dependency permits it.
   134	4. Its `api` may expose owner-defined DTOs/enums and immutable views; it does
   135	   not export domain mutability, ORM objects, adapter types, or service
   136	   locators.
   137	5. Adapters import their implemented port and public owner types. An adapter
   138	   never imports another adapter.
   139	6. Cross-context imports target exactly `ranex.<other>.api...`; private
   140	   `domain`, `application`, `ports`, and `adapters` imports are forbidden.
   141	7. The actual import graph and declared context graph are acyclic. A necessary
   142	   interaction cycle is broken through an owned integration event, a query/API
   143	   redesign, or an explicit higher-level orchestration context—not a hidden
   144	   private import.
   145	8. Imports perform no network, database, filesystem mutation, process spawn,
   146	   registration, migration, environment-dependent decision, or other effect.
   147	9. `bootstrap/composition.py` is the sole product composition root that reads
   148	   the active release profile and constructs concrete implementations. Context
   149	   factories may assemble internal objects but cannot select global policy,
   150	   routes, credentials, or alternate business behavior.
   151	
   152	No test, script, module, extension, or compatibility path receives a broader
   153	dependency rule than production code.
   154	
   155	## Cross-context state and messaging
   156	
   157	- A context owns its aggregates, tables, migrations, public contracts, and
   158	  integration events even when SQLite is physically shared.
   159	- Synchronous calls use the owning context's public API and remain outside the
   160	  callee's transaction.
   161	- State-change propagation uses versioned integration events, a transactional
   162	  owner outbox, idempotent consumers, correlation/causation IDs, and explicit
   163	  duplicate/out-of-order/reconciliation behavior.
   164	- Cross-context database reads/writes, foreign repository use, shared mutable
   165	  models, and distributed-transaction assumptions are prohibited.
   166	- `governed_execution` remains the specifically documented authority-cell
   167	  consistency boundary; its centrality does not authorize knowledge or writes
   168	  owned by another context.
   169	
   170	## Tests, schemas, generated artifacts, and migrations
   171	
   172	- Tests mirror source ownership under the ADR-0008 roots. Unit paths include
   173	  `tests/unit/<context>/domain/` and
   174	  `tests/unit/<context>/application/`; contract/integration paths name the
   175	  owning context and port/adapter.
   176	- Canonical semantic registries live in `architecture/contracts`; JSON Schema
   177	  lives once under `schemas/<owning-area>`; generated bindings live under
   178	  `packages/generated-contracts`; generated documentation lives under
   179	  `architecture/generated` or an explicitly generated docs subtree.
   180	- Generated outputs carry generator/input digests and are not hand edited.
   181	  Drift, duplicate definitions, missing owner, and schema/API incompatibility
   182	  fail validation.
   183	- Context-owned SQLite migrations live with the owning persistence adapter,
   184	  normally
   185	  `src/ranex/<context>/adapters/persistence/sqlite/migrations/`.
   186	  `src/ranex/migration` owns only ordering, compatibility, application,
   187	  verification, and rollback across those manifests.
   188	- Shared SQLite connection/transaction mechanics may live in a named platform
   189	  adapter; they cannot own context table definitions or silently coordinate
   190	  cross-context writes.
   191	
   192	## Operations, deployment, tooling, and documentation
   193	
   194	- `deploy/` contains declarative package/install/service/host-profile assets.
   195	  Runtime behavior remains in production adapters and release management.
   196	- `docs/operations/` contains versioned runbooks linked to tested commands and
   197	  recovery evidence.
   198	- `scripts/` contains thin authenticated clients of public APIs; no repository,
   199	  adapter, secret, migration, or authority shortcut is permitted.
   200	- `tools/` contains developer/build/codegen/validation programs and fixtures;
   201	  it is not installed as product authority unless explicitly packaged behind a
   202	  production port.
   203	- Accepted decisions live only in `docs/architecture/decisions/`. RFCs live in
   204	  `docs/architecture/rfcs/` and remain non-normative until an ADR accepts them.
   205	
   206	## Ownership, discovery, naming, and navigation
   207	
   208	The path registry assigns every governed path pattern:
   209	
   210	- one semantic owner/context;
   211	- one accountable human owner role and required reviewer role;
   212	- allowed dependency edges and data classification;
   213	- generated/manual/legacy status; and
   214	- applicable topology/test rules.
   215	
   216	That registry generates a CODEOWNERS-ready projection. CODEOWNERS may request
   217	review but never becomes semantic ownership or transition authority.
   218	Overlapping patterns with different owners are `CONFLICT`.
   219	
   220	Python packaging discovers only explicit packages below `src/ranex`; tests,
   221	docs, tools, `legacy`, worktrees, and generated TypeScript are excluded.
   222	Context packages use `snake_case` registry IDs and explicit `__init__.py`
   223	files. Import-time scanning/registration is prohibited; the release catalog
   224	discovers modules/adapters from manifests at the composition root.
   225	
   226	Names use domain or use-case language. Unqualified dumping grounds such as
   227	`utils.py`, `common.py`, `helpers.py`, `manager.py`, and `service.py` fail
   228	review unless the registry records one narrow responsibility. Every context
   229	README links owner, vocabulary, public API, invariants, dependency diagram,
   230	data/migrations, operations, and tests; it does not duplicate canonical
   231	contracts.
   232	
   233	## Compatibility and legacy quarantine
   234	
   235	Inherited Hermes source remains in its upstream layout during the strangler.
   236	Only `ranex.compatibility.hermes_legacy` may import it. After parity and
   237	upstream-sync-cost evidence, a frozen subset may move to `legacy/hermes/`.
   238	Neither location is part of package discovery for new Ranex code. New domain
   239	rules, schemas, migrations, or shared helpers may not be added to legacy.
   240	
   241	## Machine-checkable topology rules
   242	
   243	The contract lane projects this exact rule set into the path/context
   244	registries. `BLOCK` means a violating exact subject cannot pass architecture or
   245	contract readiness.
   246	
   247	```yaml
   248	topology_rule_set: "RANEX-TOPOLOGY-1.0"
   249	rules:
   250	  - {id: "ORG-PATH-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Each registered context root is exactly src/ranex/<context>/."}
   251	  - {id: "ORG-CONTEXT-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Every context has one owner, public API, contract projection, and no second semantic home."}
   252	  - {id: "ORG-LAYER-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Domain/application/port/adapter files match the placement and inward-dependency rules."}
   253	  - {id: "ORG-PUBLIC-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Cross-context source imports resolve only through the target context api package."}
   254	  - {id: "ORG-DEPENDENCY-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Actual imports are a subset of registered allowed edges."}
   255	  - {id: "ORG-CYCLE-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "The context graph and source import graph are acyclic."}
   256	  - {id: "ORG-IMPORT-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Imports are effect-free and do not perform runtime registration or configuration decisions."}
   257	  - {id: "ORG-COMPOSE-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Only bootstrap/composition.py selects and wires concrete product implementations."}
   258	  - {id: "ORG-MESSAGE-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Cross-context state propagation uses public APIs or registered integration events/outboxes, never table/repository sharing."}
   259	  - {id: "ORG-PERSIST-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Each table and migration has one context owner; shared persistence mechanics own no domain schema."}
   260	  - {id: "ORG-TEST-MIRROR-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Every production owner has tests in an ADR-0008 allowed root with matching context/layer metadata."}
   261	  - {id: "ORG-GENERATED-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Schemas and generated artifacts have one semantic source, generator/input digests, and zero hand-edit drift."}
   262	  - {id: "ORG-MIGRATION-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Context migrations are owner-local and cross-context ordering is explicit, tested, and reversible."}
   263	  - {id: "ORG-LEGACY-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Only the compatibility boundary imports inherited/legacy roots; new Ranex packages never do."}
   264	  - {id: "ORG-OWNERSHIP-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Every governed path resolves to one CODEOWNERS-ready semantic owner and required reviewer."}
   265	  - {id: "ORG-DISCOVERY-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Package/module discovery is manifest-driven, side-effect-free, and excludes tests/docs/tools/legacy."}
   266	  - {id: "ORG-NAV-001", enforcement: "REQUIRED", invariant: "Context README and registry links make owner, API, invariants, data, dependencies, operations, and tests findable."}
   267	  - {id: "ORG-EXEMPTION-001", enforcement: "BLOCK", invariant: "Every topology exception is exact-path, exact-rule, owned, justified, time-bounded, and non-transitive."}
   268	```
   269	
   270	Allowed exception classes are only:
   271	
   272	| Class | Scope |
   273	|---|---|
   274	| `FOUNDATION_PRIMITIVE` | Named semantics-light primitive with multiple valid context consumers |
   275	| `BOOTSTRAP_COMPOSITION` | Concrete construction/configuration at the sole composition root |
   276	| `HOST_EDGE_ADAPTER` | Genuinely multi-context delivery/platform adapter implementing named ports |
   277	| `GENERATED_PROJECTION` | Non-hand-edited output with canonical source/generator digests |
   278	| `COMPATIBILITY_QUARANTINE` | Exact inherited import required by the strangler, with removal trigger |
   279	
   280	An exception records `exception_id`, exact paths/globs, rule IDs, owner,
   281	rationale, allowed edges, security/data impact, approval, review/expiry, tests,
   282	and removal condition. Wildcards that cover a whole layer, “temporary”
   283	exceptions without expiry, and transitive exemptions are invalid.
   284	
   285	### Examples
   286	
   287	```text
   288	ALLOWED:
   289	ranex.work_management.application.transition_service
   290	  -> ranex.policy.api.AuthorizationSnapshot
   291	
   292	ALLOWED:
   293	ranex.work_management.adapters.persistence.sqlite.WorkRepository
   294	  implements ranex.work_management.application.ports.WorkRepository
   295	
   296	FORBIDDEN:
   297	ranex.delivery -> ranex.governed_execution.domain.run
   298	ranex.policy.domain -> sqlalchemy
   299	ranex.assurance.application -> ranex.routing.adapters.openrouter
   300	tests -> alternate_test_only_reducer
   301	```
   302	
   303	## Fitness evidence
   304	
   305	| ID | Required evidence |
   306	|---|---|
   307	| `FF-ORG-001` | Registered and actual paths agree; unknown/duplicate owners and misplaced packages fail. |
   308	| `FF-ORG-002` | Static import graph proves allowed inward/public edges, no cycles/private imports, and no legacy leak. |
   309	| `FF-ORG-003` | Import probes prove no filesystem/network/database/process/configuration side effect. |
   310	| `FF-ORG-004` | Composition tests build each release profile from the sole root and reject missing/extra/ambiguous bindings. |
   311	| `FF-ORG-005` | Contract and failure tests prove public API/event translation, duplicate/out-of-order handling, and no shared-table shortcut. |
   312	| `FF-ORG-006` | Migration inventory maps every table/version to one owner and passes ordering, replay, rollback, and restore checks. |
   313	| `FF-ORG-007` | Registry-to-CODEOWNERS/package-discovery/generated-doc projections are reproducible and drift-free. |
   314	| `FF-ORG-008` | Representative changes remain local to the declared owner or expose an explicit architecture decision instead of hidden coupling. |
   315	
   316	These are obligations. No current source tree or build is declared conformant.
   317	
   318	## Engineering-reference application and limitations
   319	
   320	This decision applies, without copying book text:
   361	## Alternatives considered
   362	
   363	1. **Framework-first horizontal layers.** Rejected because product ownership
   364	   and change locality disappear behind controllers/services/repositories.
   365	2. **A universal deep template in every context.** Rejected because empty
   366	   boilerplate and cargo-cult abstractions impede navigation.
   367	3. **One shared domain/common package.** Rejected because semantic ownership
   368	   and consistency boundaries become ambiguous.
   369	4. **Package-per-microservice.** Rejected because process count is unrelated to
   370	   the local-first quality attributes and adds distributed failure.
   371	5. **Let tests/import conventions enforce structure without a registry.**
   372	   Rejected because allowed exceptions and semantic ownership would be implicit.
   373	
   374	## Consequences and migration
   375	
   376	New target work uses this topology. Existing inherited paths remain quarantined
   377	during the strangler. Existing Ranex files move only through an owned,
   378	behavior-preserving migration with import/schema/test updates; no big-bang
   379	cosmetic shuffle is required. Contract generation must validate this ADR
   380	against the path/context registry before `AI-G2`. Until then the decision is
   381	paper-contracted, not enacted.
   382	
   383	## Human approval
   384	
   385	The human owner required enterprise-grade modular-DDD organization without
   386	framework cargo cult or needless boilerplate. This ADR records that target. It
   387	is not runtime conformance evidence.
