  1160	### 12.2 Web dashboard structure
  1161	
  1162	The web dashboard is a presentation application, not another control plane:
  1163	
  1164	```text
  1165	apps/web-dashboard/src/
  1166	├── app/
  1167	├── features/
  1168	│   ├── runs/
  1169	│   ├── work/
  1170	│   ├── evidence/
  1171	│   ├── decisions/
  1172	│   ├── modules/
  1173	│   ├── routes/
  1174	│   ├── operations/
  1175	│   └── recovery/
  1176	├── generated-contracts/
  1177	├── transport/
  1178	└── security/
  1179	```
  1180	
  1181	It imports generated TypeScript contracts, calls authenticated application
  1182	APIs, and renders returned authority state. It contains no transition table,
  1183	gate rule, permit rule, risk rule, or provider credential.
  1184	
  1185	## 13. Enforced source dependency rules
  1186	
  1187	1. `foundation` imports the Python standard library only and has a strict size
  1188	   and consumer review budget.
  1189	2. Any `domain/` imports only its own context domain and `foundation`.
  1190	3. A context crosses another context only through `<other>.api`.
  1191	4. Application code imports ports, never adapter implementations.
  1192	5. Adapters implement ports and may import public APIs.
  1193	6. No adapter imports another adapter; shared host mechanics become a narrowly
  1194	   named port or platform adapter.
  1195	7. Only `bootstrap/composition.py` constructs concrete implementations, reads
  2623	### 16.2 Core-SDLC and execution boundary
  2624	
  2625	- `product_definition` owns capabilities, needs, requirements, outcomes, and
  2626	  product validation.
  2627	- `work_management` alone transitions `WorkItemStatus`.
  2628	- one work item may have many `Run` attempts; `governed_execution` alone
  2629	  transitions each `RunStatus`.
  2630	- `operations` owns incidents; `release_management` owns release/update state;
  2631	  `service_management` owns service commitments and lifecycle triggers.
  2632	- maintenance, retirement, and incident response create linked work items with
  2633	  the applicable `WorkClass`; they do not smuggle new values into
  2634	  `WorkItemStatus`.
  2635	- a run success, merge, release, model verdict, board move, incident mitigation,
  2636	  or capability state change is evidence/input to a work transition, never the
  2637	  transition itself.
  2638	- cross-aggregate mappings and invalidation rules live in
  2639	  `lifecycle-crosswalks.yaml` and `invalidation-graph.yaml`.
  2640	
  2641	`RuleStage` is derived from the owning lifecycle state solely to select
  2642	applicable policy. It is not independently writable.
  2643	
  2644	### 16.3 Gate namespaces
  2645	
  2646	| Namespace | Meaning | Authority |
  2647	|---|---|---|
  2648	| `SDLC-*` | Core-SDLC stage/cross-lifecycle controls, including `SDLC-FORK-000` preflight | Owning SDLC roles plus deterministic requirements |
  2649	| `AI-G0`–`AI-G10` | Exact-subject evidence gates for one agent-assisted execution | `assurance` creates `GateEvaluation`; `governed_execution` only binds a fresh pass |
  2650	| `MAP-*` | Architecture-map completeness assertions over one `ArchitectureSubject` | `assurance` records qualified deterministic evaluation; architecture owner accepts/rejects |
  2651	| `SDLC-ADOPT-*` | Adoption/calibration of the process itself, including `SDLC-ADOPT-FLEET-*` | Process owner/human governor |
  2652	| `GateOutcome` | Runtime exact-subject result | Qualified deterministic gate |
  2653	| Human decision point | Product, architecture, risk, release, destructive or exception authority | Authenticated named human |
  2654	
  2655	IDs never cross namespaces by alias, and no passing namespace implies another
  2656	one passed.
  2657	
  2658	## 17. Command, event, and effect vocabulary
  2659	
  2660	Commands request work; events state facts that already occurred. They are
  2661	different types and cannot share one generic payload. A name without an
  2662	envelope, payload, owner/producer, trigger, consumer, ordering, delivery,
  2663	privacy, compatibility, and replay contract is `DEFINED_NAME_ONLY` and blocks
  2664	`IMPLEMENTATION_START_READY`.
  2665	
  2666	### 17.1 Canonical event envelope and delivery contract
  2667	
  2668	All 40 initially registered events use `DomainEventEnvelopeV1`, projected to
  2669	`schemas/events/domain-event-envelope-v1.schema.json`. Every field below is
  2670	required unless marked nullable:
  2671	
  2672	| Field | Exact type/invariant |
  2673	|---|---|
  2674	| `schema_version` | constant `domain-event-envelope/v1` |
  2675	| `event_id`, `event_name`, `event_version` | exact catalog ID/name and integer `1` |
  2676	| `event_instance_id` | globally unique UUIDv7; immutable and never reused |
  2677	| `owner_context`, `producer_service_id` | exact catalog values; authenticated producer must be owned by that context |
  2678	| `producer_release_digest` | `sha256:<64 lowercase hex>` of the emitting release |
  2679	| `aggregate_type`, `aggregate_id` | exact catalog aggregate and its registered opaque nonempty ID |
  2680	| `source_aggregate_version`, `aggregate_version` | nonnegative integers; resulting version is greater than source version |
  2681	| `aggregate_event_sequence` | positive gap-free integer under `(owner_context, aggregate_type, aggregate_id)` |
  2682	| `subject_ref`, `subject_digest` | governed immutable subject ref and RFC 8785 SHA-256 |
  2683	| `correlation_id` | nonempty trace/workflow ID retained across the whole causal operation |
  2684	| `causation_id` | exact command/event/decision ID that directly caused this event |
  2685	| `idempotency_key` | nonempty stable logical-operation key; same key plus different subject/payload is a conflict |
  2686	| `occurred_at`, `recorded_at` | strict UTC instants with `occurred_at <= recorded_at` |
  2687	| `payload_schema_ref`, `payload_schema_digest` | exact per-row `ranex:event-payload:<EventName>:v1` schema and RFC 8785 SHA-256 |
  2688	| `payload` | closed object matching the row below; no undeclared or nullable-by-omission field |
  3083	## 18. Workflow semantics
  3084	
  3085	The target interpreter supports:
  3086	
  3087	- sequence;
  3088	- deterministic choice;
  3089	- activity request/result;
  3090	- evidence gate;
  3091	- durable signal wait;
  3092	- durable timer;
  3093	- classified retry;
  3094	- cancellation;
  3095	- compensation request;
  3096	- reconciliation wait; and
  3097	- terminal success/failure.
  3098	
  3099	Parallel/fan-out, map, dynamic graph mutation, and richer authoring remain mapped
  3100	extension points under `governed_execution.domain.workflow`, but cannot gain
  3101	authority until their ordering, cancellation, retry, compensation, and replay
  3102	semantics are specified and tested.
  3103	
  3104	Workflow definitions are immutable release- or project-approved data. Models may
  3105	draft definitions, never activate them.
  3106	
  3107	## 19. Effects, idempotency, and reconciliation
  3108	
  3109	An `Activity` is one logical unit of workflow work. It may be pure, invoke a
  3110	worker, or request `0..N` external `Effect` records. Each effect has its own
  3111	identity, destination, authority, idempotency/retry policy, result, and
  3112	reconciliation history. An activity resolves only when its required effects
  3113	have acceptable terminal facts; optional effects and compensation are declared
  3114	in the workflow definition.
  3115	
  3116	Every `ActivityRequest` declares:
  3117	
  3118	- exact subject;
  3119	- activity/effect type;
  3120	- canonical argument digest;
  3121	- required capabilities and declared effects;
  3122	- route/module/isolation profile;
  3123	- one absolute deadline and remaining budgets;
  3124	- idempotency key;
  3125	- timeout and retry policy;
  3126	- expected result/evidence schemas; and
  3127	- compensation/reconciliation contract.
  3128	
  3129	The outbox relay:
  3130	
  3131	1. leases a committed intent;
  3132	2. dispatches with the same logical idempotency key;
  3133	3. validates the typed result;
  3134	4. records provider/external receipts as artifacts;
  3135	5. marks success, classified failure, denial, or `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`;
  3136	6. retries only when policy permits;
  3137	7. sends ambiguous outcomes to an adapter-specific reconciler; and
  3138	8. records the reconciled fact before the run advances.
  3139	
  3140	Reconciliation is an orthogonal record, not a terminal effect outcome. It
  3141	transitions `PENDING -> RUNNING -> RESOLVED | UNRESOLVED`; `RESOLVED` stores the
  3142	discovered effect disposition and moves `EffectStatus` from `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`
  3143	to the proven `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED_*`, or `DENIED` value. History never erases
  3144	that the original acknowledgement was unknown.
  3145	
  3146	GitHub, Git, messaging, provider, filesystem, and database effects each define
  3147	how to query or prove outcome after a lost acknowledgement. “Probably happened”
  3148	is never a terminal state.
  3149	
  3150	## 20. Evidence, review, gates, and human authority
  3151	
  3152	The data flow is deliberately one-way:
  3153	
  3154	```text
  3155	raw artifact
  3156	  -> EvidenceEnvelope
  3157	  -> immutable ReviewObservation reference or qualified CheckerResult
  3158	  -> validated exact-subject evidence snapshot
  3159	  -> GateEvaluation
  3160	  -> optional authenticated HumanDecision
  3161	  -> exact-subject Permit
  3162	  -> authorized transition/effect
  3163	```
  3164	
  3165	Forbidden flows:
  3166	
  3167	- `ReviewObservation -> PASS` without a qualified gate evaluator;
  3168	- model output -> `HumanDecision`;
  3169	- implementer summary -> proof;
  3170	- telemetry record -> evidence merely because it contains a success message;
  3171	- missing result -> zero, pass, approval, or abstaining vote;
  3172	- waiver -> machine `PASS`.
  3173	
  3174	A blocking gate advances automatically only on a fresh exact-subject `PASS`.
  3175	`FAIL`, `UNKNOWN`, `CONFLICT`, `CHECKER_FAULT`, missing/stale evidence, and
  3176	unproven `NOT_APPLICABLE` block.
  3177	
  3178	`analytical_review` alone owns `ReviewObservation` and related review lifecycle
  3179	records. `assurance` alone owns the qualified evidence snapshot and immutable
  3180	`GateEvaluation`. `governed_execution` may bind that evaluation and consume an
  3181	eligible permit in its authority transaction; it cannot create or edit the
  3182	evaluation. `policy` determines requirements/eligibility but does not issue or
  3183	consume the permit.
  3184	
  3185	## 21. Policy, identity, secrets, and human decisions
  3186	
  3187	`identity_access` owns:
  3188	
  3189	- local human principal;
  3190	- service and adapter principals;
  3191	- authenticated sessions and remote-device bindings;
  3192	- nonce, expiry, replay prevention, and challenge confirmation;
  3193	- secret metadata and opaque secret references;
  3194	- data classification and authenticated destination identity/resolution facts;
  3195	  and
  3196	- credential projection records under policy-defined rules.
  3197	
  3198	Secret values are not canonical domain fields. A granted adapter receives the
  3199	smallest read-only projection for one bounded operation. Tool-bearing processes
  3200	receive no real operator home and no authority database mount.
  3201	
  3202	The same `HumanDecision` contract is used by CLI, TUI, local web, phone, and
  3203	GitHub edges. Each surface may render differently, but none defines a separate
  3204	approval authority.
  3205	
  3206	IAM authenticates the principal and presentation/challenge. `policy` records
  3207	the decision and evaluates its eligibility. `governed_execution` copies the
  3208	eligible exact-subject decision into a one-shot `ConsumableAuthorityGrant`.
  3209	Only the grant/permit has `CONSUMED`; the append-only human decision remains
  3210	`APPROVED`, `DENIED`, `EXPIRED`, or `REVOKED`. Revocation and currency are
  3211	checked again before permit issue and effect dispatch.
  3212	
  3337	## 25. Delivery surfaces
  3338	
  3339	All surfaces use the same commands, queries, subject views, authentication, and
  3340	human-decision contracts.
  3341	
  3342	| Surface | Target role | Boundary |
  3343	|---|---|---|
  3344	| CLI | Primary administration and scripted operation | Local authenticated process |
  3345	| TUI | Interactive local operation | Same APIs as CLI |
  3346	| Local web | Queue, evidence, approvals, recovery, health | Bind `127.0.0.1`; authenticated; no public listener |
  3347	| Phone | Remote text control and decisions | Channel-neutral port; Telegram first adapter; nonce/expiry/replay controls |
  3348	| GitHub | Issue intake, PR status, permitted completion effects | Signed/authenticated API/webhook adapter |
  3349	| Webhooks/triggers | Start predeclared workflows | Authenticated, idempotent, no direct transition authority |
  3350	
  3351	The desktop application and its packaging/updater/settings state are excluded.
  3352	The local web surface supplies the visual control plane without duplicating the
  3353	product runtime.
  3354	
  3355	## 26. Storage and data lifecycle
  3356	
  3357	### 26.1 Physical storage
  3358	
  3359	The local target uses:
  3360	
  3361	```text
  3362	$RANEX_HOME/
  3363	├── db/ranex.db
  3364	├── artifacts/sha256/
  3365	├── workspaces/
  3366	├── modules/<module-id>/
  3367	├── profiles/
  3368	├── telemetry/
  3369	├── backups/
  3370	└── compatibility/          # read-only/quarantined legacy input
  3371	```
  3372	
  3373	Configuration shipped in the repository is immutable input. Runtime state lives
  3374	under `$RANEX_HOME`; it is never written into the source tree.
  3375	
  3376	`$RANEX_HOME` has one local-owner identity, mode `0700` or platform-equivalent
  3377	ACL, no worker mount, and explicit subdirectory ownership for authority state,
  3378	artifacts, interaction history, indexes, backups, logs, temporary material, and
  3379	compatibility data. Files containing secrets or classified content use `0600`
  3380	or equivalent. Data classified as sensitive requires an accepted at-rest
  3381	encryption/key-recovery profile; encryption keys never live in the same backup
  3382	payload as the protected data.
  3383	
  3384	### 26.2 Ownership
  3385	
  3386	- Core contexts share one physical SQLite file but own distinct logical tables
  3387	  and repositories.
  3388	- Only the governed authority UoW performs the multi-table transition write.
  3389	- Every other context writes only its owned tables through its repository.
  3390	- Artifacts are written by exclusive temporary creation, fsync, atomic rename,
  3391	  and digest verification before a database record references them.
  3392	- Safe unreferenced blobs may be garbage-collected after policy delay.
  3393	- A missing referenced artifact whose `ArtifactStatus` is not `PURGED` is
  3394	  corruption and cannot satisfy a gate; an effective legal hold neither makes
  3395	  missing bytes valid nor changes availability.
  3396	- Authorized purge first writes a durable tombstone containing artifact ID,
  3397	  former digest, classification, retention/legal decision, authorization,
  3398	  purge method and time. `PURGED` artifacts are intentionally unavailable and
  3399	  cannot satisfy replay or a gate; replay yields typed `EVIDENCE_PURGED`, not
  3400	  generic corruption.
  3401	- Availability, quarantine, expiry, purge, and backup inclusion are explicit
  3402	  artifact state/policy; legal hold is the independently authorized append-only
  3403	  fact chain defined in §16.1. Purge locks and rechecks that no effective hold
  3404	  exists and retains the considered hold-fact digests in its tombstone.
  3405	
  3406	## 27. Security and isolation
  3407	
  3408	Threat assumptions:
  3409	
  3410	- model output and repository content are untrusted;
  3411	- a tool-bearing harness may be mistaken or compromised;
  3412	- a third-party extension is hostile until isolated;
  3413	- external providers are egress destinations;
  3414	- local logs and hash chains are rewriteable by an attacker with equivalent host
  3415	  authority;
  3416	- a symlink, subprocess, background job, plugin, MCP server, or alternate tool
  3417	  path can bypass naive wrappers; and
  3418	- upstream changes can reintroduce removed commercial or privileged behavior.
  3419	
  3420	Required controls:
  3421	
  3422	- deny-by-default capability vocabulary;
  3423	- one application PEP;
  3424	- exact realpath, repository, worktree, and commit validation;
  3608	## 31. Explicit product exclusions
  3609	
  3610	The full map includes explicit borders:
  3611	
  3612	- Electron/desktop application and desktop updater;
  3613	- every Hermes/Nous live inference or parent-agent model loop;
  3614	- Nous Portal/model service and every Hermes/Nous credential, OAuth,
  3615	  entitlement, billing, credit, subscription, payment, managed-tool, purchase,
  3616	  sales, and promotional path;
  3617	- a public internet dashboard;
  3618	- automatic merge;
  3619	- a user-installed plugin in the authority path;
  3620	- same-process hostile Python;
  3621	- prompt text as enforcement;
  3622	- model verdict or model consensus as transition authority;
  3623	- remote catalog activation;
  3624	- broad cross-project shared mutable memory;
  3625	- automatic activation of learned policy or skills;
  3626	- indiscriminate MCP catalog exposure;
  3627	- Kubernetes/microservice control plane for the one-host product;
  3628	- claims of exactly-once external effects;
  3629	- event sourcing every module;
  3630	- an unbounded general workflow platform in the kernel;
  3631	- worker-created assignments, provider subagents/teams, nested delegation, or
  3632	  model-controlled fleet coordination;
  3633	- generic full-tool roles or treating provider `allowedTools` as restriction;
  3634	- ambient worker settings, MCP servers, apps/plugins, skills, agents, memory, or
  3635	  background execution;
  3686	## 33. Architecture and completeness gates
  3687	
  3688	### 33.1 Full-map gates
  3689	
  3690	| ID | Requirement |
  3691	|---|---|
  3692	| `MAP-001` | Every capability zone has one owner, public boundary, lifecycle, trust tier, source location, and security boundary. |
  3693	| `MAP-002` | Every explicit exclusion is listed; no omitted area is mislabeled as deferred. |
  3694	| `MAP-003` | The lifecycle registry has one owner per lifecycle and no orphan states. |
  3695	| `MAP-004` | The path/state/data-ownership registries agree with this document and the source tree. |
  3696	| `MAP-005` | The whole fork layout shows new code, inherited code, compatibility, migration, and upstream-sync boundaries. |
  3697	| `MAP-006` | CLI, TUI, web, phone, and GitHub use the same command/decision authority contracts. |
  3698	| `MAP-007` | Security, operations, data lifecycle, and compliance each have a named owner. |
  3699	| `MAP-008` | Knowledge, tools/MCP, routes, extensions, backup, release, sync, and migration have final attachment points even when inactive. |
  3700	
  3701	Each row is evaluated against the same immutable `ArchitectureSubject`
  3702	manifest. A qualified deterministic map checker produces evidence;
  3703	`assurance` records its `GateEvaluation`; the architecture owner records the
  3704	accept/reject decision. The aggregate map passes only when all applicable rows
  3705	are fresh `PASS` and no blocking `UNKNOWN` or `CONFLICT` exists. `AI-G1`
  3706	consumes those exact evaluations for a run. Accepted paper architecture,
  3707	`AI-G1`, `AI-G2`, and runtime qualification remain distinct facts.
  3708	
  3709	### 33.2 Structural gates
  3710	
  3711	- canonical context/path/layer/public placement:
  3712	  `ORG-PATH-001`, `ORG-CONTEXT-001`, `ORG-LAYER-001`, and `ORG-PUBLIC-001`;
  3713	- allowed-edge, no-cycle, effect-free import, and sole-composition checks:
  3714	  `ORG-DEPENDENCY-001`, `ORG-CYCLE-001`, `ORG-IMPORT-001`, and
  3715	  `ORG-COMPOSE-001`;
  3716	- public messaging, single-owner persistence, test mirroring, generated drift,
  3717	  migration, and legacy quarantine: `ORG-MESSAGE-001`, `ORG-PERSIST-001`,
  3718	  `ORG-TEST-MIRROR-001`, `ORG-GENERATED-001`, `ORG-MIGRATION-001`, and
  3719	  `ORG-LEGACY-001`;
  3720	- semantic owner/reviewer resolution, explicit package discovery, navigation,
  3721	  and exact non-transitive exceptions: `ORG-OWNERSHIP-001`,
  3722	  `ORG-DISCOVERY-001`, `ORG-NAV-001`, and `ORG-EXEMPTION-001`;
  3723	- no inactive module registration or migration;
  3724	- no canonical write outside the authority UoW;
  3725	- no `ExecutionContext` in domain method signatures;
  3726	- generated schema/config/example drift check; and
  3727	- architecture graph diff on every change.
  3728	
  3729	All eighteen `ORG-*` evaluations bind the same exact subject. A missing
  3730	applicable evaluation, unknown/duplicate path owner, private cross-context
  3731	import, unregistered central adapter, or expired/broad exception fails
  3732	structural readiness. Only `ORG-NAV-001` and risk-selected diagnostic evidence
  3733	may be nonblocking when policy explicitly says so; an enforcement label never
  3734	permits a higher invariant to be waived.
  3735	
  3736	### 33.3 Behavioral P0 gates
  3737	
  3738	- reducer purity, property tests, replay, upcasters, and snapshot corruption;
  3739	- crash before/after every authority and artifact boundary;
  3740	- policy/checker timeout, exception, malformed response, and disappearance;
  3741	- exact-subject mismatch and permit CAS/reuse;
  3742	- evidence-versus-observation isolation;
  3743	- packet digest stability over resolved inputs;
  3744	- target-host secret/read/write/process/argv/network/output denial;
  3745	- official-runtime Codex/Claude and every later qualified adapter bypass matrix;
  3746	- sole Ranex cross-worker orchestration, provider nested-lineage denial, and
  3747	  absence of Agent/team/delegation/scheduler tools;
  3748	- immutable role ceiling versus exact assignment-minimal proper-subset grant,
  3749	  startup-attested actual tools, catch-all pre-tool/custom-tool mediation, and
  3750	  no ambient setting/MCP/app/plugin/skill/agent/memory/background source;
  3751	- one explicit model/runtime/auth route with no fallback, auxiliary call, or
  3752	  worker route mutation under outage/overload/malformed-result faults;
  3753	- interrupt, correlated drain, SDK disconnect, outer-supervisor cleanup, exact
  3754	  resume, and assignment/session-affinity isolation;
  3755	- cold/warm first-event/content and total latency, process/model/tool
  3756	  amplification, CPU, memory, cancellation, correctness, and leakage evidence;
  3757	- double-claim, lease-expiry, stale-epoch fencing, heartbeat/reclaim, mailbox
  3758	  dead-letter, child-budget escape, and orphan-worker matrix;
  3759	- result-aware loop detection across syntactic variants and unchanged failure
  3760	  results;
  3761	- path/worktree enforcement through symlink, subprocess, helper, alternate Git,
  3762	  generated-path, and new-fixture collision routes;
  3763	- verifier/human-capacity backpressure without assurance reduction;
  3764	- split-source reconciliation with Hermes/Kanban/projections;
  3765	- ambiguous external effect reconciliation;
  3766	- route identity and re-probation;
  3767	- zero Hermes/Nous inference, credential/entitlement, monetization,
  3768	  runtime/package/network/SBOM gate;
  3769	- clean backup and restore with external reconciliation; and
  3770	- upstream-sync anti-recontamination gate.
  3771	
  3772	### 33.4 Default TDD construction and verification gates
  3773	
  3774	[ADR-0008](./decisions/ADR-0008-make-tdd-the-default-development-discipline.md)
  3775	requires acceptance/risk/failure modeling followed by inspectable
     1	# ADR-0007: Establish Modular-DDD Repository Organization
     2	
     3	| Field | Value |
     4	|---|---|
     5	| ADR ID | `ADR-0007` |
     6	| Version | `1.0.0` |
     7	| Status | `ACCEPTED` |
     8	| Decision owner | Human owner |
     9	| Decision date | 2026-07-28 |
    10	| Effective revision | Working tree based on `4baad4a67843b02d5970f442fb54aed8d6525dda`; executable projection and enactment pending |
    11	| Content binding | Exact digest is recorded externally in each immutable review/release source manifest |
    12	| Affected contexts | Entire repository, every bounded context, adapters, tests, schemas, migrations, deployment, tooling, documentation, and compatibility code |
    13	| Supersedes | No ADR; narrows and completes architecture §§11–13 |
    14	| Review/expiry date | First contract-registry projection, first target context tracer, then on topology/ownership change |
    15	| Compatibility/migration class | Target organization with strangler quarantine for inherited Hermes layout |
    16	| Security/data class | Public architecture decision |
    17	
    18	## Decision
    19	
    20	Ranex uses a modular monolith whose primary organizing unit is the owned
    21	bounded context, not a framework, adapter technology, horizontal “services”
    22	folder, or model/tool vendor. The canonical context path remains:
    23	
    24	```text
    25	src/ranex/<context>/
    26	```
    27	
    28	The package name makes product/domain intent visible. The architecture
    29	registries, not directory inference, decide which names are bounded contexts.
    30	Folder count does not prove modularity, and every context does not need every
    31	optional folder.
    32	
    33	## Canonical repository topology
    34	
    35	```text
    36	ranex/
    37	├── src/ranex/
    38	│   ├── foundation/                 # semantics-light shared primitives
    39	│   ├── <context>/                  # one registered bounded context
    40	│   ├── modules/                    # first-party capability modules
    41	│   ├── adapters/                   # multi-context/host-edge adapters only
    42	│   ├── compatibility/              # anti-corruption boundary only
    43	│   ├── bootstrap/                  # composition root and startup
    44	│   └── migration/                  # cross-context migration ordering
    45	├── apps/                           # delivery applications; no authority rules
    46	├── architecture/contracts/         # owner-authored semantic registries
    47	├── architecture/generated/         # generated projections; never hand edited
    48	├── schemas/<owning-area>/           # canonical wire/artifact schemas
    49	├── packages/generated-contracts/   # generated language bindings
    50	├── tests/                           # ADR-0008 taxonomy, mirroring owners
    51	├── config/                          # release-pinned declarative profiles
    52	├── deploy/                          # declarative packaging/install/host assets
    53	├── docs/
    54	│   ├── architecture/               # normative architecture and specifications
    55	│   │   ├── decisions/              # ADRs
    56	│   │   └── rfcs/                   # non-accepted proposals
    57	│   ├── research/                   # evidence/advice, never authority
    58	│   └── operations/                 # tested runbooks
    59	├── scripts/                        # thin public-API operator/CI clients
    60	├── tools/                          # build, contract generation, validation
    61	├── legacy/hermes/                  # optional post-parity frozen source home
    62	└── legal/                          # license, provenance, SBOM policy
    63	```
    64	
    65	Production domain/application behavior lives only below `src/ranex`. `apps`,
    66	`scripts`, `deploy`, and `tools` cannot become alternate business-rule homes.
    67	Configuration that changes behavior remains versioned, schema-validated, owned,
    68	and tested.
    69	
    70	## Bounded-context internal contract

     2

     5	| Review ID | `REV-KERNEL-TRACER-001` |

    14	
    15	## Why three models
    16	
    17	The prior capability probe recorded that reviewer disagreement is more
    18	informative than reviewer consensus, provided it is resolvable. That held here:
    19	the most consequential result of this audit is a **correction of one reviewer by
    20	another**, and it changes what the fix is.
    21	
    22	## The correction that matters most
    23	
    24	**HY3's blocking finding was wrong, and Codex and the orchestrator shared its
    25	error.**
    26	
    27	HY3 reported as a BLOCKER that the state-authority principle — *"the relational
    28	snapshot, not journal replay, is canonical state authority"* — is declared nowhere
    29	in the corpus, and therefore an undeclared inference enacted silently by code.
    30	Codex did not contradict it. The orchestrator searched independently and also
    31	found nothing. Three readers, plus the prior session that produced the phrase,
    32	all concluded absence.
    33	
    34	Grok found it. `docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md`
    35	§8.3, verified verbatim:
    36	
    37	> - The current row is the operational read source.
    38	> - The ordered journal is the replay and audit oracle.
    39	> - Snapshots may accelerate replay but never replace the journal.
    40	> - A current-row/journal mismatch is corruption and blocks advancement; code
    41	>   does not choose whichever source is convenient.
    42	
    43	Three consequences:
    44	
    45	1. **The architecture declares a dual model, not snapshot supremacy.** The
    46	   session phrase is not merely undeclared — its "snapshot is canonical
    47	   authority" reading is *contrary* to a declared obligation that snapshots never
    48	   replace the journal.
    49	2. **The defect is a violation, not an omission.** The kernel implements the
    50	   first bullet and omits the fourth entirely. There is no mismatch detection
    51	   anywhere in the read path.
    52	3. **The fix changes completely.** "Write the principle down" was the wrong
    53	   remedy. The right one is to implement the blocking mismatch gate §8.3 already
    54	   requires.
    55	
    56	The four earlier searches failed because their terms matched the session phrasing
    57	("canonical state authority", "journal replay") rather than §8.3's own wording
    58	("current row", "operational read source"). A negative search result is evidence
    59	about the search, not about the corpus.
    60	
    61	## Confirmed findings
    62	
    63	Each reproduced by the orchestrator at the cited lines before being recorded.
    64	
    65	### 1. No mismatch detection in the authority read path — violates §8.3
    66	
    67	`load()` selects only from `execution_state`
    68	(`execution_store.py:210-221`). `_decode_state_row` checks row-internal
    69	consistency only — canonical form, and the `version`/`last_event_id`/`execution_id`
    70	columns against the JSON document (`:398-419`). Journal chain continuity is
    71	enforced by no Python code and no SQL constraint: the migration checks
    72	`resulting_version = previous_version + 1` and digest *lengths*
    73	(`001_execution_kernel.sql:11-33`), and `UNIQUE (execution_id, resulting_version)`
    74	does not require a new `previous_version` to equal the current chain head. The
    75	only journal readers in `src/` are row counters (`:366-382`); no event decoder
    76	exists anywhere.
    77	
    78	**Grok executed the exploit rather than inferring it.** After two honest appends
    79	reaching `READY` at version 2, direct SQL rewrote `canonical_state_json` to
    80	`PROPOSED` while keeping version and `last_event_id` internally consistent;
    81	`load()` returned `PROPOSED`. A further forge to `SUCCEEDED` at version 99 with an
    82	invented event ID was likewise returned. The store's advertised tamper detection
    83	catches only internally *inconsistent* forgeries, which is the half its tests
    84	cover (`test_sqlite_execution_store.py:174-189`, `:192-227`).
    85	
    86	### 2. Authorization is bound to the request by identifier alone
    87	
    88	`ApplicationControlPEP.decide` consumes only `well_formed`, `request_bound`,
    89	`gate_passed`, `gate_authorized`, and `reason_codes`
    90	(`application_control_pep.py:42-52`), where `request_bound` is
    91	`evaluation.request_id == request.request_id` (`:48`). It never cross-checks
    92	`gate_id`, action, `policy_digest`, `catalog_digest`, or `evidence_digest`, though
    93	it holds the evidence tuple it forwarded and could recompute the digest exactly as
    94	`gate_controller.py:73-77` does.
    95	
    96	**Codex sharpened this decisively:** `GateEvaluation.__post_init__` forbids PASS
    97	with reason codes and ties `authorized` to PASS (`assurance/domain/gates.py:97-100`)
    98	but **never requires `missing_claim_ids` to be empty on PASS**. So a `PASS` that
    99	simultaneously reports missing critical claims is constructible, and the PEP
   100	discards that field — yielding `permitted=True`. Verified: the field exists at
   101	`gates.py:85` and appears nowhere in the PEP's fact construction.
   102	
   103	### 3. The two Phase 1 exit-criterion tests do not test what their names claim
   104	
   105	**Replay.** `test_execution_replay.py:59-66` builds `direct` by folding
   106	`reduce_execution` over in-memory events and compares it to
   107	`replay_execution(events)` — which is that same fold (`execution.py:226-237`). The
   108	production reducer is compared with itself. No path reads
   109	`execution_journal.event_json`; no serialization round-trip; only the happy path
   110	`PROPOSED→READY→RUNNING→SUCCEEDED`; and no "same commands" comparison though
   111	`HERMES-PROMOTION-032` requires it.
   112	
   113	**Crash boundary.** `test_execution_sqlite_crash_boundary.py:50-59` installs a
   114	SQLite `RAISE(ABORT)` trigger. The resulting exception is caught and rolled back
   115	by the store's own handler (`execution_store.py:359-362`) — the path a real crash
   116	would never execute. No process dies, no fsync or torn-write scenario, and no
   117	`PRAGMA journal_mode` is ever set, so recovery behaviour depends on an unpinned
   118	SQLite default.
   119	
   120	### 4. Confirmed sound: the reducer is pure
   121	
   122	Attacked by all three reviewers and dissolved with evidence by two.
   123	`execution.py` imports only `dataclasses`, `collections.abc`, and sibling domain
   124	modules; timestamps come exclusively from `event.occurred_at`; new state is built
   125	by `replace()` on frozen inputs so an exception can leave no partial state;
   126	`frozenset`s are used for membership only, never iterated into output. The
   127	architecture test independently bans effectful imports and calls in domain code
   128	(`test_kernel_imports.py:18-52`). **No reviewer invented a defect here**, which was
   129	one of the graded expectations of this audit.
   130	
   131	### 5. Lesser confirmed findings
   132	
   133	- `catalog_digest` is accepted on format alone and never bound to catalog bytes
   134	  (`policy/adapters/configuration/.../deterministic.py:27-30`), then forwarded into
   135	  every evaluation. Tests normalize a digest of nothing.
   136	- `_execution_from_document` reconstructs state without cross-field invariants
   137	  (`execution_store.py:83-125`), so a persisted `BLOCKED` with a null
   138	  `blocked_from_status` loads cleanly and steers which `ExecutionUnblocked` the
   139	  reducer will later accept.
   140	- `int(row["version"])` silently coerces (`:408`): a relational `1.5` reads as
   141	  version 1; `'abc'` leaks a raw `ValueError` rather than the store's own
   142	  integrity error.
   143	- The database is world-readable for a window at creation — `chmod(0o600)` runs
   144	  after migration and close (`:200-208`) — while `hash_chain_ledger.py:145-146`
   145	  demonstrates the correct `touch(mode=0o600)`-first pattern.
   146	- The evidence ledger derives both its starting point and expected history from
   147	  the local file (`hash_chain_ledger.py:65-80`), so wholesale replacement or
   148	  truncation recomputes as valid. It detects edits, not substitution.
   149	
   150	## Claims that did not survive
   151	
   152	- **HY3 F1 (BLOCKER)** — "declared nowhere." Disproved above.
   153	- **Codex finding 9 (BLOCKER)** — malformed evidence reaching a decision via
   154	  `exit_code=False` and `artifact_verified="yes"`. `EvidenceRecord.__post_init__`
   155	  already rejects both by type (`gates.py:66-76`). Survives only for duck-typed
   156	  impostors bypassing the dataclass; real, but not blocking.
   157	- Six further claims were self-dissolved by HY3 with evidence, including
   158	  snapshot-column normalization and outbox dispatch state, both correctly
   159	  identified as out of Phase 1 scope.
   160	
   161	## What production practice says the fix is
   162	
   163	From the prior-art sweep (`artifacts/2026-07-30/agent-reports/priorart-B.md`),
   164	four controls make a snapshot and a journal provably agree. **Ranex has one.**
   165	
   166	| Control | Present |
   167	|---|---|
   168	| The journal is the recovery authority; the snapshot is a rebuildable cache | No |
   169	| The snapshot names the exact journal position it represents — Temporal stores the latest history event ID and treats mutable state as valid only when reflected in history | No |
   170	| Events and the synchronous projection commit atomically | **Yes** |
   171	| Verify-or-rebuild: replay from original event bytes must reproduce the snapshot | No |
   172	
   173	And the load-bearing caution: atomicity is not integrity. One transaction proves
   174	all three writes committed or none did; it cannot prove the snapshot was computed
   175	from the journal event it names. A shared implementation bug can atomically commit
   176	mutually inconsistent data.
   177	
   178	For crash testing, SQLite's own documented methodology applies: a parent creates a
   179	durable database, a separate **child process** performs the transition, the parent
   180	kills the child at systematically varied write and commit points, a fresh process
   181	reopens the real file, runs `PRAGMA integrity_check`, and accepts only
   182	complete-old or complete-new state — never a mixture.
   183	
   184	## Disposition
   185	
   186	No code is changed by this review. The confirmed findings become work items:
   187	implement the §8.3 mismatch gate, bind authorization to more than a reusable
   188	identifier, and replace both exit-criterion tests with ones that can fail. None of
   189	this authorizes product code, and `IMPLEMENTATION_START_READY` remains
   190	`NOT_ASSESSED`.
   191	
   192	## Method notes
   193	
   194	- Grok ran at **default** reasoning effort while every HY3 run used `high`, so the
   195	  comparison was tilted against the model that performed best.
   196	- Briefs deliberately withheld the orchestrator's own hypotheses. Of three
   197	  withheld, one was independently found, one was found in a different and more
   198	  serious form, and one was not surfaced.
   199	- Report length did not track quality: the shortest report (36 KB) missed none of
   200	  the longest report's (93 KB) findings and added the §8.3 discovery plus an
   201	  executed exploit.

The accepted architecture is already much more decisive than the five rows suggest: it fixes one governed-execution consistency cell, separates assurance and work-management ownership, makes Kanban a projection, and explicitly excludes desktop. I’m now checking whether those fixed obligations render some “owner choices” already settled, and whether the rewritten replay/crash tests qualify event sourcing rather than merely improve journal integrity.

     1	"""Replay from persisted journal bytes, not from in-memory events.
     2	
     3	The previous replay test folded `reduce_execution` over the same in-memory
     4	events that `replay_execution` folds, and compared the two results. That is a
     5	comparison of the production reducer with itself: it cannot fail unless the
     6	wrapper diverges from the function it wraps, so it could not evidence the
     7	Phase 1 exit criterion. These tests replay the recorded `event_json` bytes and
     8	compare against the snapshot, which is the form that can detect encoder loss,
     9	field drift, and a broken digest chain.
    10	"""

    13

   104	def test_replaying_persisted_journal_reproduces_the_snapshot(
   105	    store: SQLiteExecutionStore,
   106	) -> None:
   107	    drive_to_success(store)
   108	    snapshot = store.load(RUN_ID)

   117	def test_replay_detects_a_corrupted_persisted_event(
   118	    store: SQLiteExecutionStore,
   119	    database_path: Path,
   120	) -> None:
   121	    """Encoder loss or field drift must surface, which the old test could not do."""
   122	    drive_to_success(store)
   123	
   124	    with sqlite3.connect(database_path) as connection:
   125	        connection.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
   126	        row = connection.execute(
   127	            "SELECT event_json FROM execution_journal WHERE resulting_version = 1"
   128	        ).fetchone()
   129	        document = json.loads(str(row["event_json"]))
   130	        document["workflow_request_ref"] = "workflow-request:sha256:drifted"
   131	        connection.execute("DROP TRIGGER execution_journal_reject_update")
   132	        connection.execute(
   133	            "UPDATE execution_journal SET event_json = ? WHERE resulting_version = 1",
   134	            (json.dumps(document, separators=(",", ":"), sort_keys=True),),
   135	        )
   136	
   137	    with pytest.raises(ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError) as raised:
   138	        store.replay_history(RUN_ID)
   139	    assert "digest" in str(raised.value)
   140	
   141	
   142	def test_replay_rejects_an_unknown_persisted_event_type(
   143	    store: SQLiteExecutionStore,
   144	    database_path: Path,
   145	) -> None:
   146	    """An event outside the declared union must fail closed, not be treated as a base event."""
   147	    drive_to_success(store)

   159	            (json.dumps(document, separators=(",", ":"), sort_keys=True),),
   160	        )
   161

     1	"""Rollback-on-error test. NOT a crash test — see test_execution_crash_recovery.py.
     2	
     3	This exercises a SQLite `RAISE(ABORT)` trigger whose exception is caught by the
     4	store's own handler, which issues `ROLLBACK`. That verifies error handling
     5	inside a live process; it does not verify crash durability, because no process
     6	dies and the filesystem is never exposed to an interrupted commit. An
     7	independent audit found the test name over-claimed relative to what its
     8	assertions establish.
     9	
    10	Retained deliberately: rollback-on-statement-error is a real property worth
    11	covering. Crash recovery is covered by killing an actual child process with
    12	SIGKILL in test_execution_crash_recovery.py, following SQLite's documented
    13	methodology.
    14	"""

    17

    52	    store.append(

    54	            event_id=identity("transition", "a12"),

    56	            expected_version=0,
    57	            occurred_at="2026-07-29T03:00:00Z",

    59	            created_by_principal_id=identity("principal", "a14"),

    64	    with sqlite3.connect(database_path) as connection:

    67	            CREATE TRIGGER simulate_crash_before_outbox
    68	            BEFORE INSERT ON execution_outbox
    69	            WHEN NEW.aggregate_version = 2
    70	            BEGIN
    71	                SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'simulated crash before outbox insert');
    72	            END;
    73	            """
    74	        )

FILE .claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/resilience/test_execution_crash_recovery.py
     1	"""Crash recovery exercised by killing a real process, per SQLite's own method.
     2	
     3	The existing crash-boundary test installs a SQLite `RAISE(ABORT)` trigger. The
     4	resulting exception is caught by the store's own `except` handler, which issues
     5	`ROLLBACK` — the code path a real crash never executes. No process dies, the
     6	filesystem is never exposed to an interrupted commit, and removing durability
     7	settings would not fail that test.
     8	
     9	SQLite documents the credible procedure (https://sqlite.org/testing.html): a
    10	parent creates a known durable database, a separate child performs the
    11	transition, the parent kills the child, a fresh process reopens the real file,
    12	runs `PRAGMA integrity_check`, and accepts only the complete old state or the
    13	complete new state — never a mixture.
    14	"""
    15	
    16	from __future__ import annotations
    17	
    18	import os
    19	import signal
    20	import sqlite3
    21	import subprocess
    22	import sys
    23	import textwrap
    24	from pathlib import Path
    25	
    26	import pytest
    27	
    28	from ranex.foundation.identity import Identity
    29	from ranex.governed_execution.adapters.persistence.sqlite.execution_store import (
    30	    SQLiteExecutionStore,
    31	)
    32	from ranex.governed_execution.domain.events import (
    33	    ExecutionCreated,
    34	    ExecutionMarkedReady,
    35	)
    36	from ranex.governed_execution.domain.status import ExecutionStatus
    37	
    38	RUN_ID = Identity.parse(
    39	    "run_01890f47-25a1-7e01-98b3-5f5f6bb25af7", expected_prefix="run"
    40	)
    41	WORK_ITEM_ID = Identity.parse(
    42	    "work_01890f47-25a1-7e02-98b3-5f5f6bb25af7", expected_prefix="work"
    43	)
    44	PRINCIPAL_ID = Identity.parse(
    45	    "principal_01890f47-25a1-7e03-98b3-5f5f6bb25af7", expected_prefix="principal"
    46	)
    47	
    48	
    49	def event_id(sequence: int) -> Identity:
    50	    return Identity.parse(
    51	        f"transition_01890f47-25a1-7f{sequence:02x}-98b3-5f5f6bb25af7",
    52	        expected_prefix="transition",
    53	    )
    54	
    55	
    56	CHILD = textwrap.dedent(
    57	    """
    58	    import os, signal, sys
    59	    from ranex.foundation.identity import Identity
    60	    from ranex.governed_execution.adapters.persistence.sqlite.execution_store import (
    61	        SQLiteExecutionStore,
    62	    )
    63	    from ranex.governed_execution.domain.events import ExecutionMarkedReady
    64	
    65	    database_path, hold = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    66	    run_id = Identity.parse(
    67	        "run_01890f47-25a1-7e01-98b3-5f5f6bb25af7", expected_prefix="run"
    68	    )
    69	    store = SQLiteExecutionStore(database_path)
    70	
    71	    # Die abruptly while the write transaction is open. SIGKILL cannot be
    72	    # handled, so no rollback handler, atexit hook, or context manager runs --
    73	    # the operating system removes the process mid-transaction.
    74	    import ranex.governed_execution.adapters.persistence.sqlite.execution_store as mod
    75	    original = mod.SQLiteExecutionStore._require_journal_agreement
    76	
    77	    real_execute = None
    78	
    79	    class Killer:
    80	        def __init__(self, connection):
    81	            self._connection = connection
    82	
    83	        def __getattr__(self, name):
    84	            return getattr(self._connection, name)
    85	
    86	        def execute(self, statement, *args):
    87	            result = self._connection.execute(statement, *args)
    88	            if hold in statement:
    89	                os.kill(os.getpid(), signal.SIGKILL)
    90	            return result
    91	
    92	    connect = mod.SQLiteExecutionStore._connect
    93	    mod.SQLiteExecutionStore._connect = lambda self: Killer(connect(self))
    94	
    95	    store.append(
    96	        ExecutionMarkedReady(
    97	            event_id=Identity.parse(
    98	                "transition_01890f47-25a1-7f02-98b3-5f5f6bb25af7",
    99	                expected_prefix="transition",
   100	            ),
   101	            execution_id=run_id,
   102	            expected_version=1,
   103	            occurred_at="2026-07-30T02:00:01Z",
   104	            readiness_snapshot_ref="snapshot:sha256:ready",
   105	        )

   111	def seed(database_path: Path) -> None:
   112	    store = SQLiteExecutionStore(database_path)
   113	    store.initialize()
   114	    store.append(
   115	        ExecutionCreated(
   116	            event_id=event_id(1),
   117	            execution_id=RUN_ID,
   118	            expected_version=0,
   119	            occurred_at="2026-07-30T02:00:00Z",
   120	            work_item_id=WORK_ITEM_ID,
   121	            created_by_principal_id=PRINCIPAL_ID,
   122	            workflow_request_ref="workflow-request:sha256:crash",
   123	        )
   124	    )

   127	@pytest.mark.parametrize(
   128	    "kill_after",
   129	    ["UPDATE execution_state", "INSERT INTO execution_journal", "COMMIT"],
   130	    ids=["after-state-update", "after-journal-insert", "at-commit"],
   131	)
   132	def test_process_kill_leaves_only_complete_old_or_new_state(
   133	    tmp_path: Path,
   134	    kill_after: str,
   135	) -> None:
   136	    database_path = tmp_path / "kernel.sqlite3"
   137	    seed(database_path)
   138	
   139	    child = subprocess.run(
   140	        [sys.executable, "-c", CHILD, str(database_path), kill_after],
   141	        capture_output=True,
   142	        env={**os.environ, "PYTHONPATH": "src"},
   143	    )
   144	    # SIGKILL is not catchable; a negative return code proves the process was
   145	    # killed rather than exiting through any application code path.
   146	    assert child.returncode == -signal.SIGKILL, child.stderr.decode()[-400:]
   147	
   148	    # A FRESH process reopens the real file, as SQLite's method requires.
   149	    with sqlite3.connect(database_path) as connection:
   150	        assert connection.execute("PRAGMA integrity_check").fetchone()[0] == "ok"
   151	        assert connection.execute("PRAGMA foreign_key_check").fetchall() == []
   152	
   153	    reopened = SQLiteExecutionStore(database_path)
   154	    state = reopened.load(RUN_ID)
   155	    assert state is not None
   156	
   157	    # Only the complete old state or the complete new state is acceptable.
   158	    assert state.status in {ExecutionStatus.PROPOSED, ExecutionStatus.READY}
   159	    assert state.version in {1, 2}
   160	    if state.status is ExecutionStatus.PROPOSED:
   161	        assert state.version == 1
   162	    else:
   163	        assert state.version == 2
   164	
   165	    # The journal must agree with whichever state survived, which load() now
   166	    # enforces, and replay of persisted bytes must reproduce it.
   167	    assert reopened.replay_history(RUN_ID) == state

    50	class ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(RuntimeError):
    51	    """Stored execution bytes disagree with their relational metadata."""
    52

    54	class ConcurrentExecutionWriteError(RuntimeError):
    55	    """The expected canonical execution version changed before commit."""

    58	def _execution_to_document(execution: Execution) -> dict[str, object]:
    59	    return {
    60	        "schema_version": "execution-state/v1",
    61	        "execution_id": str(execution.execution_id),
    62	        "work_item_id": str(execution.work_item_id),
    63	        "created_by_principal_id": str(execution.created_by_principal_id),
    64	        "workflow_request_ref": execution.workflow_request_ref,
    65	        "status": execution.status.value,
    66	        "version": execution.version,
    67	        "last_event_id": str(execution.last_event_id),
    68	        "updated_at": execution.updated_at,
    69	        "blocked_from_status": (
    70	            execution.blocked_from_status.value
    71	            if execution.blocked_from_status is not None
    72	            else None
    73	        ),
    74	    }

    77	def _require_string(value: object, field: str) -> str:
    78	    if not isinstance(value, str) or not value:
    79	        raise ValueError(f"{field} must be a non-empty string")
    80	    return value
    81

    83	def _execution_from_document(document: Mapping[str, object]) -> Execution:
    84	    if set(document) != _STATE_FIELDS:
    85	        raise ValueError("canonical execution state has unexpected fields")
    86	    if document["schema_version"] != "execution-state/v1":
    87	        raise ValueError("unsupported execution state schema version")
    88	    version = document["version"]
    89	    if isinstance(version, bool) or not isinstance(version, int) or version < 1:
    90	        raise ValueError("execution state version must be a positive integer")
    91	    blocked_from_value = document["blocked_from_status"]
    92	    blocked_from = (
    93	        None
    94	        if blocked_from_value is None
    95	        else ExecutionStatus(_require_string(blocked_from_value, "blocked_from_status"))
    96	    )
    97	    return Execution(
    98	        execution_id=Identity.parse(
    99	            _require_string(document["execution_id"], "execution_id"),
   100	            expected_prefix="run",
   101	        ),
   102	        work_item_id=Identity.parse(
   103	            _require_string(document["work_item_id"], "work_item_id"),
   104	            expected_prefix="work",
   105	        ),
   106	        created_by_principal_id=Identity.parse(
   107	            _require_string(
   108	                document["created_by_principal_id"],
   109	                "created_by_principal_id",
   110	            ),
   111	            expected_prefix="principal",
   112	        ),
   113	        workflow_request_ref=_require_string(
   114	            document["workflow_request_ref"],
   115	            "workflow_request_ref",
   116	        ),
   117	        status=ExecutionStatus(_require_string(document["status"], "status")),
   118	        version=version,
   119	        last_event_id=Identity.parse(
   120	            _require_string(document["last_event_id"], "last_event_id"),
   121	            expected_prefix="transition",
   122	        ),
   123	        updated_at=_require_string(document["updated_at"], "updated_at"),
   124	        blocked_from_status=blocked_from,
   125	    )

   128	_EVENT_TYPES: Mapping[str, type[ExecutionEvent]] = {
   129	    "ExecutionCreated": ExecutionCreated,

   146	
   147	def _event_from_document(document: Mapping[str, object]) -> ExecutionEvent:
   148	    """Rebuild an event from its persisted canonical document.
   149	
   150	    The inverse of `_event_to_document`. Without it a replay test cannot read
   151	    the journal at all, which is why the previous replay test could only fold
   152	    the same in-memory events through the same reducer and compare the result
   153	    with itself. Replay of persisted bytes is the only form that can detect
   154	    encoder loss, field drift, or a broken digest chain.
   155	    """
   156	    if document.get("schema_version") != "execution-event/v1":
   157	        raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   158	            "journal event has an unsupported schema version"
   159	        )
   160	    event_type = document.get("event_type")
   161	    if not isinstance(event_type, str) or event_type not in _EVENT_TYPES:
   162	        raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   163	            "journal event has an unknown event type"
   164	        )
   165	    cls = _EVENT_TYPES[event_type]
   166	    payload: dict[str, object] = {
   167	        "event_id": Identity.parse(
   168	            str(document["event_id"]), expected_prefix="transition"
   169	        ),
   170	        "execution_id": Identity.parse(
   171	            str(document["execution_id"]), expected_prefix="run"
   172	        ),
   173	        "expected_version": document["expected_version"],
   174	        "occurred_at": document["occurred_at"],
   175	    }
   176	    known = set(payload) | {"schema_version", "event_type"}
   177	    for name, value in document.items():
   178	        if name in known:
   179	            continue
   180	        if name in _EVENT_IDENTITY_FIELDS:
   181	            payload[name] = Identity.parse(
   182	                str(value), expected_prefix=_EVENT_IDENTITY_FIELDS[name]
   183	            )
   184	        elif name == "target_status":
   185	            payload[name] = ExecutionStatus(str(value))
   186	        elif name == "blocking_refs":
   187	            if not isinstance(value, list):
   188	                raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   189	                    "journal event blocking_refs is not a list"
   190	                )
   191	            payload[name] = tuple(str(item) for item in value)
   192	        else:
   193	            payload[name] = value
   194	    try:
   195	        return cls(**payload)  # type: ignore[arg-type]
   196	    except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
   197	        raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   198	            "journal event does not satisfy its event contract"
   199	        ) from exc
   200	
   201	
   202	def _event_to_document(event: ExecutionEvent) -> dict[str, object]:
   203	    document: dict[str, object] = {
   204	        "schema_version": "execution-event/v1",
   205	        "event_type": type(event).__name__,
   206	        "event_id": str(event.event_id),
   207	        "execution_id": str(event.execution_id),
   208	        "expected_version": event.expected_version,
   209	        "occurred_at": event.occurred_at,
   210	    }
   211	    if isinstance(event, ExecutionCreated):
   212	        document.update(
   213	            {
   214	                "work_item_id": str(event.work_item_id),
   215	                "created_by_principal_id": str(event.created_by_principal_id),
   216	                "workflow_request_ref": event.workflow_request_ref,
   217	            }
   218	        )
   219	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionMarkedReady):
   220	        document["readiness_snapshot_ref"] = event.readiness_snapshot_ref
   221	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionStarted):
   222	        document["authorization_ref"] = event.authorization_ref
   223	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionWaited):
   224	        document["wait_reason_code"] = event.wait_reason_code
   225	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionResumed):
   226	        document["signal_ref"] = event.signal_ref
   227	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionBlocked):
   228	        document.update(
   229	            {
   230	                "block_reason_code": event.block_reason_code,
   231	                "blocking_refs": list(event.blocking_refs),
   232	            }
   233	        )
   234	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionUnblocked):
   235	        document.update(
   236	            {
   237	                "target_status": event.target_status.value,
   238	                "refreshed_evidence_ref": event.refreshed_evidence_ref,
   239	            }
   240	        )
   241	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionSucceeded):
   242	        document["outcome_ref"] = event.outcome_ref
   243	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionFailed):
   244	        document.update(
   245	            {
   246	                "failure_reason_code": event.failure_reason_code,
   247	                "evidence_ref": event.evidence_ref,
   248	            }
   249	        )
   250	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionCancelled):
   251	        document["decision_ref"] = event.decision_ref
   252	    else:
   253	        raise TypeError(f"unsupported execution event: {type(event).__name__}")
   254	    return document

   256	
   257	class SQLiteExecutionStore(ExecutionRepository):
   258	    """State, audit journal, and outbox committed in one SQLite transaction."""
   259	
   260	    def __init__(self, path: Path) -> None:
   261	        self._path = path
   262	
   263	    def _connect(self) -> sqlite3.Connection:
   264	        connection = sqlite3.connect(
   265	            self._path,
   266	            isolation_level=None,
   267	            timeout=5.0,
   268	        )
   269	        connection.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
   270	        connection.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
   271	        connection.execute("PRAGMA synchronous = FULL")
   272	        return connection
   273	
   274	    def initialize(self) -> None:
   275	        self._path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   276	        migration = _MIGRATION.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   277	        connection = self._connect()
   278	        try:
   279	            connection.executescript(migration)
   280	        finally:
   281	            connection.close()
   282	        self._path.chmod(0o600)
   283	
   284	    def load(self, execution_id: Identity) -> Execution | None:
   285	        self._require_run_id(execution_id)
   286	        with closing(self._connect()) as connection:
   287	            row = connection.execute(
   288	                """
   289	                SELECT execution_id, canonical_state_json, version, last_event_id
   290	                FROM execution_state
   291	                WHERE execution_id = ?
   292	                """,
   293	                (str(execution_id),),
   294	            ).fetchone()
   295	            if row is None:
   296	                return None
   297	            state = self._decode_state_row(row)
   298	            self._require_journal_agreement(connection, row, state)
   299	        return state
   300	
   301	    @staticmethod
   302	    def _require_journal_agreement(
   303	        connection: sqlite3.Connection,
   304	        row: sqlite3.Row,
   305	        state: Execution,
   306	    ) -> None:
   307	        """Reject a snapshot that disagrees with the append-only journal.
   308	
   309	        The architecture declares a dual model: the current row is the
   310	        operational read source and the ordered journal is the replay and
   311	        audit oracle, snapshots never replace the journal, and a
   312	        current-row/journal mismatch is corruption that blocks advancement
   313	        (HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md section 8.3).
   314	
   315	        Row-internal checks in `_decode_state_row` cannot satisfy that
   316	        obligation: a forger who rewrites `canonical_state_json`, `version`
   317	        and `last_event_id` together passes every one of them. Agreement is
   318	        only demonstrable against the journal, which independently records
   319	        the resulting version, the canonical state digest and the event
   320	        identity for each transition.
   321	        """
   322	        head = connection.execute(
   323	            """
   324	            SELECT resulting_version, resulting_state_sha256, event_id
   325	            FROM execution_journal
   326	            WHERE execution_id = ?
   327	            ORDER BY resulting_version DESC
   328	            LIMIT 1
   329	            """,
   330	            (str(state.execution_id),),
   331	        ).fetchone()
   332	        if head is None:
   333	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   334	                "execution state has no journal history"
   335	            )
   336	        if int(head["resulting_version"]) != state.version:
   337	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   338	                "execution state version disagrees with journal head version"
   339	            )
   340	        if str(head["event_id"]) != str(state.last_event_id):
   341	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   342	                "execution state last event disagrees with journal head event"
   343	            )
   344	        snapshot_digest = canonical_sha256(
   345	            json.loads(str(row["canonical_state_json"]))
   346	        )
   347	        if str(head["resulting_state_sha256"]) != snapshot_digest:
   348	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   349	                "execution state digest disagrees with journal head digest"
   350	            )
   351	        gap = connection.execute(
   352	            """
   353	            SELECT COUNT(*) AS present
   354	            FROM execution_journal
   355	            WHERE execution_id = ?
   356	            """,
   357	            (str(state.execution_id),),
   358	        ).fetchone()
   359	        if int(gap["present"]) != state.version:
   360	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   361	                "journal row count disagrees with execution state version"
   362	            )
   363	
   364	    def replay_history(self, execution_id: Identity) -> Execution:
   365	        """Rebuild state by replaying the persisted journal, ignoring the snapshot.
   366	
   367	        This is the verify-or-rebuild control: the journal is the replay and
   368	        audit oracle, and replaying its recorded bytes must reproduce the
   369	        snapshot. Because it decodes `event_json` rather than reusing
   370	        in-memory events, it can detect encoder loss, field drift, and a
   371	        broken digest chain that a same-reducer comparison cannot.
   372	        """
   373	        self._require_run_id(execution_id)
   374	        with closing(self._connect()) as connection:
   375	            rows = connection.execute(
   376	                """
   377	                SELECT event_json, resulting_version, resulting_state_sha256
   378	                FROM execution_journal
   379	                WHERE execution_id = ?
   380	                ORDER BY resulting_version ASC
   381	                """,
   382	                (str(execution_id),),
   383	            ).fetchall()
   384	        if not rows:
   385	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   386	                "execution has no journal history to replay"
   387	            )
   388	        state: Execution | None = None
   389	        for expected_version, row in enumerate(rows, start=1):
   390	            if int(row["resulting_version"]) != expected_version:
   391	                raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   392	                    "journal history has a version gap"
   393	                )
   394	            event = _event_from_document(json.loads(str(row["event_json"])))
   395	            state = reduce_execution(state, event)
   396	            if canonical_sha256(_execution_to_document(state)) != str(
   397	                row["resulting_state_sha256"]
   398	            ):
   399	                raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   400	                    "replayed state digest disagrees with journal digest"
   401	                )
   402	        assert state is not None
   403	        return state
   404	
   405	    def append(self, event: ExecutionEvent) -> Execution:
   406	        connection = self._connect()
   407	        connection.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
   408	        try:
   409	            current_row = connection.execute(
   410	                """
   411	                SELECT execution_id, canonical_state_json, version, last_event_id
   412	                FROM execution_state
   413	                WHERE execution_id = ?
   414	                """,
   415	                (str(event.execution_id),),
   416	            ).fetchone()
   417	            current = (
   418	                None if current_row is None else self._decode_state_row(current_row)
   419	            )
   420	            next_state = reduce_execution(current, event)
   421	            next_document = _execution_to_document(next_state)
   422	            next_json = canonical_json(next_document)
   423	
   424	            if current is None:
   425	                connection.execute(
   426	                    """
   427	                    INSERT INTO execution_state(
   428	                        execution_id,
   429	                        canonical_state_json,
   430	                        version,
   431	                        last_event_id,
   432	                        updated_at
   433	                    ) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
   434	                    """,
   435	                    (
   436	                        str(next_state.execution_id),
   437	                        next_json,
   438	                        next_state.version,
   439	                        str(next_state.last_event_id),
   440	                        next_state.updated_at,
   441	                    ),
   442	                )
   443	                previous_json = None
   444	                previous_version = 0
   445	            else:
   446	                assert current_row is not None
   447	                previous_json = str(current_row["canonical_state_json"])
   448	                previous_version = current.version
   449	                cursor = connection.execute(
   450	                    """
   451	                    UPDATE execution_state
   452	                    SET canonical_state_json = ?,
   453	                        version = ?,
   454	                        last_event_id = ?,
   455	                        updated_at = ?
   456	                    WHERE execution_id = ?
   457	                      AND version = ?
   458	                      AND canonical_state_json = ?
   459	                    """,
   460	                    (
   461	                        next_json,
   462	                        next_state.version,
   463	                        str(next_state.last_event_id),
   464	                        next_state.updated_at,
   465	                        str(next_state.execution_id),
   466	                        current.version,
   467	                        previous_json,
   468	                    ),
   469	                )
   470	                if cursor.rowcount != 1:
   471	                    raise ConcurrentExecutionWriteError(
   472	                        "canonical execution compare-and-swap failed"
   473	                    )
   474	
   475	            event_document = _event_to_document(event)
   476	            event_json = canonical_json(event_document)
   477	            resulting_digest = canonical_sha256(next_document)
   478	            previous_digest = (
   479	                None
   480	                if previous_json is None
   481	                else canonical_sha256(json.loads(previous_json))
   482	            )
   483	            connection.execute(
   484	                """
   485	                INSERT INTO execution_journal(
   486	                    event_id,
   487	                    execution_id,
   488	                    event_type,
   489	                    previous_version,
   490	                    resulting_version,
   491	                    event_json,
   492	                    previous_state_sha256,
   493	                    resulting_state_sha256,
   494	                    resulting_state_json,
   495	                    recorded_at
   496	                ) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
   497	                """,
   498	                (
   499	                    str(event.event_id),
   500	                    str(event.execution_id),
   501	                    type(event).__name__,
   502	                    previous_version,
   503	                    next_state.version,
   504	                    event_json,
   505	                    previous_digest,
   506	                    resulting_digest,
   507	                    next_json,
   508	                    event.occurred_at,
   509	                ),
   510	            )
   511	
   512	            outbox_document = {
   513	                "schema_version": "execution-outbox/v1",
   514	                "event_id": str(event.event_id),
   515	                "event_type": type(event).__name__,
   516	                "execution_id": str(event.execution_id),
   517	                "aggregate_version": next_state.version,
   518	                "occurred_at": event.occurred_at,
   519	                "event": event_document,
   520	            }
   521	            connection.execute(
   522	                """
   523	                INSERT INTO execution_outbox(
   524	                    event_id,
   525	                    execution_id,
   526	                    aggregate_version,
   527	                    payload_json,
   528	                    created_at
   529	                ) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)

   542	            if connection.in_transaction:
   543	                connection.execute("ROLLBACK")
   544	            raise

   548	    def count_journal_entries(self, execution_id: Identity) -> int:
   549	        return self._count_rows("execution_journal", execution_id)
   550	
   551	    def count_outbox_entries(self, execution_id: Identity) -> int:
   552	        return self._count_rows("execution_outbox", execution_id)
   553	
   554	    def _count_rows(self, table: str, execution_id: Identity) -> int:
   555	        self._require_run_id(execution_id)
   556	        if table not in {"execution_journal", "execution_outbox"}:
   557	            raise ValueError("unsupported execution table")
   558	        with closing(self._connect()) as connection:
   559	            row = connection.execute(
   560	                f"SELECT COUNT(*) AS count FROM {table} WHERE execution_id = ?",
   561	                (str(execution_id),),
   562	            ).fetchone()
   563	        assert row is not None
   564	        return int(row["count"])
   565	
   566	    @staticmethod
   567	    def _require_run_id(execution_id: Identity) -> None:
   568	        if not isinstance(execution_id, Identity) or execution_id.prefix != "run":
   569	            raise ValueError("execution_id must be a canonical run identity")

   572	    def _decode_state_row(row: sqlite3.Row) -> Execution:
   573	        raw = str(row["canonical_state_json"])
   574	        try:

   577	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   578	                "canonical execution state is not JSON"

   580	        if not isinstance(document, dict) or canonical_json(document) != raw:
   581	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   582	                "execution state is not canonically serialized"
   583	            )

   585	            state = _execution_from_document(document)
   586	        except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
   587	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   588	                "canonical execution state is invalid"
   589	            ) from exc
   590	        if int(row["version"]) != state.version:
   591	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   592	                "relational version disagrees with canonical state version"
   593	            )
   594	        if str(row["last_event_id"]) != str(state.last_event_id):
   595	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(

   597	            )
   598	        if str(row["execution_id"]) != str(state.execution_id):
   599	            raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   600	                "relational identity disagrees with canonical state identity"
   601	            )

     1	BEGIN IMMEDIATE;
     2	
     3	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS execution_state (
     4	    execution_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
     5	    canonical_state_json TEXT NOT NULL,
     6	    version INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (version >= 1),
     7	    last_event_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
     8	    updated_at TEXT NOT NULL
     9	);
    10	
    11	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS execution_journal (
    12	    sequence INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    13	    event_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    14	    execution_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    15	    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    16	    previous_version INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (previous_version >= 0),
    17	    resulting_version INTEGER NOT NULL,
    18	    event_json TEXT NOT NULL,
    19	    previous_state_sha256 TEXT,
    20	    resulting_state_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL,
    21	    resulting_state_json TEXT NOT NULL,
    22	    recorded_at TEXT NOT NULL,
    23	    FOREIGN KEY (execution_id)
    24	        REFERENCES execution_state(execution_id),
    25	    UNIQUE (execution_id, resulting_version),
    26	    CHECK (resulting_version = previous_version + 1),
    27	    CHECK (
    28	        (previous_version = 0 AND previous_state_sha256 IS NULL)
    29	        OR
    30	        (previous_version > 0 AND length(previous_state_sha256) = 64)
    31	    ),
    32	    CHECK (length(resulting_state_sha256) = 64)
    33	);
    34	
    35	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS execution_outbox (
    36	    sequence INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    37	    event_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    38	    execution_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    39	    aggregate_version INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (aggregate_version >= 1),
    40	    payload_json TEXT NOT NULL,
    41	    created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
    42	    FOREIGN KEY (event_id)
    43	        REFERENCES execution_journal(event_id),
    44	    UNIQUE (execution_id, aggregate_version)
    45	);
    46	
    47	CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS execution_journal_reject_update
    48	BEFORE UPDATE ON execution_journal
    49	BEGIN
    50	    SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'execution_journal is append-only');
    51	END;
    52	
    53	CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS execution_journal_reject_delete
    54	BEFORE DELETE ON execution_journal
    55	BEGIN
    56	    SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'execution_journal is append-only');
    57	END;
    58	
    59	CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS execution_outbox_reject_update
    60	BEFORE UPDATE ON execution_outbox
    61	BEGIN
    62	    SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'execution_outbox is append-only');
    63	END;
    64	
    65	CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS execution_outbox_reject_delete
    66	BEFORE DELETE ON execution_outbox
    67	BEGIN
    68	    SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'execution_outbox is append-only');
    69	END;
    70	
    71	PRAGMA user_version = 1;
    72	
    73	COMMIT;

   127	@pytest.mark.parametrize(
   128	    "kill_after",
   129	    ["UPDATE execution_state", "INSERT INTO execution_journal", "COMMIT"],
   130	    ids=["after-state-update", "after-journal-insert", "at-commit"],

   132	def test_process_kill_leaves_only_complete_old_or_new_state(
   133	    tmp_path: Path,
   134	    kill_after: str,
   135	) -> None:
   136	    database_path = tmp_path / "kernel.sqlite3"
   137	    seed(database_path)
   138	
   139	    child = subprocess.run(
   140	        [sys.executable, "-c", CHILD, str(database_path), kill_after],
   141	        capture_output=True,
   142	        env={**os.environ, "PYTHONPATH": "src"},
   143	    )
   144	    # SIGKILL is not catchable; a negative return code proves the process was
   145	    # killed rather than exiting through any application code path.
   146	    assert child.returncode == -signal.SIGKILL, child.stderr.decode()[-400:]
   147	
   148	    # A FRESH process reopens the real file, as SQLite's method requires.
   149	    with sqlite3.connect(database_path) as connection:
   150	        assert connection.execute("PRAGMA integrity_check").fetchone()[0] == "ok"
   151	        assert connection.execute("PRAGMA foreign_key_check").fetchall() == []
   152	
   153	    reopened = SQLiteExecutionStore(database_path)
   154	    state = reopened.load(RUN_ID)
   155	    assert state is not None
   156	
   157	    # Only the complete old state or the complete new state is acceptable.
   158	    assert state.status in {ExecutionStatus.PROPOSED, ExecutionStatus.READY}
   159	    assert state.version in {1, 2}
   160	    if state.status is ExecutionStatus.PROPOSED:
   161	        assert state.version == 1
   162	    else:
   163	        assert state.version == 2
   164	
   165	    # The journal must agree with whichever state survived, which load() now
   166	    # enforces, and replay of persisted bytes must reproduce it.
   167	    assert reopened.replay_history(RUN_ID) == state

    83	def _execution_from_document(document: Mapping[str, object]) -> Execution:
