   145	
   146	
   147	def _event_from_document(document: Mapping[str, object]) -> ExecutionEvent:
   148	    """Rebuild an event from its persisted canonical document.
   149	
   150	    The inverse of `_event_to_document`. Without it a replay test cannot read
   151	    the journal at all, which is why the previous replay test could only fold
   152	    the same in-memory events through the same reducer and compare the result
   153	    with itself. Replay of persisted bytes is the only form that can detect
   154	    encoder loss, field drift, or a broken digest chain.
   155	    """
   156	    if document.get("schema_version") != "execution-event/v1":
   157	        raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   158	            "journal event has an unsupported schema version"
   159	        )
   160	    event_type = document.get("event_type")
   161	    if not isinstance(event_type, str) or event_type not in _EVENT_TYPES:
   162	        raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   163	            "journal event has an unknown event type"
   164	        )
   165	    cls = _EVENT_TYPES[event_type]
   166	    payload: dict[str, object] = {
   167	        "event_id": Identity.parse(
   168	            str(document["event_id"]), expected_prefix="transition"
   169	        ),
   170	        "execution_id": Identity.parse(
   171	            str(document["execution_id"]), expected_prefix="run"
   172	        ),
   173	        "expected_version": document["expected_version"],
   174	        "occurred_at": document["occurred_at"],
   175	    }
   176	    known = set(payload) | {"schema_version", "event_type"}
   177	    for name, value in document.items():
   178	        if name in known:
   179	            continue
   180	        if name in _EVENT_IDENTITY_FIELDS:
   181	            payload[name] = Identity.parse(
   182	                str(value), expected_prefix=_EVENT_IDENTITY_FIELDS[name]
   183	            )
   184	        elif name == "target_status":
   185	            payload[name] = ExecutionStatus(str(value))
   186	        elif name == "blocking_refs":
   187	            if not isinstance(value, list):
   188	                raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   189	                    "journal event blocking_refs is not a list"
   190	                )
   191	            payload[name] = tuple(str(item) for item in value)
   192	        else:
   193	            payload[name] = value
   194	    try:
   195	        return cls(**payload)  # type: ignore[arg-type]
   196	    except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
   197	        raise ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError(
   198	            "journal event does not satisfy its event contract"
   199	        ) from exc
   200	
   201	
   202	def _event_to_document(event: ExecutionEvent) -> dict[str, object]:
   203	    document: dict[str, object] = {
   204	        "schema_version": "execution-event/v1",
   205	        "event_type": type(event).__name__,
   206	        "event_id": str(event.event_id),
   207	        "execution_id": str(event.execution_id),
   208	        "expected_version": event.expected_version,
   209	        "occurred_at": event.occurred_at,
   210	    }
   211	    if isinstance(event, ExecutionCreated):
   212	        document.update(
   213	            {
   214	                "work_item_id": str(event.work_item_id),
   215	                "created_by_principal_id": str(event.created_by_principal_id),
   216	                "workflow_request_ref": event.workflow_request_ref,
   217	            }
   218	        )
   219	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionMarkedReady):
   220	        document["readiness_snapshot_ref"] = event.readiness_snapshot_ref
   221	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionStarted):
   222	        document["authorization_ref"] = event.authorization_ref
   223	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionWaited):
   224	        document["wait_reason_code"] = event.wait_reason_code
   225	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionResumed):
   226	        document["signal_ref"] = event.signal_ref
   227	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionBlocked):
   228	        document.update(
   229	            {
   230	                "block_reason_code": event.block_reason_code,
   231	                "blocking_refs": list(event.blocking_refs),
   232	            }
   233	        )
   234	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionUnblocked):
   235	        document.update(
   236	            {
   237	                "target_status": event.target_status.value,
   238	                "refreshed_evidence_ref": event.refreshed_evidence_ref,
   239	            }
   240	        )
   241	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionSucceeded):
   242	        document["outcome_ref"] = event.outcome_ref
   243	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionFailed):
   244	        document.update(
   245	            {
   246	                "failure_reason_code": event.failure_reason_code,
   247	                "evidence_ref": event.evidence_ref,
   248	            }
   249	        )
   250	    elif isinstance(event, ExecutionCancelled):
   251	        document["decision_ref"] = event.decision_ref
   252	    else:
   253	        raise TypeError(f"unsupported execution event: {type(event).__name__}")
   254	    return document
   255

    80	def test_transition_commits_canonical_state_version_journal_and_outbox_together(
    81	    store: SQLiteExecutionStore,
    82	    database_path: Path,
    83	) -> None:
    84	    proposed = store.append(create_event())
    85	    ready = store.append(ready_event())
    86	
    87	    assert proposed.status is ExecutionStatus.PROPOSED
    88	    assert proposed.version == 1
    89	    assert ready.status is ExecutionStatus.READY
    90	    assert ready.version == 2
    91	    assert store.load(RUN_ID) == ready
    92	
    93	    with sqlite3.connect(database_path) as connection:
    94	        connection.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    95	        state_row = connection.execute(
    96	            """
    97	            SELECT canonical_state_json, version, last_event_id
    98	            FROM execution_state
    99	            WHERE execution_id = ?
   100	            """,
   101	            (str(RUN_ID),),
   102	        ).fetchone()
   103	        journal_rows = connection.execute(
   104	            """
   105	            SELECT event_id, previous_version, resulting_version,
   106	                   event_json, resulting_state_json
   107	            FROM execution_journal
   108	            ORDER BY sequence
   109	            """
   110	        ).fetchall()
   111	        outbox_rows = connection.execute(
   112	            """
   113	            SELECT event_id, aggregate_version, payload_json
   114	            FROM execution_outbox
   115	            ORDER BY sequence
   116	            """
   117	        ).fetchall()
   118	
   119	    assert state_row is not None
   120	    state_document = json.loads(state_row["canonical_state_json"])
   121	    assert state_row["canonical_state_json"] == canonical_json(state_document)
   122	    assert state_row["version"] == state_document["version"] == 2
   123	    assert state_row["last_event_id"] == str(event_id(2))
   124	
   125	    assert [
   126	        (row["event_id"], row["previous_version"], row["resulting_version"])
   127	        for row in journal_rows
   128	    ] == [
   129	        (str(event_id(1)), 0, 1),
   130	        (str(event_id(2)), 1, 2),
   131	    ]
   132	    assert all(
   133	        row["event_json"] == canonical_json(json.loads(row["event_json"]))
   134	        and row["resulting_state_json"]
   135	        == canonical_json(json.loads(row["resulting_state_json"]))
   136	        for row in journal_rows
   137	    )
   138	
   139	    assert [(row["event_id"], row["aggregate_version"]) for row in outbox_rows] == [
   140	        (str(event_id(1)), 1),
   141	        (str(event_id(2)), 2),
   142	    ]
   143	    assert all(
   144	        row["payload_json"] == canonical_json(json.loads(row["payload_json"]))
   145	        for row in outbox_rows
   146	    )

   149	def test_journal_is_database_enforced_append_only(
   150	    store: SQLiteExecutionStore,
   151	    database_path: Path,
   152	) -> None:
   153	    store.append(create_event())
   154	
   155	    with sqlite3.connect(database_path) as connection:
   156	        with pytest.raises(sqlite3.DatabaseError, match="append-only"):
   157	            connection.execute("UPDATE execution_journal SET event_type = 'forged'")
   158	        with pytest.raises(sqlite3.DatabaseError, match="append-only"):
   159	            connection.execute("DELETE FROM execution_journal")

   231	def test_concurrent_same_version_transitions_have_one_cas_winner(
   232	    store: SQLiteExecutionStore,
   233	) -> None:
   234	    store.append(create_event())
   235	    barrier = Barrier(2)
   236	
   237	    def attempt() -> Execution | Exception:
   238	        barrier.wait()
   239	        try:
   240	            return store.append(ready_event())
   241	        except Exception as exc:
   242	            return exc
   243	
   244	    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as executor:
   245	        results = tuple(executor.map(lambda _index: attempt(), range(2)))
   246	
   247	    assert sum(isinstance(result, Execution) for result in results) == 1
   248	    assert sum(isinstance(result, Exception) for result in results) == 1
   249	    assert store.load(RUN_ID).version == 2  # type: ignore[union-attr]
   250	    assert store.count_journal_entries(RUN_ID) == 2
   251	    assert store.count_outbox_entries(RUN_ID) == 2
   252

   254	def _journal_head_digest(database_path: Path) -> str:
   255	    with sqlite3.connect(database_path) as connection:
   256	        connection.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
   257	        return str(
   258	            connection.execute(
   259	                """
   260	                SELECT resulting_state_sha256
   261	                FROM execution_journal
   262	                WHERE execution_id = ?
   263	                ORDER BY resulting_version DESC
   264	                LIMIT 1
   265	                """,
   266	                (str(RUN_ID),),
   267	            ).fetchone()["resulting_state_sha256"]
   268	        )

   271	def test_load_blocks_internally_consistent_snapshot_forgery(
   272	    store: SQLiteExecutionStore,
   273	    database_path: Path,
   274	) -> None:
   275	    """A forged snapshot that passes every row-internal check must still block.
   276	
   277	    HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md section 8.3 requires that a
   278	    current-row/journal mismatch is corruption and blocks advancement. This is
   279	    the exact attack an independent adversarial audit executed successfully
   280	    against the previous implementation: rewrite canonical_state_json, version
   281	    and last_event_id together so the row agrees with itself, and load()
   282	    returned the forged state.
   283	    """
   284	    store.append(create_event())
   285	    store.append(ready_event())
   286	    honest = store.load(RUN_ID)
   287	    assert honest is not None
   288	    assert honest.status is ExecutionStatus.READY
   289	    assert honest.version == 2
   290	
   291	    document = json.loads(canonical_json(_execution_to_document(honest)))
   292	    document["status"] = ExecutionStatus.SUCCEEDED.value
   293	    document["version"] = 99
   294	    document["last_event_id"] = str(event_id(0x5A))
   295	    forged = canonical_json(document)
   296	
   297	    with sqlite3.connect(database_path) as connection:
   298	        connection.execute(
   299	            """
   300	            UPDATE execution_state
   301	            SET canonical_state_json = ?, version = ?, last_event_id = ?
   302	            WHERE execution_id = ?
   303	            """,
   304	            (forged, 99, str(event_id(0x5A)), str(RUN_ID)),
   305	        )
   306	
   307	    with pytest.raises(ExecutionPersistenceIntegrityError) as raised:
   308	        store.load(RUN_ID)
   309	    assert "journal" in str(raised.value)
   310	
   311

   317	    store.append(create_event())
   318	    store.append(ready_event())
   319	    loaded = store.load(RUN_ID)
   320	    assert loaded is not None

  6685	
  6686	## Adopt shortlist
  6687	
  6688	1. **Temporal/Cadence history replay semantics**
  6689	
  6690	   Adopt actual-history replay, command matching, non-determinism failure, and recorded version markers—not either server wholesale. Temporal is MIT ([licence](https://github.com/temporalio/temporal/blob/main/LICENSE)); Cadence is Apache-2.0 ([licence](https://github.com/cadence-workflow/cadence/blob/master/LICENSE)). Both are compatible with Ranex’s commercial optionality when notices and Apache patent terms are observed. Temporal reports more than 1,000 paying cloud customers including NVIDIA, Snap, Alaska Airlines, Retool, and Turo ([adoption evidence](https://temporal.io/news/temporal-surpasses-1-000-active-customers-milestone-in-just-over-a-year)).
  6691	
  6692	2. **SQLite-backed transactional state/journal/outbox**
  6693	
  6694	   Keep the current one-unit-of-work direction, adding explicit head agreement and delivery receipts. SQLite is public domain ([copyright](https://www.sqlite.org/copyright.html)); DBOS’s open-source SDK is MIT ([licence](https://github.com/dbos-inc/dbos-transact-py/blob/main/LICENSE)); the AWS outbox pattern itself is architecture guidance, not licensed library code. DBOS names Walmart, Notion, Roche, Yutori, and Bristol Myers Squibb as users/customers ([customer stories](https://www.dbos.dev/customer-stories)).
  6695	
  6696	3. **SQLite’s real crash-test model**
  6697	
  6698	   Adopt the parent/child crash loop, all-old-or-all-new oracle, integrity checks, and—where the guarantee warrants it—fault-injecting VFS. SQLite’s public-domain status is fully compatible. This is much stronger evidence than an application exception test.
  6699	
  6700	4. **Argo/Airflow server-owned graph projection plus Kubernetes cursor semantics**
  6701	
  6702	   Adopt the behavioral contracts, not necessarily their implementations. Argo, Airflow, and Kubernetes are Apache-2.0 and commercially compatible with notice/patent compliance ([Argo licence](https://github.com/argoproj/argo-workflows/blob/main/LICENSE), [Airflow licence](https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/main/LICENSE), [Kubernetes licence](https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes)). Argo is CNCF graduated with reported production use by Adobe, BlackRock, Capital One, Google, Intuit, and Tesla ([CNCF evidence](https://www.cncf.io/announcements/2022/12/06/the-cloud-native-computing-foundation-announces-argo-has-graduated/)). Apache reported Airflow use across more than 200 organizations including Adobe, Airbnb, Etsy, Google, Lyft, PayPal, Reddit, and Twitter ([ASF evidence](https://news.apache.org/foundation/entry/the-apache-software-foundation-announces44)).
  6703	
  6704	5. **React Flow plus Dagre**
  6705	
  6706	   Both are MIT and compatible with the Ranex licence if notices are preserved. They are suitable view dependencies because their output need not be authoritative.
  6707	
  6708	6. **Axon-style event revisions and upcasters**
  6709	
  6710	   Adopt the revision/upcaster behavior and fixtures. Axon Framework is Apache-2.0 ([repository/licence](https://github.com/AxonIQ/AxonFramework)), compatible with future commercial use subject to normal Apache obligations.
  6711	
  6712	Licence caveats:
  6713	
  6714	- **Restate:** useful corroboration because it explicitly reports an exact journal mismatch during replay and pins invocations to deployments ([errors](https://docs.restate.dev/references/errors), [versioning](https://docs.restate.dev/services/versioning)), but its server is BSL 1.1 with service-use restrictions before conversion to Apache-2.0 ([licence](https://github.com/restatedev/restate/blob/main/LICENSE)). Study the protocol; do not embed it without commercial/legal review.
  6715	- **Dagu:** GPL-3.0; unsuitable for code incorporation into the present proprietary product without separate terms.
  6716	- **ELK:** EPL-2.0; potentially usable, but reciprocal obligations need legal review.
  6717	- **NServiceBus:** cited for its protocol, not recommended as a code dependency; it is a commercial product.
  6718	- This is an engineering compatibility assessment, not legal advice.
  6719	
  6720	## Answers to the three direct questions
  6721	
  6722	### 1. How do production systems make a snapshot and a journal provably agree?
  6723	
  6724	They use four complementary controls:
  6725	
  6726	1. **The event stream is the recovery authority.** A snapshot is a cache or materialized projection that can be discarded and rebuilt, not a second independent truth ([Microsoft Event Sourcing](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/event-sourcing), [Axon snapshots](https://docs.axoniq.io/axon-framework-reference/development/tuning/snapshotting/)).
  6727	
  6728	2. **The snapshot identifies the precise journal position it represents.** Temporal stores the latest history event ID in mutable state and considers that state valid only when the event is reflected in history ([Temporal History Service](https://github.com/temporalio/temporal/blob/main/docs/architecture/history-service.md)).
  6729	
  6730	3. **Events and synchronous projections are committed atomically.** Marten calls this an inline projection and updates it in the same ACID transaction as the events ([Marten projections](https://martendb.io/tutorials/read-model-projections)).
  6731	
  6732	4. **They verify or rebuild.** Replay from original event bytes must reproduce the snapshot, and a corrupt or incompatible snapshot falls back to full stream replay.
  6733	
  6734	Ranex currently has only item 3. One transaction proves that all three writes committed or none did; it does **not** prove that the snapshot was computed from the journal event it names. A shared implementation bug can atomically commit mutually inconsistent data.
  6735	
  6736	Hashes help detect byte disagreement, but they cannot prove that the reducer or command semantics were correct.
  6737	
  6738	### 2. What is the accepted way to test crash recovery, concretely?
  6739	
  6740	SQLite’s own method is the credible baseline:
  6741	
  6742	1. A parent process creates a known durable starting database.
  6743	2. A separate child performs the transition.
  6744	3. The parent kills the child at systematically varied write/commit points.
  6745	4. A fresh process reopens the real database file.
  6746	5. It runs `PRAGMA integrity_check` and relevant foreign-key checks.
  6747	6. It accepts only the complete old state or complete new state—never a mixture.
  6748	7. It repeats across crash points and compound failures.
  6749	8. For power-loss claims, a test VFS models lost, reordered, or corrupted unsynced writes.
  6750	
  6751	That procedure is documented by SQLite itself ([testing methodology](https://sqlite.org/testing.html)). Ranex’s trigger-induced `ABORT` is a useful rollback test, but no process dies, the application catches the exception, and the filesystem is never exposed to an interrupted commit.
  6752	
  6753	For Ranex, the semantic oracle must check the tuple together: state head, journal head, outbox intent/receipt, reconstructed reducer state, and stable message identities. Retrying after an ambiguous send must also demonstrate idempotency.
  6754	
  6755	### 3. Is one-SQLite-unit-of-work recognized, or unusual?
  6756	
  6757	It is a recognized local-authority form of the transactional-outbox/event-store pattern, not an unusual invention. DBOS explicitly supports colocating application changes with durable workflow records in one SQL transaction, including SQLite use ([DBOS transaction tutorial](https://docs.dbos.dev/golang/tutorials/transaction-tutorial)); AWS documents the general same-database outbox pattern ([AWS](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/transactional-outbox.html)).
  6758	
  6759	Its limits are well known:
  6760	
  6761	- SQLite serializes writers; WAL still has only one writer at a time.
  6762	- WAL is intended for processes on the same host, not a network filesystem.
  6763	- A transaction spanning multiple attached databases is not atomic as a set in WAL mode.
  6764	- Durability depends on journal mode, synchronous settings, filesystem behavior, and hardware assumptions.
  6765	- External effects cannot join the SQLite transaction. The outbox gives atomic intent plus at-least-once relay, requiring idempotent consumers.
  6766	
  6767	These limits are documented by SQLite’s [WAL](https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html), [synchronous pragma](https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_synchronous), and [atomic-commit assumptions](https://www.sqlite.org/atomiccommit.html).
  6768	
  6769	## Genuinely novel
  6770	
  6771	No row in the assigned layers merits **NOVEL**.
  6772	
  6773	The exact composition—single-host SQLite authority, AI-agent governance constraints, policy identities, deterministic replay, and a server-owned graph—may be Ranex-specific as a product package. Its constituent correctness mechanisms are established:
  6774	
  6775	- history replay and non-determinism detection;
  6776	- workflow-definition/version pinning;
  6777	- event revisions and upcasters;
  6778	- event-sourced snapshots;
  6779	- transactional outbox;
  6780	- SQLite crash certification;
  6781	- server-owned graph projections;
  6782	- snapshot-plus-cursor live-update protocols.
  6783	
  6784	The Ranex-specific governance vocabulary and policy semantics may contain original product work, but that falls outside proof that these execution, storage, recovery, or visualization mechanisms lack prior art.
  6785	
  6786	## Inferences
  6787	
  6788	- **INFERENCE:** The normal store path probably writes a mutually consistent snapshot and journal because one reducer invocation produces both. A reducer bug, serialization bug, migration, or direct database write can nevertheless commit disagreement because no persisted cross-check rejects it.
  6789	- **INFERENCE:** The active database’s journal mode is `UNVERIFIED`. The connection code does not set it; SQLite’s default is normally rollback journal, but journal mode is persistent and could have been changed externally.
  6790	- **INFERENCE:** The scoped tracer demonstrates outbox persistence, not a complete relay protocol. No durable publisher/receipt implementation was found in the requested tracer paths.
  6791	- **INFERENCE:** The graph document is a proposal, not evidence of a functioning server projection or cursor protocol.
  6792	- **INFERENCE:** SSE is operationally appropriate for a single-host, server-to-browser feed, but it is not what makes the feed correct. The version/cursor/relist contract does.
  6793

  6843	## Adopt shortlist
  6844	
  6845	1. **Temporal/Cadence history replay semantics**
  6846	
  6847	   Adopt actual-history replay, command matching, non-determinism failure, and recorded version markers—not either server wholesale. Temporal is MIT ([licence](https://github.com/temporalio/temporal/blob/main/LICENSE)); Cadence is Apache-2.0 ([licence](https://github.com/cadence-workflow/cadence/blob/master/LICENSE)). Both are compatible with Ranex’s commercial optionality when notices and Apache patent terms are observed. Temporal reports more than 1,000 paying cloud customers including NVIDIA, Snap, Alaska Airlines, Retool, and Turo ([adoption evidence](https://temporal.io/news/temporal-surpasses-1-000-active-customers-milestone-in-just-over-a-year)).
  6848	
  6849	2. **SQLite-backed transactional state/journal/outbox**
  6850	
  6851	   Keep the current one-unit-of-work direction, adding explicit head agreement and delivery receipts. SQLite is public domain ([copyright](https://www.sqlite.org/copyright.html)); DBOS’s open-source SDK is MIT ([licence](https://github.com/dbos-inc/dbos-transact-py/blob/main/LICENSE)); the AWS outbox pattern itself is architecture guidance, not licensed library code. DBOS names Walmart, Notion, Roche, Yutori, and Bristol Myers Squibb as users/customers ([customer stories](https://www.dbos.dev/customer-stories)).
  6852	
  6853	3. **SQLite’s real crash-test model**
  6854	
  6855	   Adopt the parent/child crash loop, all-old-or-all-new oracle, integrity checks, and—where the guarantee warrants it—fault-injecting VFS. SQLite’s public-domain status is fully compatible. This is much stronger evidence than an application exception test.
  6856	
  6857	4. **Argo/Airflow server-owned graph projection plus Kubernetes cursor semantics**
  6858	
  6859	   Adopt the behavioral contracts, not necessarily their implementations. Argo, Airflow, and Kubernetes are Apache-2.0 and commercially compatible with notice/patent compliance ([Argo licence](https://github.com/argoproj/argo-workflows/blob/main/LICENSE), [Airflow licence](https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/main/LICENSE), [Kubernetes licence](https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes)). Argo is CNCF graduated with reported production use by Adobe, BlackRock, Capital One, Google, Intuit, and Tesla ([CNCF evidence](https://www.cncf.io/announcements/2022/12/06/the-cloud-native-computing-foundation-announces-argo-has-graduated/)). Apache reported Airflow use across more than 200 organizations including Adobe, Airbnb, Etsy, Google, Lyft, PayPal, Reddit, and Twitter ([ASF evidence](https://news.apache.org/foundation/entry/the-apache-software-foundation-announces44)).

  6862

  6876	
  6877	## Answers to the three direct questions
  6878	
  6879	### 1. How do production systems make a snapshot and a journal provably agree?
  6880	
  6881	They use four complementary controls:
  6882	
  6883	1. **The event stream is the recovery authority.** A snapshot is a cache or materialized projection that can be discarded and rebuilt, not a second independent truth ([Microsoft Event Sourcing](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/event-sourcing), [Axon snapshots](https://docs.axoniq.io/axon-framework-reference/development/tuning/snapshotting/)).
  6884	
  6885	2. **The snapshot identifies the precise journal position it represents.** Temporal stores the latest history event ID in mutable state and considers that state valid only when the event is reflected in history ([Temporal History Service](https://github.com/temporalio/temporal/blob/main/docs/architecture/history-service.md)).
  6886	
  6887	3. **Events and synchronous projections are committed atomically.** Marten calls this an inline projection and updates it in the same ACID transaction as the events ([Marten projections](https://martendb.io/tutorials/read-model-projections)).
  6888	
  6889	4. **They verify or rebuild.** Replay from original event bytes must reproduce the snapshot, and a corrupt or incompatible snapshot falls back to full stream replay.
  6890	
  6891	Ranex currently has only item 3. One transaction proves that all three writes committed or none did; it does **not** prove that the snapshot was computed from the journal event it names. A shared implementation bug can atomically commit mutually inconsistent data.
  6892	
  6893	Hashes help detect byte disagreement, but they cannot prove that the reducer or command semantics were correct.
  6894	
  6895	### 2. What is the accepted way to test crash recovery, concretely?
  6896	
  6897	SQLite’s own method is the credible baseline:
  6898	
  6899	1. A parent process creates a known durable starting database.
  6900	2. A separate child performs the transition.
  6901	3. The parent kills the child at systematically varied write/commit points.
  6902	4. A fresh process reopens the real database file.
  6903	5. It runs `PRAGMA integrity_check` and relevant foreign-key checks.
  6904	6. It accepts only the complete old state or complete new state—never a mixture.
  6905	7. It repeats across crash points and compound failures.
  6906	8. For power-loss claims, a test VFS models lost, reordered, or corrupted unsynced writes.
  6907	
  6908	That procedure is documented by SQLite itself ([testing methodology](https://sqlite.org/testing.html)). Ranex’s trigger-induced `ABORT` is a useful rollback test, but no process dies, the application catches the exception, and the filesystem is never exposed to an interrupted commit.
  6909	
  6910	For Ranex, the semantic oracle must check the tuple together: state head, journal head, outbox intent/receipt, reconstructed reducer state, and stable message identities. Retrying after an ambiguous send must also demonstrate idempotency.
  6911	
  6912	### 3. Is one-SQLite-unit-of-work recognized, or unusual?
  6913	
  6914	It is a recognized local-authority form of the transactional-outbox/event-store pattern, not an unusual invention. DBOS explicitly supports colocating application changes with durable workflow records in one SQL transaction, including SQLite use ([DBOS transaction tutorial](https://docs.dbos.dev/golang/tutorials/transaction-tutorial)); AWS documents the general same-database outbox pattern ([AWS](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/transactional-outbox.html)).
  6915	
  6916	Its limits are well known:
  6917	
  6918	- SQLite serializes writers; WAL still has only one writer at a time.
  6919	- WAL is intended for processes on the same host, not a network filesystem.
  6920	- A transaction spanning multiple attached databases is not atomic as a set in WAL mode.
  6921	- Durability depends on journal mode, synchronous settings, filesystem behavior, and hardware assumptions.
  6922	- External effects cannot join the SQLite transaction. The outbox gives atomic intent plus at-least-once relay, requiring idempotent consumers.
  6923	
  6924	These limits are documented by SQLite’s [WAL](https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html), [synchronous pragma](https://sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_synchronous), and [atomic-commit assumptions](https://www.sqlite.org/atomiccommit.html).
  6925	
  6926	## Genuinely novel
  6927	
  6928	No row in the assigned layers merits **NOVEL**.
  6929	
  6930	The exact composition—single-host SQLite authority, AI-agent governance constraints, policy identities, deterministic replay, and a server-owned graph—may be Ranex-specific as a product package. Its constituent correctness mechanisms are established:
  6931	
  6932	- history replay and non-determinism detection;
  6933	- workflow-definition/version pinning;
  6934	- event revisions and upcasters;
  6935	- event-sourced snapshots;
  6936	- transactional outbox;
  6937	- SQLite crash certification;
  6938	- server-owned graph projections;
  6939	- snapshot-plus-cursor live-update protocols.
  6940	
  6941	The Ranex-specific governance vocabulary and policy semantics may contain original product work, but that falls outside proof that these execution, storage, recovery, or visualization mechanisms lack prior art.
  6942	
  6943	## Inferences
  6944	
  6945	- **INFERENCE:** The normal store path probably writes a mutually consistent snapshot and journal because one reducer invocation produces both. A reducer bug, serialization bug, migration, or direct database write can nevertheless commit disagreement because no persisted cross-check rejects it.
  6946	- **INFERENCE:** The active database’s journal mode is `UNVERIFIED`. The connection code does not set it; SQLite’s default is normally rollback journal, but journal mode is persistent and could have been changed externally.
  6947	- **INFERENCE:** The scoped tracer demonstrates outbox persistence, not a complete relay protocol. No durable publisher/receipt implementation was found in the requested tracer paths.
  6948	- **INFERENCE:** The graph document is a proposal, not evidence of a functioning server projection or cursor protocol.
  6949	- **INFERENCE:** SSE is operationally appropriate for a single-host, server-to-browser feed, but it is not what makes the feed correct. The version/cursor/relist contract does.
  6950	
  6951	## Search coverage
  6952	
  6953	Verified from primary documentation or source:
  6954	
  6955	- Temporal, Cadence, DBOS, Restate and Azure Durable workflow versioning/replay.
  6956	- Airflow DAG bundles, GitHub Actions commit pinning, and Kestra flow revisions.
  6957	- Axon event upcasting/snapshots, Marten inline projections, Microsoft event sourcing.
  6958	- AWS transactional outbox, NServiceBus outbox protocol, and Debezium outbox routing.
  6959	- SQLite atomic commit, WAL, synchronous modes, public-domain status, and crash-test methodology.
  6960	- Argo server-side workflow nodes and resolved specs.
  6961	- Airflow graph source and progressive server summaries.
  6962	- Kubernetes List/Watch snapshot/cursor/gap semantics.
  6963	- React Flow, Dagre, ELK, Dagu, and all recommended-project licences.
  6964	- Named adoption evidence for Temporal, DBOS, Argo, and Airflow.
  6965	
  6966	Searched but not used as dispositive evidence:
  6967	
  6968	- **Netflix/OSS Conductor:** current materials claim replay/rerun capabilities, but I did not verify a primary contract equivalent to Temporal’s “re-execute history and compare emitted commands.” The original Netflix repository is also archived. Deterministic replay equivalence is therefore **UNVERIFIED** for this report.
  6969	- **Dagster:** snapshot/reexecution material was found, but it did not add a stronger result than the verified Temporal/Airflow/Argo evidence.
  6970	- **Kestra:** flow revisions are verified ([revision documentation](https://kestra.io/docs/concepts/revision)), but no stronger command-by-command deterministic replay guarantee was verified.

commit acf155aeedd9fc4638439e4928f614ba9af73966
Author:     Anthony Garces <anthonykewl20@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Jul 30 21:03:33 2026 +0800
Commit:     Anthony Garces <anthonykewl20@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Jul 30 21:03:33 2026 +0800

    fix(kernel): enforce section 8.3 journal agreement, add real crash and replay tests
    
    Three defects found by independent cross-model audit, all reproduced at
    path:line before being fixed.
    
    1. Section 8.3 mismatch gate. HERMES_GROUND_ZERO section 8.3 declares a dual
       model -- the current row is the operational read source, the journal is the
       replay and audit oracle, snapshots never replace it, and a current-row/journal
       mismatch is corruption that BLOCKS advancement. load() previously read only
       execution_state and checked row-internal consistency, so a forger who rewrote
       canonical_state_json, version and last_event_id together passed every check.
       An auditor executed exactly that and load() returned SUCCEEDED at version 99.
       load() now reconciles against the journal head on version, event identity and
       canonical state digest, plus a row-count-versus-version gap check.
    
    2. Replay tested nothing. The old test folded reduce_execution over in-memory
       events and compared the result to replay_execution, which is that same fold:
       the production reducer compared with itself. Adds _event_from_document, the
       missing inverse of the encoder, and replay_history(), which decodes persisted
       event_json and verifies each step's digest against the journal. New tests
       detect a drifted persisted field and an event type outside the declared union
       -- failures the old test could not express.
    
    3. Crash test did not crash. The old test raised SQLite ABORT and let the
       store's own handler ROLLBACK, a path no real crash executes. Adds
       test_execution_crash_recovery.py following SQLite's documented methodology:
       a child process is SIGKILLed at three points (after the state update, after
       the journal insert, and at COMMIT), a fresh process reopens the real file,
       integrity_check and foreign_key_check must pass, and only the complete old or
       complete new state is accepted -- never a mixture. The negative return code
       asserts the process was killed rather than exiting through application code.
       The old test is retained for rollback-on-error but its docstring now states
       plainly that it is not a crash test.
    
    Defence in depth: journal deletion is rejected by the append-only trigger, and a
    test drops that trigger first to prove the mismatch gate still blocks, answering
    the audit's counter-argument that an attacker with write access removes triggers.
    
    82 tests pass, up from 72. No check was relaxed.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

+[project]
+name = "ranex-kernel-tracer"
+version = "0.0.0"
+description = "Non-authoritative R&D tracer for the proposed Ranex kernel"
+requires-python = ">=3.11,<3.15"
+dependencies = [
+    "PyYAML>=6.0.2,<7",
+]
+
+[dependency-groups]
+dev = [

+

+

+pythonpath = ["src"]
+testpaths = ["tests"]

+target-version = "py311"

+

diff --git a/src/ranex/assurance/adapters/filesystem/artifact_verifier.py b/src/ranex/assurance/adapters/filesystem/artifact_verifier.py
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..3dca82693
--- /dev/null
+++ b/src/ranex/assurance/adapters/filesystem/artifact_verifier.py
@@ -0,0 +1,128 @@
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import errno
+import hashlib
+import hmac
+import os
+import stat
+from dataclasses import replace
+from pathlib import Path
+
+from ranex.assurance.api.contracts import EvidenceRecord
+
+
+class EvidenceArtifactError(ValueError):
+    """The referenced artifact cannot serve as verified evidence."""
+
+
+def _open_flags(*, directory: bool) -> int:
+    flags = (
+        os.O_RDONLY
+        | getattr(os, "O_CLOEXEC", 0)
+        | getattr(
+            os,
+            "O_NOFOLLOW",
+            0,
+        )
+    )
+    if directory:
+        flags |= getattr(os, "O_DIRECTORY", 0)
+    else:
+        flags |= getattr(os, "O_NONBLOCK", 0)
+    return flags
+
+
+def _open_artifact_descriptor(
+    evidence_root: Path,
+    relative: Path,
+) -> tuple[int, list[int]]:
+    opened: list[int] = []
+    try:
+        directory_fd = os.open(evidence_root, _open_flags(directory=True))
+        opened.append(directory_fd)
+        for component in relative.parts[:-1]:
+            directory_fd = os.open(
+                component,
+                _open_flags(directory=True),
+                dir_fd=directory_fd,
+            )
+            opened.append(directory_fd)
+        artifact_fd = os.open(
+            relative.parts[-1],
+            _open_flags(directory=False),
+            dir_fd=directory_fd,
+        )
+        opened.append(artifact_fd)
+        return artifact_fd, opened
+    except OSError as exc:
+        for descriptor in reversed(opened):
+            os.close(descriptor)
+        if exc.errno in {errno.ELOOP, errno.ENOTDIR}:
+            raise EvidenceArtifactError(
+                "artifact path contains a symlink or non-directory component"
+            ) from exc
+        raise EvidenceArtifactError("artifact path cannot be opened safely") from exc
+
+
+def _sha256_descriptor(
+    descriptor: int,
+) -> tuple[str, os.stat_result, os.stat_result]:
+    before = os.fstat(descriptor)
+    if not stat.S_ISREG(before.st_mode):
+        raise EvidenceArtifactError("artifact must be a regular file")
+
+    digest = hashlib.sha256()
+    os.lseek(descriptor, 0, os.SEEK_SET)
+    while chunk := os.read(descriptor, 1024 * 1024):
+        digest.update(chunk)
+    after = os.fstat(descriptor)
+    return digest.hexdigest(), before, after
+
+
+def _stable_file(before: os.stat_result, after: os.stat_result) -> bool:
+    return (
+        before.st_dev,
+        before.st_ino,
+        before.st_size,
+        before.st_mtime_ns,
+        before.st_ctime_ns,
+    ) == (
+        after.st_dev,
+        after.st_ino,
+        after.st_size,
+        after.st_mtime_ns,
+        after.st_ctime_ns,
+    )
+
+
+def verify_evidence_artifact(
+    evidence: EvidenceRecord,
+    *,
+    artifact_path: str,
+    evidence_root: Path,
+) -> EvidenceRecord:
+    """Hash the same race-safe descriptor opened beneath a trusted root."""
+    relative = Path(artifact_path)
+    if (
+        relative.is_absolute()
+        or not relative.parts
+        or any(component in {"", ".", ".."} for component in relative.parts)
+    ):
+        raise EvidenceArtifactError(
+            "artifact_path must be a contained relative path without traversal"
+        )
+
+    artifact_fd, opened = _open_artifact_descriptor(evidence_root, relative)
+    try:
+        observed_digest, before, after = _sha256_descriptor(artifact_fd)
+    finally:
+        for descriptor in reversed(opened):
+            os.close(descriptor)
+
+    if not _stable_file(before, after):

    60	- **Previously omitted 1.2.0 normative changes (recorded retroactively in
    61	  1.3.0)** — `HERMES-PROMOTION-058` renamed
    62	  `SHARED_IDENTITY_AND_CANONICAL_SERIALIZATION_DEFINE_KERNEL_RECORDS` to
    63	  `CLEAN_KERNEL_PROVIDES_SHARED_IDENTITY_AND_CANONICAL_SERIALIZATION` and
    64	  removed the unsupported `kernel records` scope.
    65	  `HERMES-PROMOTION-059` replaced “whose state evolution is computed by a pure
    66	  reducer without observable side effects” with “every Execution state
    67	  transition is computed by its pure reducer.”
    68	- **DEFECT 1** — `HERMES-PROMOTION-011` again requires removal of the Nous
    69	  commercial subsystem, and `HERMES-PROMOTION-041` again requires removal of
    70	  Hermes/Nous branding. Neither action is represented as an asserted
    71	  absent-state.
    72	- **DEFECT 2** — `HERMES-PROMOTION-040` restores rebranding as the specific
    73	  context that does not authorize erasing legal notices or Git history.
    74	- **DEFECT 3** — `HERMES-PROMOTION-041` restores package metadata, remote
    75	  endpoints, headers, telemetry tags, help text, screenshots, generated
    76	  assets, and defaults as eight separately checkable surfaces. The same
    77	  row-by-row specificity check restores the cited item-level terms in
    78	  `HERMES-PROMOTION-037`, `HERMES-PROMOTION-038`,
    79	  `HERMES-PROMOTION-044`, `HERMES-PROMOTION-045`,
    80	  `HERMES-PROMOTION-050`, `HERMES-PROMOTION-051`,
    81	  `HERMES-PROMOTION-053`, `HERMES-PROMOTION-054`, and
    82	  `HERMES-PROMOTION-057`.
    83	- **DEFECT 4** — `HERMES-PROMOTION-005`, `HERMES-PROMOTION-010`,
    84	  `HERMES-PROMOTION-012`, and `HERMES-PROMOTION-018` remove the unsupported
    85	  `governed`, `accepted`, generalized domain-package, and `authority`
    86	  qualifiers and restore their exact cited scopes.
    87	- **DEFECT 5** — Phase 1 lines 1899–1912 remain represented by
    88	  `HERMES-PROMOTION-058` through `HERMES-PROMOTION-065`, rather than by an
    89	  exclusion-equivalence claim. Phase 0/0A activity and Phase 2–6 sequencing
    90	  and exit material remain research-only and are not represented by promoted
    91	  gates.
    92	
    93	### 1.1.0 — 2026-07-29
    94	
    95	By owner decision, this revision overturns the prior “implementation
    96	sequencing, not fixed authority” exclusion for Phase 1. Research lines
    97	1899–1912 define the clean kernel's binding structural inventory and behavior,
    98	not a delivery schedule.
    99	
   100	This revision adds eight promoted provisions,
   101	`HERMES-PROMOTION-058` through `HERMES-PROMOTION-065`:
   102	
   103	1. shared identity and canonical serialization;
   104	2. the `Execution` aggregate and pure reducer;
   105	3. canonical relational execution state and version;
   106	4. the append-only transition/audit journal and outbox in one SQLite unit of
   107	   work;
   108	5. the evidence-gated, `Execution`-only event-sourcing boundary;
   109	6. the fail-closed application-control PEP, pure domain decisions, and
   110	   deterministic policy adapter;
   111	7. architecture import tests before feature code; and
   112	8. the replay, crash-boundary, and no-Hermes-import exit gate.
   113	
   114	It also adds `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-020` as an
   115	`OWNER_DECISION_REQUIRED` row for whether qualified `Execution` event sourcing
   116	is activated, narrows `HERMES-RESEARCH-ONLY-008` to Phases 0/0A, and
   117	adds `HERMES-RESEARCH-ONLY-013` for Phases 2–6.
   118	Phase 1 is no longer included in a research-only sequencing disposition.
   119	
   120	### 1.0.0 — 2026-07-29
   121	
   122	Established the initial line-bound Hermes-research promotion catalog,
   123	owner-decision register, and research-only dispositions.
   124	
   125	## Decision
   126	
   127	The Hermes architecture research is advisory evidence, not authority by itself.
   128	This ADR promotes only the closed set below into an accepted documentation
   129	contract. Each promoted provision names one exact research line, one
   130	underscore-only guard, a deterministic check class, and the stage that must
   131	block when the check does not pass.
   132	
   133	The licensing, copyright, provenance, attribution, and history-preservation
   134	provision is an external-obligation boundary. No owner, model, waiver, feature
   135	flag, profile, or passing unrelated check may compensate for it.
   136	
   137	Phase 1 is a binding clean-kernel inventory. Its heading is phased migration
   138	context, but lines 1901–1911 define required parts, required behavior,
   139	construction precedence, and a falsifiable gate. They are not treated as a
   140	calendar, delivery estimate, or optional schedule.
   141	
   142	`OWNER_DECISION_REQUIRED` has one precise meaning here: the choice is registered
   143	but not selected by this ADR. Its `owner_decision_ref` and `default` remain
   144	null, absence yields `BLOCK`, and activation without the exact accepted
   145	decision is `DENIED`. The documentation-contract validator passes when that
   146	fail-closed definition is intact; it does not convert an unresolved owner
   147	choice into a runtime or release pass.
   148	
   149	Later architecture material may discuss the same subject, but topical overlap
   150	is not treated as an exact owner-decision binding. Satisfying one of these rows
   151	requires a catalog revision that names the accepted decision and its
   152	predeclared acceptance test; until then the reference remains null and the
   153	named stage blocks.
   154	
   155	The following YAML block is the one canonical source for the generated
   156	promotion registry. The compiler and validator require the exact denominators,
   157	field sets, source lines, source-excerpt digests, guard syntax, guard
   158	uniqueness, and fail-closed outcomes. Runtime evidence begins
   159	`NOT_ASSESSED`.

  1144	## Noncompensating fitness functions
  1145	
  1146	| ID | Required result |
  1147	|---|---|
  1148	| `FF-HERMES-PROMOTION-001` | The generated registry exactly projects all 65 promoted provisions, 20 owner-decision gates, and 13 research-only dispositions; every cited line and excerpt digest resolves to the immutable research source. |
  1149	| `FF-HERMES-GUARD-001` | Every promoted or owner-decision guard is unique and matches `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*$`; a missing, malformed, duplicated, or hyphenated guard fails validation. |
  1150	| `FF-HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-001` | Every genuine owner choice is `OWNER_DECISION_REQUIRED`, has no default or synthetic decision reference, and blocks activation or progression while the exact accepted owner decision is absent. |
  1151	| `FF-HERMES-LEGAL-001` | License, copyright, provenance, required-attribution, legal-notice, and Git-history preservation are non-waivable release obligations; separate de-commercialization, package, network, credential, data, and branding checks remain noncompensating owner requirements rather than being mislabeled as law. |
  1152	| `FF-HERMES-KERNEL-001` | The clean-kernel inventory contains all eight Phase 1 provisions, binds only research lines 1901–1911, retains the one-SQLite-unit-of-work and Execution-only event-sourcing boundaries, requires import-test precedence, and cannot advance its gate without replay, crash-boundary, and no-Hermes-import proof. |
  1153	
  1154	## What remains research
  1155	
  1156	The thirteen `RESEARCH_ONLY` rows retain historical facts, advisory assessments,
  1157	illustrative shapes, conditional migration guidance, and unselected product
  1158	scope. They are not silently discarded. Promotion later requires a new accepted
  1159	ADR, an exact line binding, a deterministic checker contract, and migration or
  1160	compatibility treatment where applicable.
     1	# ADR-0003: Accept the Ranex Target Architecture and Authority Kernel
     2	
     3	| Field | Value |
     4	|---|---|
     5	| ADR ID | `ADR-0003` |
     6	| Version | `1.0.0` |
     7	| Status | `ACCEPTED` |
     8	| Decision owner | Human owner |
     9	| Decision date | 2026-07-28 |
    10	| Effective revision | Working tree based on `4baad4a758f70d39af6a21e73488c61db5f82f32` |
    11	| Content binding | Exact digest is recorded externally in each immutable review/release source manifest |
    12	| Affected contexts | All Ranex bounded contexts, authority records, evidence records, repository layout, adapters, and migration work |
    13	| RFC | Not required; closes the owner-directed architecture contract |
    14	| Supersedes | None |
    15	| Review/expiry date | First target-mode tracer, then on any authority-boundary change |
    16	| Compatibility/migration class | New target architecture with strangler migration from the pinned Hermes-derived substrate |
    17	| Security/data class | Public architecture decision |
    18	
    19	## Decision
    20	
    21	The
    22	[full-system architecture](../HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md)
    23	is accepted as Ranex's normative target architecture. Acceptance means that the
    24	paper contract is the construction destination. It does **not** mean that the
    25	current branch has passed fork preflight, executable-contract validation,
    26	security qualification, recovery drills, performance objectives, or a
    27	target-mode runtime tracer.
    28	
    29	Ranex is one release-pinned modular monolith with explicit bounded-context
    30	packages. The only canonical context source path is:
    31	
    32	```text
    33	src/ranex/<context>/
    34	```
    35	
    36	`src/ranex/contexts/<context>/` is rejected. Compatibility code lives below
    37	`src/ranex/compatibility/`; adapters live below the owning context or the
    38	explicit composition boundary. A second context-root convention may not be
    39	introduced by a route plan, generator, example, or migration.
    40	
    41	The authority kernel is single-valued:
    42	
    43	| Authoritative record or decision | Sole owner | Explicit non-owners |
    44	|---|---|---|
    45	| `WorkItemStatus` and legal work-item transition | `work_management` | Runs, boards, models, agent assignments, review, assurance |
    46	| `RunStatus`, run reducer, workflow node, gate binding, authority unit of work | `governed_execution` | Worker fleet, policy, assurance, models |
    47	| `ConsumableAuthorityGrant`, `Permit`, permit consumption, effect intent/outcome/reconciliation | `governed_execution` | Policy, identity, adapters, workers |
    48	| Rules, risk derivation, authorization snapshot, `HumanDecisionRecord` policy requirements | `policy` | Models, workers, delivery channels |
    49	| Principal authentication, challenges, session/device binding, secret handles | `identity_access` | Policy, workers, delivery channels |
    50	| Claims, `EvidenceEnvelope`, qualified `CheckerResult`, `EvidenceSnapshot`, `GateEvaluation` | `assurance` | Reviewers, governed execution, process assurance |
    51	| `ReviewSpecification`, `ReviewRequest`, `AnalysisAttempt`, `ReviewObservation`, `ReviewVerdict`, `IndependenceEvaluation` | `analytical_review` | Assurance, governed execution, process assurance |
    52	| SDLC conformance audits, capability assessments, corrective actions, fleet experiments | `process_assurance` | Runtime gate evaluator, workers |
    53	
    54	`analytical_review` may publish immutable references to observations.
    55	`assurance` may ingest those references as candidate evidence, qualify them,
    56	and create an exact-subject evidence snapshot and `GateEvaluation`; it may not
    57	rewrite or co-own the underlying `ReviewObservation`. `governed_execution` may
    58	atomically bind a fresh passing `GateEvaluation` to a run and consume a valid
    59	permit; it may not author the evaluation.
    60	
    61	An authenticated human decision is an input to policy and assurance where the
    62	governing rule requires judgment. It is never represented as a machine
    63	`GateOutcome=PASS`. Policy may establish eligibility for an authority grant,
    64	but only `governed_execution` issues and consumes the grant/permit inside the
    65	authority transaction.
    66	
    67	## Architectural standing
    68	
    69	The target has three independently reported maturity states:
    70	
    71	1. **Paper contract:** accepted by this ADR.
    72	2. **Executable contract:** pending until canonical registries, schemas,
    73	   generators, and drift checks pass `AI-G2`.
    74	3. **Runtime qualification:** pending until fork preflight and the applicable
    75	   structural, behavioral, security, recovery, and operating gates pass against
    76	   an immutable exact subject.
    77	
    78	A pass in one state cannot be inferred from another. In particular, a
    79	`MAP-*` result cannot substitute for `AI-G2`, and neither can authorize an
    80	implementation commit while `SDLC-FORK-000` is pending.
    81	
    82	## Alternatives considered
    83	
    84	1. **Keep the architecture conditionally selected until runtime exists.**
    85	   Rejected because it leaves constructors without one accepted destination;
    86	   implementation maturity is tracked separately instead.
    87	2. **Use `src/ranex/contexts/<context>`.** Rejected because it conflicts with
    88	   the complete target repository map and adds a redundant physical layer.
    89	3. **Let each workflow component own its local gate or permit.** Rejected
    90	   because duplicated authority permits disagreement and bypass.
    91	4. **Let assurance own review observations.** Rejected because evidence
    92	   qualification and independent analytical judgment are distinct lifecycles.
    93	5. **Make every context a service.** Rejected because the local-first product
    94	   does not justify distributed failure, consistency, deployment, and
    95	   operations cost.
    96	6. **Use one shared domain package.** Rejected because it erases ownership and
    97	   makes authority writes difficult to constrain mechanically.
    98	
    99	## Fitness functions and acceptance evidence
   100	
   101	| ID | Required result |
   102	|---|---|
   103	| `FF-PATH-001` | The path registry, architecture tree, generated packages, import rules, and source tree contain only `src/ranex/<context>/` for context packages. |
   104	| `FF-AUTH-001` | The lifecycle/data-ownership registries assign every authority-bearing type to exactly one owner and reject duplicate or missing owners. |
   105	| `FF-AUTH-002` | Static imports plus integration tests prove that only `governed_execution` writes its authority tables and only `work_management` transitions work items. |
   106	| `FF-EVID-001` | A review observation cannot directly produce `PASS`; only an `assurance` evaluator can create a subject-bound `GateEvaluation`. |
   107	| `FF-PERMIT-001` | Stale, reused, wrong-subject, revoked, expired, or concurrently consumed grants/permits fail atomically. |
   108	| `FF-LIFE-001` | Model/property tests cover every legal and illegal work/run transition, including block/resume, terminality, retry, and cancellation. |
   109	| `FF-FORK-001` | `SDLC-FORK-000` binds ancestry, license, provenance, selected adoption strategy, and immutable baseline before runtime implementation. |
   110	| `FF-BOUNDARY-001` | Dependency-graph checks report no forbidden edge, cycle, compatibility leak, or adapter-to-domain authority mutation. |
   125	shapes above (registered PDF, Chapter 11, sequence pages 99–101); this remains
   126	a proportionate alternative record, not a claim that book citation proves the
   127	decision or its runtime outcome.
   128	
   129	## Consequences
   130	
   131	- Construction has one target path, context granularity, and record owner.
   132	- The architecture can be accepted without overstating current runtime
   133	  maturity.
   134	- Cross-context writes require commands/events or the named atomic authority
   135	  transaction, not shared-table convenience.
   136	- Contract registries and tests are mandatory before implementation readiness
   137	  can be claimed.
   138	
   139	## Migration and rollback
   140	
     1	# ADR-0006: Register the Fixed Decisions and Fitness Crosswalk
     2	
     3	| Field | Value |
     4	|---|---|
     5	| ADR ID | `ADR-0006` |
     6	| Version | `1.1.0` |
     7	| Status | `ACCEPTED` |
     8	| Decision owner | Human owner |
     9	| Decision date | 2026-07-28 |
    10	| Effective revision | Working tree based on `4baad4a758f70d39af6a21e73488c61db5f82f32` |
    11	| Content binding | Exact digest is recorded externally in each immutable review/release source manifest |
    12	| Affected contexts | Complete Ranex target and all implementation packets |
    13	| RFC | Not required; normalizes decisions already selected by the architecture/owner ADRs |
    14	| Supersedes | No decision ID; ADR-0011 supersedes the selected meaning and governing ADR for rows `DEC-RANEX-017`, `025`, `026`, and `027` |
    15	| Review/expiry date | On any fixed-decision change or architecture graph diff |
    16	| Compatibility/migration class | Machine-readable decision register |
    17	| Security/data class | Public architecture decision |
    18	
    19	## Decision
    20	
    21	The following fenced YAML block is the canonical, machine-checkable crosswalk
    22	for the 29 fixed architecture decisions. The denominator remains 29 after
    23	ADR-0011: that ADR strengthens four existing product dimensions rather than
    24	creating overlapping new ones. A contract compiler must parse this block,
    25	require exactly 29 unique `decision_id` values in the contiguous range
    26	`DEC-RANEX-001` through `DEC-RANEX-029`, resolve every named ADR and fitness
    27	function, and reject unknown fields or a non-`ACCEPTED` item.

   268	## Fitness-function resolution
   269	
   270	Fitness IDs defined in ADR-0003 through ADR-0005 and ADR-0011 resolve there.
   271	In particular, ADR-0011 owns `FF-FLEET-LEAF-001`, `FF-ROLE-TOOLS-001`,
   272	`FF-RUNTIME-001`, `FF-NO-FALLBACK-001`, `FF-SESSION-001`,
   273	`FF-DISPATCH-PERF-001`, `FF-AUTH-ROUTE-001`, and the strengthened
   274	`FF-DECOMM-001`. The remaining registry-level functions resolve as follows:
   275	
   276	| ID | Required result |
   277	|---|---|
   278	| `FF-SDLC-001` | Every execution/activity maps to one Core-SDLC work item/state and no subordinate lifecycle can transition it. |
   279	| `FF-REF-001` | Each reference/profile has an exact registered source, applicability, limitation, required behavior, deviation, and verification mapping. |
   280	| `FF-PACKET-001` | A sealed packet is deterministic from pinned inputs and invalidates when a dependency changes. |
   281	| `FF-GUIDE-001` | No active normative link, packet, generator, or route treats the retired guide as construction authority. |
   282	| `FF-MIG-001` | Characterization, shadow/dual-read comparison, cutover, rollback, and no-dual-authority tests pass. |
   283	| `FF-EFFECT-001` | Each effect declares attempt semantics, idempotency key, durable intent, outcome query, and reconciliation path. |
   284	| `FF-SUPPLY-001` | Release manifest pins dependencies/providers and passes provenance, license, vulnerability, support, and exit checks. |
   285	| `FF-EXCL-001` | Source, routes, packages, runtime, network, and SBOM contain no excluded surface. |
   286	| `FF-DELIVERY-001` | Every channel uses the same command, challenge, decision, permit, authentication, replay, and audit contracts. |
   287	| `FF-PRIV-001` | Consent, minimization, classification, retention, deletion, and egress tests pass for personal/media data. |
   288	| `FF-LANDING-001` | Only the authenticated human landing path can integrate the exact reviewed/verified head, with rollback prepared. |
   289	
   290	The compiler rejects a fixed decision if its referenced fitness evidence is
   291	missing, stale, for another subject, or reports a blocking non-pass outcome.
   292	The decision remains accepted while implementation readiness remains pending.
   293	
   294	## Alternatives considered
   295	
   296	1. **Keep the prose table only.** Rejected because row naming and completeness
   297	   cannot be checked reliably across packets and generators.
   298	2. **Create one ADR per row immediately.** Rejected as needless ceremony for
   299	   already selected, related constraints; a row can receive a dedicated
   300	   superseding ADR when evidence calls it into question.
   301	3. **Treat the register as runtime proof.** Rejected because a decision and a
   302	   passing implementation are different artifacts.
   303	
   304	## Change and supersession
   305	
   306	A change to one row requires a new accepted ADR naming the stable decision ID,
   307	alternatives, evidence, migration, compatibility, security/operations impact,
   308	fitness-function changes, and rollback. The register then points that stable ID
   309	to the superseding ADR, as rows `017`, `025`, `026`, and `027` now point to
   310	ADR-0011. IDs are never recycled or silently renumbered. A superseding ADR that
   311	refines an existing dimension does not increase `required_count`; a genuinely
   312	new, nonoverlapping fixed dimension requires an explicit denominator decision
   313	and compatible migration of every exact-subject consumer.
   314	
   315	## Engineering-reference application
   316	
   317	The crosswalk operationalizes the advisory
   318	[Engineering Reference Application Map](../ENGINEERING_REFERENCE_APPLICATION_MAP.md)
   319	§3, §5.1–§5.4, §6, §8, and §10. It records alternatives, ownership, decisions,
   320	and falsifiable tests without treating any book as authority or copying its

A focused read-only run passed all 7 replay/crash/migration tests. The remaining event-sourcing question turns on a different gate: the sole migration test covers only repeatable creation of schema version 1, not historical event-version evolution or upcasters, and the crash harness still does not simulate power loss or an interrupted low-level commit.
