codex
## 1. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-003` — transaction ownership

### Recommendation

Accept this boundary:

**One Governed Execution SQLite transaction must atomically write:**

- the Execution aggregate’s current state and version;
- its ordered transition/audit journal entry;
- immutable bindings to the exact gate, evidence, policy, module, and route snapshots used;
- issuance or consumption of the applicable grant/permit;
- zero or more effect intents;
- the Governed Execution integration-outbox records generated by that transition.

**Separate transactions must write:**

- evidence, EvidenceSnapshots, and GateEvaluations authored by Assurance;
- canonical WorkItemStatus and Kanban projections owned by Work Management;
- policy-owned HumanDecisions;
- actual external effects, which occur only after commit through the relay.

This is exactly the transaction enumerated by the accepted architecture at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:441](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:441). Assurance authors the gate evaluation before Governed Execution binds it, while Work Management consumes events in its own transaction at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:421](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:421).

### Why, from evidence

The decision row asks for this exact boundary and otherwise blocks implementation at [ADR-0013:847](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:847).

The accepted ownership map already assigns execution state, gate binding, permits, and effect intents to Governed Execution, while Assurance alone authors evidence and gate evaluations and Work Management alone changes work state at [ADR-0003:41](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:41). ADR-0007 expressly prohibits cross-context table writes and distributed transactions, even where contexts share one physical SQLite database, at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155).

This matches established practice:

- Microsoft’s tactical DDD guidance, updated 2026-02-26, treats the aggregate as a transactional consistency boundary and uses events/eventual consistency across aggregates: [Tactical DDD design](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/microservices/model/tactical-domain-driven-design).
- The transactional-outbox pattern writes business state and the outbound message in the same database transaction, then dispatches later with idempotent handling: [AWS Transactional Outbox, accessed 2026-07-30](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/transactional-outbox.html).
- Marten v9.x distinguishes inline projections—updated in the event append transaction—from asynchronous projections that trade immediate consistency for throughput: [Marten read-model projections, v9.x](https://martendb.io/tutorials/read-model-projections).

See **Inference I-003**.

### What it costs

- Work status and Kanban can temporarily lag completed execution.
- Consumers need retry, deduplication, gap detection, and reconciliation.
- The Governed Execution transaction becomes wider and can increase SQLite write contention.
- SQLite state and an external effect cannot be exactly one atomic transaction. The best available contract is durable intent plus idempotent, at-least-once dispatch.
- A user cannot be promised that execution and board state become visible simultaneously.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

- **AT-003-01 — atomic authority tuple:** inject failure and real process termination after every Governed Execution write boundary. A fresh connection must observe either the complete old tuple or complete new tuple across state/version, journal, bindings, permit, effect intents, and outbox—never a mixture.
- **AT-003-02 — ownership enforcement:** tests and architecture checks must reject Governed Execution writes to Assurance or Work Management tables and reject those contexts writing Governed Execution authority tables.
- **AT-003-03 — projection delivery:** after commit, deliver duplicate, delayed, and out-of-order completion events. Work Management must converge idempotently without changing the underlying execution record.
- **AT-003-04 — rollback/relay isolation:** a rolled-back effect intent must never be dispatchable. A committed intent may be retried, but duplicate delivery must not duplicate the business effect.
- **AT-003-05 — concurrent permit use:** two transitions attempting to consume the same permit must yield exactly one committed transition and one no-effect rejection.

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** the null/default-BLOCK row is procedurally unresolved, but its substantive choice is already fixed.

- ADR-0003 fixes the ownership and atomic authority transaction at [ADR-0003:41](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:41).
- ADR-0006 fixes one physical SQLite database with logical ownership, journal, and outbox at [ADR-0006:106](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:106).
- ADR-0007 prohibits using the shared database to create a cross-context transaction at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155).
- ADR-0012 says implementation-start and production readiness are separate, non-compensating gates; neither can waive this unresolved catalog binding at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-058` through `-065` respectively constrain identity/serialization, the aggregate/reducer, the relational state row, atomic journal/outbox, conditional event sourcing, fail-closed enforcement, dependency boundaries, and replay/crash tests: [058:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [059:752](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:752), [060:762](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:762), [061:772](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:772), [062:782](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:782), [063:792](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:792), [064:802](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:802), [065:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3 requires agreement between the relational current row and journal; it does not authorize a transaction spanning Assurance or Work Management at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

ADR-0013 requires an accepted decision and predeclared tests even when later text overlaps an existing decision at [ADR-0013:125](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:125). The required remedy is therefore an explicit binding to the existing decisions, not a new transaction design.

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you accept the existing Governed Execution-only atomic boundary, or do you intend to supersede ADR-0003, ADR-0006, and ADR-0007 to require cross-context atomic writes?**

---

## 2. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-013` — Kanban authority

### Recommendation

**Kanban must be a disposable, rebuildable projection of canonical WorkItemStatus owned by Work Management.**

Selected Kanban tables must not be adapted into a second authority. A drag or board action is a command request to Work Management; it succeeds only if the canonical legal transition succeeds.

### Why, from evidence

The row poses projection versus adapted-table authority at [ADR-0013:967](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:967). The accepted fixed decision assigns WorkItemStatus exclusively to Work Management and expressly rejects board authority at [ADR-0006:98](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:98). The full architecture says Kanban and dashboard state are never completion authorities at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:244](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:244), and separately names Work Management as owner of canonical status and Kanban projections at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:507](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:507).

Established projection practice agrees:

- Microsoft’s CQRS pattern, updated 2025-02-21, treats read models as purpose-built projections synchronized from the write model, potentially with eventual consistency: [CQRS pattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/cqrs).
- Marten v9.x calls events the source of truth and read models derived projections: [Marten read-model projections](https://martendb.io/tutorials/read-model-projections).
- EventStoreDB/KurrentDB 24.10 makes projection outputs projection-owned and checkpointed so they can be replayed predictably: [EventStoreDB projections, v24.10](https://docs.kurrent.io/server/v24.10/features/projections/).
- Argo Workflows 3.7 exposes controller-owned `WorkflowStatus`/`NodeStatus` to clients rather than making the visual graph authoritative: [Argo Workflows 3.7 fields](https://argo-workflows.readthedocs.io/en/release-3.7/fields/).
- Airflow 3.1.0’s graph and grid views display DAG/task-run state maintained by the scheduler: [Airflow 3.1.0 documentation](https://airflow.apache.org/docs/apache-airflow/3.1.0/).

See **Inference I-013**.

### What it costs

- The board may briefly lag canonical state.
- Projection infrastructure needs checkpoints, idempotency, gap detection, rebuild, and reconciliation.
- A drag operation can be rejected after the user initiates it.
- Legacy board-table integrations cannot continue treating those tables as authoritative.
- Availability of the canonical command API becomes more important than direct table editing.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

- **AT-013-01 — deterministic rebuild:** delete the Kanban projection, replay canonical Work Management events, and obtain the same columns, cards, order, and status mapping.
- **AT-013-02 — no reverse authority:** directly mutate a projection row. Canonical WorkItemStatus and legal-transition history must remain unchanged; reconciliation must replace the mutation.
- **AT-013-03 — command path:** every board move must invoke the Work Management command API. An illegal or stale transition must reject without changing canonical or projected state.
- **AT-013-04 — delivery disorder:** duplicates, delay, and out-of-order events must either converge automatically or produce an explicit gap requiring rebuild—never silently invent status.
- **AT-013-05 — split-source detection:** if a legacy/adapted table disagrees with canonical WorkItemStatus, the system must report the discrepancy and rebuild the projection from canonical state.

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** choosing adapted Kanban authority would directly contradict an accepted fixed decision.

- ADR-0003 reserves legal WorkItemStatus transitions to Work Management at [ADR-0003:41](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:41).
- ADR-0006’s `DEC-RANEX-009` already selects Work Management authority over board authority at [ADR-0006:98](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:98).
- ADR-0007 prohibits shared mutable models and cross-context table access at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155).
- ADR-0012 cannot treat a working board as compensation for an unresolved implementation-start obligation at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-058` through `-065` constrain how canonical events are identified, stored, enforced, and tested; none grants authority to a read projection: [ADR-0013:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [ADR-0013:772](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:772), [ADR-0013:782](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:782), [ADR-0013:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3 explicitly says other projections are rebuilt or reconciled from canonical events at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

The correct administrative action is to bind `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-013` to `DEC-RANEX-009` and the tests above.

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you accept Kanban as a rebuildable Work Management projection, or do you intend to supersede `DEC-RANEX-009` and make a board table authoritative?**

---

## 3. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-017` — Electron/desktop applications

### Recommendation

**Remove and keep excluded all Electron and desktop bootstrap, packaging, updater, and desktop-only application surfaces. Retain only the selected local web and TUI paths.**

`DEC-RANEX-020` already settles the substantive decision. The remaining action is to bind this owner-decision row to ADR-0006 and `FF-EXCL`.

### Why, from evidence

The pending row appears at [ADR-0013:1015](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:1015). ADR-0006 explicitly records “desktop excluded,” names Electron/updater as rejected alternatives, and assigns `FF-EXCL` at [ADR-0006:186](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:186). The architecture repeats that there is no Electron, desktop bootstrap, or updater surface at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:220](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:220) and [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:3608](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:3608).

Electron’s own documentation confirms that retaining it creates a distinct lifecycle:

- Electron ships Chromium and Node.js, grants powerful filesystem/shell capabilities, and requires timely framework updates: [Electron security guidance, accessed 2026-07-30](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/security).
- Electron supports only its latest three stable major releases and follows an approximately eight-week release cadence: [Electron release timelines, accessed 2026-07-30](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/electron-timelines).
- Desktop updates require platform packaging and update metadata: [Electron update documentation, accessed 2026-07-30](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/updates).

See **Inference I-017**.

### What it costs

- No native tray, window-management, installer, auto-updater, or OS-specific desktop integration.
- No Electron-packaged offline desktop product.
- Users must use the loopback web interface, CLI, or TUI.
- Any existing desktop-only workflows must be retired or expressed through the retained interfaces.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

- **AT-017-01 — source exclusion:** repository scans must find no Electron bootstrap, preload script, renderer main process, desktop updater, or desktop-only route.
- **AT-017-02 — dependency exclusion:** lockfiles, build manifests, packages, SBOM, and runtime dependency graphs must contain no Electron or desktop-updater dependency.
- **AT-017-03 — build exclusion:** no build profile or feature flag may produce a desktop bundle; attempted activation must fail.
- **AT-017-04 — network/runtime exclusion:** runtime tests must show no desktop updater endpoint, download, or desktop-only listener.
- **AT-017-05 — retained-path parity:** local web and TUI operations must call the same public context APIs and authority kernel used by other retained surfaces.

These instantiate the existing `FF-EXCL` requirements recorded at [ADR-0006:276](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:276).

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-017` is substantively settled. Its null/default-BLOCK state conflicts with `DEC-RANEX-020` only at the catalog/procedure level.

- ADR-0003 accepts the full architecture as the normative destination at [ADR-0003:21](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:21).
- ADR-0006 fixes desktop exclusion and says changing a fixed decision requires a superseding accepted ADR at [ADR-0006:304](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:304).
- ADR-0007 keeps delivery applications free of authority and business logic at [ADR-0007:33](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:33); it does not preserve an excluded desktop application.
- ADR-0012 cannot waive the unresolved catalog binding merely because web/TUI paths work at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-058` through `-065` are kernel identity, persistence, enforcement, dependency, and test obligations; none selects a delivery surface: [ADR-0013:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [ADR-0013:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3 governs journal/current-state agreement and has no basis for retaining Electron at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you intend to supersede `DEC-RANEX-020` and accept permanent ownership of a separate desktop security, packaging, and updater lifecycle?**

---

## 4. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-019` — Governed Execution consistency context

### Recommendation

**Keep execution, gate binding, grant/permit handling, and effect intent/reconciliation as separate submodules of one strong-consistency Governed Execution bounded context, sharing one persistence authority and transaction coordinator.**

They may have separate domain files and types, but not independently persistent authority contexts. Assurance and Policy remain external contexts; Governed Execution binds immutable results from them rather than absorbing their authoring responsibilities.

### Why, from evidence

The owner row asks exactly this at [ADR-0013:1039](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:1039). The architecture already describes Governed Execution as one bounded consistency cell with four subdomains and one invariant/SQLite transaction—not four independent authorities—at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:409](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:409). ADR-0003 similarly assigns run state, gate binding, permit, and effect intent to the same authority at [ADR-0003:41](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:41).

Prior art supports drawing the strong boundary around invariants that must change together:

- Microsoft’s tactical DDD guidance, updated 2026-02-26, says aggregates protect invariants and form transactional consistency boundaries: [Tactical DDD design](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/microservices/model/tactical-domain-driven-design).
- Fowler’s dated bounded-context guidance, published 2014-01-15, confines a model’s meaning and relationships within an explicit boundary: [Bounded Context](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/BoundedContext.html).
- Cross-boundary eventual consistency is appropriate where independent aggregates do not need one invariant-preserving commit; that is why Assurance and Work Management remain separate, not why permit and effect intent should be split.

See **Inference I-019**.

### What it costs

- The four submodules cannot be independently deployed, stored, or scaled.
- Changes to their shared invariant require coordination.
- SQLite’s single-writer behavior can become a throughput constraint.
- The Governed Execution context is larger and requires strict internal dependency discipline.
- Independent persistence technologies for permit or effect intent are unavailable without superseding the consistency guarantee.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

- **AT-019-01 — one transaction coordinator:** all state-changing paths for execution, gate binding, permit, and effect intent must enter the same Governed Execution unit of work.
- **AT-019-02 — fault matrix:** terminate or fail after each write boundary. State, gate binding, permit consumption, journal, and effect intent must be all old or all new.
- **AT-019-03 — permit CAS:** concurrent consumption attempts must produce exactly one successful transition.
- **AT-019-04 — fail-closed invariant:** no effect intent may commit unless the same transaction proves a fresh compatible gate and consumes the required permit.
- **AT-019-05 — persistence ownership:** static and runtime tests must find exactly one owner for these tables and reject independent repositories that bypass the Governed Execution unit of work.
- **AT-019-06 — external ownership:** Governed Execution must be unable to author Assurance evidence/GateEvaluations or Policy HumanDecisions.

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** independently persistent subcontexts would conflict directly with accepted architecture.

- ADR-0003 identifies one authority transaction and rejects component-local gate/permit enforcement at [ADR-0003:89](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:89).
- ADR-0006’s `DEC-RANEX-010` assigns the four responsibilities to Governed Execution at [ADR-0006:106](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:106).
- ADR-0007 calls Governed Execution the specifically documented authority cell and prohibits silent cross-context transaction coordination at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155) and [ADR-0007:183](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:183).
- ADR-0012 leaves implementation-start blocked until the exact owner choice is accepted; production evidence cannot compensate at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-059` through `-063` align the aggregate, current row, atomic journal/outbox, optional event sourcing, and fail-closed enforcement inside this cell; `-058`, `-064`, and `-065` constrain its identities, dependencies, and tests: [ADR-0013:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [ADR-0013:752](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:752), [ADR-0013:772](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:772), [ADR-0013:792](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:792), [ADR-0013:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3’s current-row/journal agreement depends on those writes being produced by the same authority unit of work at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you accept the existing one-cell Governed Execution boundary, or do you intend to supersede the accepted ADRs and accept distributed consistency between execution, gate, permit, and effect intent?**

---

## 5. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-020` — Execution event sourcing

### Recommendation

**Do not activate event sourcing for the Execution aggregate now.**

Retain the accepted hybrid:

- relational current state/version as the operational read source;
- append-only ordered journal as the replay/audit oracle;
- atomic journal, current-state, and outbox writes;
- mandatory snapshot/journal agreement checks.

The journal remains required, but it is not yet the sole authoritative write model.

### Why, from evidence

The row makes activation conditional and default-BLOCK at [ADR-0013:1051](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:1051). Promotion `-062` permits event sourcing only inside Execution and only if replay and migration tests justify it at [ADR-0013:782](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:782). ADR-0006 fixes a selective replay journal while explicitly rejecting whole-product event sourcing at [ADR-0006:122](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:122). Section 8.3 still names the current row as the operational read source and the journal as the replay/audit oracle at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

The rewritten tests are materially stronger:

- They replay persisted journal bytes and compare the result with the current snapshot at [test_journal_replay.py:1](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/replay/governed_execution/test_journal_replay.py:1) and [test_journal_replay.py:104](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/replay/governed_execution/test_journal_replay.py:104).
- They reject corrupted current events and unknown event types at [test_journal_replay.py:117](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/replay/governed_execution/test_journal_replay.py:117) and [test_journal_replay.py:142](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/replay/governed_execution/test_journal_replay.py:142).
- The resilience test starts a child process, sends `SIGKILL`, opens a fresh connection, runs integrity checks, and verifies old-or-new state plus replay agreement at [test_execution_crash_recovery.py:127](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/resilience/test_execution_crash_recovery.py:127).

A focused read-only run of the journal replay, real-process crash, and migration files passed **7/7**.

That is enough evidence for the accepted hybrid’s replay/crash controls, but not yet enough to make the event stream the sole source of truth:

- The migration test proves only idempotent creation of schema version 1 and its three tables at [test_execution_store_migration.py:11](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/migration/governed_execution/test_execution_store_migration.py:11).
- The decoder accepts exactly schema version 1 and the current event types at [execution_store.py:147](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/src/ranex_kernel/governed_execution/execution_store.py:147). No released-history upcaster chain is exercised.
- The crash wrapper executes the wrapped SQL statement before sending `SIGKILL` at [test_execution_crash_recovery.py:86](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/resilience/test_execution_crash_recovery.py:86).

Established event-sourcing systems demand more:

- Temporal Python SDK 1.30.0, released 2026-07-02, recommends replaying exported historical workflow histories and treating nondeterminism as failure: [Temporal SDK 1.30.0 workflow replay](https://github.com/temporalio/sdk-python/tree/1.30.0#workflow-replay).
- Axon Framework 4.13 requires event revisions and ordered upcaster chains because stored events must remain readable after model evolution: [Axon 4.13 event versioning](https://docs.axoniq.io/axon-framework-reference/4.13/events/event-versioning/).
- Microsoft’s Event Sourcing pattern, updated 2026-03-28, identifies schema evolution, replay compatibility, snapshots, migration, and operational complexity as explicit costs and recommends applying event sourcing selectively: [Event Sourcing pattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/event-sourcing).
- SQLite’s published testing methodology uses separate processes and VFS-level fault simulation to test interrupted writes and old-or-new durability; the cited page describes the 3.42.0 test posture dated 2023-05-16: [How SQLite Is Tested](https://sqlite.org/testing.html).

The supplied prior-art report identifies the same four journal/snapshot production controls—recovery authority, exact journal position, atomic updates, and verify/rebuild—at [priorart-B.md:6879](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/reviews/artifacts/2026-07-30/agent-reports/priorart-B.md:6879). The adversarial audit records those controls and warns that atomicity alone does not establish semantic integrity at [kernel-tracer-adversarial-audit.md:161](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/reviews/2026-07-30-kernel-tracer-adversarial-audit.md:161).

See **Inference I-020** and **Inference I-020-CRASH**.

### What it costs

- The relational state schema still requires migrations.
- The product must maintain and continuously verify two agreeing representations: current state and journal.
- It cannot claim that deleting every current-state row and replaying events is the complete production recovery contract.
- It loses some benefits of an event-store-only model, including arbitrary historical read-model construction and a single append-only persistence abstraction.
- A later activation will require a separate compatibility qualification rather than being obtained automatically from today’s journal.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

For the recommended **not-activated** decision:

- **AT-020-01 — explicit mode:** the accepted register must say `EVENT_SOURCING_NOT_ACTIVATED`; startup and authority transitions must require the relational current row.
- **AT-020-02 — dual agreement:** every load must verify current version, journal head, event count, and digest. Mismatch, truncation, unknown type, or digest corruption must fail closed.
- **AT-020-03 — atomic hybrid:** injected exceptions and real process termination across state, journal, and outbox writes must yield only the complete old or complete new state.
- **AT-020-04 — scope guard:** static tests must reject event-sourced repositories or event-store authority in any other context and reject treating the Execution journal as sole authority while activation is false.
- **AT-020-05 — rebuild control:** discarding a non-authoritative snapshot/projection and replaying persisted journal bytes must reproduce the expected state, while disagreement with the operational current row must be reported as corruption—not silently preferred.

Any future proposal to activate event sourcing must additionally pass, before acceptance:

- **AT-020-F01 — released-history corpus:** raw journal fixtures from every released event revision replay under the proposed code.
- **AT-020-F02 — upcaster chain:** every old revision has an ordered, deterministic migration/upcaster path, including explicit rejection of unsupported histories.
- **AT-020-F03 — behavioral equivalence:** replay proves state plus deterministic commands/effect intents/outbox consequences, not merely a digest generated by the same implementation.
- **AT-020-F04 — snapshot independence:** snapshots can be discarded and rebuilt from journal bytes, with exact event-position metadata.
- **AT-020-F05 — upgrade/rollback:** old-code/new-code, new-code/old-data, interrupted migration, and rollback matrices preserve readable history.
- **AT-020-F06 — durability faults:** VFS-level or equivalent tests cover interrupted commit, I/O errors, truncation, torn writes, and power-loss behavior.
- **AT-020-F07 — test sensitivity:** mutations to event field mapping, ordering, revision, digest, reducer behavior, or emitted intents must cause the qualification suite to fail.

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** “do not activate now” has no substantive conflict with the accepted architecture. Activating now would conflict with the conditional qualification requirement because migration/evolution evidence is incomplete.

- ADR-0003 permits the target architecture but does not declare the event stream the sole source of truth at [ADR-0003:21](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:21).
- ADR-0006 fixes a selective Governed Execution replay journal and says the product is not wholly event sourced at [ADR-0006:122](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:122).
- ADR-0007 requires Governed Execution to own its aggregate, tables, migrations, contracts, events, and outbox; either persistence style must preserve that ownership at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155).
- ADR-0012 means passing the current replay/crash tests does not by itself declare implementation-start or production readiness at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-058`, `-059`, `-060`, `-061`, `-063`, `-064`, and `-065` remain mandatory regardless of activation; `-062` is the conditional activation gate: [ADR-0013:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [ADR-0013:752](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:752), [ADR-0013:762](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:762), [ADR-0013:772](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:772), [ADR-0013:782](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:782), [ADR-0013:792](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:792), [ADR-0013:802](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:802), [ADR-0013:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3’s dual current-row/journal contract is satisfied by non-activation and would need explicit supersession if an event store became sole authority at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you accept “event sourcing not activated” for implementation start, or do you want to fund and wait for the full historical-evolution and durability qualification above before deciding?**

## Inferences

- **INFERENCE I-003:** The smallest boundary satisfying all accepted invariants is the Governed Execution authority tuple only. Adding Assurance or Work Management writes would enlarge the transaction beyond the accepted consistency boundary without evidence that their canonical state must commit atomically.
- **INFERENCE I-013:** Because canonical WorkItemStatus already has one owner and §8.3 requires derived projections to be rebuildable, adapting selected Kanban tables as authority would create an unnecessary split source of truth.
- **INFERENCE I-017:** `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-017` is not an open product-design decision. It is an unresolved catalog binding to an already accepted exclusion.
- **INFERENCE I-019:** Execution, gate binding, permit consumption, and effect intent belong in one consistency cell because failure to change any one atomically can authorize an effect that the other records do not justify.
- **INFERENCE I-020:** The current tests justify the hybrid journal/current-state contract, but do not yet justify making the journal the sole authoritative write model.
- **INFERENCE I-020-CRASH:** The child-process test proves abrupt process death at selected SQL-call boundaries. Because the wrapper calls the wrapped SQL statement before killing the process, its `COMMIT` case does not prove interruption inside SQLite’s low-level commit or power-loss path.
- **INFERENCE I-MINIMAL:** None of the five recommendations requires a new bounded context, artifact type, or dependency. Four require binding existing accepted decisions; the fifth records non-activation under an already declared conditional gate.

## Unverified

- **UNVERIFIED:** interrupted low-level SQLite commit, torn-write, filesystem I/O-error, and power-loss behavior. The current crash harness does not provide VFS-level fault injection.
- **UNVERIFIED:** replay of histories written by a previously released event schema. The examined migration test covers schema version 1 creation, not event-revision migration or upcasters.
- **UNVERIFIED:** deterministic replay equivalence for emitted commands, effect intents, or outbox records. The current journal test proves reconstructed state/digest agreement.
- **UNVERIFIED:** the complete `feature/kernel-tracer` test suite. I ran only the three focused replay, crash-recovery, and migration files; they passed 7/7.
- **UNVERIFIED:** implementation-start or production-readiness status. The focused test result cannot establish either ADR-0012 tier.

## 1. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-003` — transaction ownership

### Recommendation

Accept this boundary:

**One Governed Execution SQLite transaction must atomically write:**

- the Execution aggregate’s current state and version;
- its ordered transition/audit journal entry;
- immutable bindings to the exact gate, evidence, policy, module, and route snapshots used;
- issuance or consumption of the applicable grant/permit;
- zero or more effect intents;
- the Governed Execution integration-outbox records generated by that transition.

**Separate transactions must write:**

- evidence, EvidenceSnapshots, and GateEvaluations authored by Assurance;
- canonical WorkItemStatus and Kanban projections owned by Work Management;
- policy-owned HumanDecisions;
- actual external effects, which occur only after commit through the relay.

This is exactly the transaction enumerated by the accepted architecture at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:441](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:441). Assurance authors the gate evaluation before Governed Execution binds it, while Work Management consumes events in its own transaction at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:421](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:421).

### Why, from evidence

The decision row asks for this exact boundary and otherwise blocks implementation at [ADR-0013:847](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:847).

The accepted ownership map already assigns execution state, gate binding, permits, and effect intents to Governed Execution, while Assurance alone authors evidence and gate evaluations and Work Management alone changes work state at [ADR-0003:41](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:41). ADR-0007 expressly prohibits cross-context table writes and distributed transactions, even where contexts share one physical SQLite database, at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155).

This matches established practice:

- Microsoft’s tactical DDD guidance, updated 2026-02-26, treats the aggregate as a transactional consistency boundary and uses events/eventual consistency across aggregates: [Tactical DDD design](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/microservices/model/tactical-domain-driven-design).
- The transactional-outbox pattern writes business state and the outbound message in the same database transaction, then dispatches later with idempotent handling: [AWS Transactional Outbox, accessed 2026-07-30](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/transactional-outbox.html).
- Marten v9.x distinguishes inline projections—updated in the event append transaction—from asynchronous projections that trade immediate consistency for throughput: [Marten read-model projections, v9.x](https://martendb.io/tutorials/read-model-projections).

See **Inference I-003**.

### What it costs

- Work status and Kanban can temporarily lag completed execution.
- Consumers need retry, deduplication, gap detection, and reconciliation.
- The Governed Execution transaction becomes wider and can increase SQLite write contention.
- SQLite state and an external effect cannot be exactly one atomic transaction. The best available contract is durable intent plus idempotent, at-least-once dispatch.
- A user cannot be promised that execution and board state become visible simultaneously.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

- **AT-003-01 — atomic authority tuple:** inject failure and real process termination after every Governed Execution write boundary. A fresh connection must observe either the complete old tuple or complete new tuple across state/version, journal, bindings, permit, effect intents, and outbox—never a mixture.
- **AT-003-02 — ownership enforcement:** tests and architecture checks must reject Governed Execution writes to Assurance or Work Management tables and reject those contexts writing Governed Execution authority tables.
- **AT-003-03 — projection delivery:** after commit, deliver duplicate, delayed, and out-of-order completion events. Work Management must converge idempotently without changing the underlying execution record.
- **AT-003-04 — rollback/relay isolation:** a rolled-back effect intent must never be dispatchable. A committed intent may be retried, but duplicate delivery must not duplicate the business effect.
- **AT-003-05 — concurrent permit use:** two transitions attempting to consume the same permit must yield exactly one committed transition and one no-effect rejection.

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** the null/default-BLOCK row is procedurally unresolved, but its substantive choice is already fixed.

- ADR-0003 fixes the ownership and atomic authority transaction at [ADR-0003:41](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:41).
- ADR-0006 fixes one physical SQLite database with logical ownership, journal, and outbox at [ADR-0006:106](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:106).
- ADR-0007 prohibits using the shared database to create a cross-context transaction at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155).
- ADR-0012 says implementation-start and production readiness are separate, non-compensating gates; neither can waive this unresolved catalog binding at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-058` through `-065` respectively constrain identity/serialization, the aggregate/reducer, the relational state row, atomic journal/outbox, conditional event sourcing, fail-closed enforcement, dependency boundaries, and replay/crash tests: [058:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [059:752](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:752), [060:762](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:762), [061:772](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:772), [062:782](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:782), [063:792](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:792), [064:802](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:802), [065:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3 requires agreement between the relational current row and journal; it does not authorize a transaction spanning Assurance or Work Management at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

ADR-0013 requires an accepted decision and predeclared tests even when later text overlaps an existing decision at [ADR-0013:125](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:125). The required remedy is therefore an explicit binding to the existing decisions, not a new transaction design.

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you accept the existing Governed Execution-only atomic boundary, or do you intend to supersede ADR-0003, ADR-0006, and ADR-0007 to require cross-context atomic writes?**

---

## 2. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-013` — Kanban authority

### Recommendation

**Kanban must be a disposable, rebuildable projection of canonical WorkItemStatus owned by Work Management.**

Selected Kanban tables must not be adapted into a second authority. A drag or board action is a command request to Work Management; it succeeds only if the canonical legal transition succeeds.

### Why, from evidence

The row poses projection versus adapted-table authority at [ADR-0013:967](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:967). The accepted fixed decision assigns WorkItemStatus exclusively to Work Management and expressly rejects board authority at [ADR-0006:98](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:98). The full architecture says Kanban and dashboard state are never completion authorities at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:244](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:244), and separately names Work Management as owner of canonical status and Kanban projections at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:507](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:507).

Established projection practice agrees:

- Microsoft’s CQRS pattern, updated 2025-02-21, treats read models as purpose-built projections synchronized from the write model, potentially with eventual consistency: [CQRS pattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/cqrs).
- Marten v9.x calls events the source of truth and read models derived projections: [Marten read-model projections](https://martendb.io/tutorials/read-model-projections).
- EventStoreDB/KurrentDB 24.10 makes projection outputs projection-owned and checkpointed so they can be replayed predictably: [EventStoreDB projections, v24.10](https://docs.kurrent.io/server/v24.10/features/projections/).
- Argo Workflows 3.7 exposes controller-owned `WorkflowStatus`/`NodeStatus` to clients rather than making the visual graph authoritative: [Argo Workflows 3.7 fields](https://argo-workflows.readthedocs.io/en/release-3.7/fields/).
- Airflow 3.1.0’s graph and grid views display DAG/task-run state maintained by the scheduler: [Airflow 3.1.0 documentation](https://airflow.apache.org/docs/apache-airflow/3.1.0/).

See **Inference I-013**.

### What it costs

- The board may briefly lag canonical state.
- Projection infrastructure needs checkpoints, idempotency, gap detection, rebuild, and reconciliation.
- A drag operation can be rejected after the user initiates it.
- Legacy board-table integrations cannot continue treating those tables as authoritative.
- Availability of the canonical command API becomes more important than direct table editing.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

- **AT-013-01 — deterministic rebuild:** delete the Kanban projection, replay canonical Work Management events, and obtain the same columns, cards, order, and status mapping.
- **AT-013-02 — no reverse authority:** directly mutate a projection row. Canonical WorkItemStatus and legal-transition history must remain unchanged; reconciliation must replace the mutation.
- **AT-013-03 — command path:** every board move must invoke the Work Management command API. An illegal or stale transition must reject without changing canonical or projected state.
- **AT-013-04 — delivery disorder:** duplicates, delay, and out-of-order events must either converge automatically or produce an explicit gap requiring rebuild—never silently invent status.
- **AT-013-05 — split-source detection:** if a legacy/adapted table disagrees with canonical WorkItemStatus, the system must report the discrepancy and rebuild the projection from canonical state.

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** choosing adapted Kanban authority would directly contradict an accepted fixed decision.

- ADR-0003 reserves legal WorkItemStatus transitions to Work Management at [ADR-0003:41](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:41).
- ADR-0006’s `DEC-RANEX-009` already selects Work Management authority over board authority at [ADR-0006:98](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:98).
- ADR-0007 prohibits shared mutable models and cross-context table access at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155).
- ADR-0012 cannot treat a working board as compensation for an unresolved implementation-start obligation at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-058` through `-065` constrain how canonical events are identified, stored, enforced, and tested; none grants authority to a read projection: [ADR-0013:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [ADR-0013:772](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:772), [ADR-0013:782](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:782), [ADR-0013:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3 explicitly says other projections are rebuilt or reconciled from canonical events at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

The correct administrative action is to bind `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-013` to `DEC-RANEX-009` and the tests above.

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you accept Kanban as a rebuildable Work Management projection, or do you intend to supersede `DEC-RANEX-009` and make a board table authoritative?**

---

## 3. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-017` — Electron/desktop applications

### Recommendation

**Remove and keep excluded all Electron and desktop bootstrap, packaging, updater, and desktop-only application surfaces. Retain only the selected local web and TUI paths.**

`DEC-RANEX-020` already settles the substantive decision. The remaining action is to bind this owner-decision row to ADR-0006 and `FF-EXCL`.

### Why, from evidence

The pending row appears at [ADR-0013:1015](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:1015). ADR-0006 explicitly records “desktop excluded,” names Electron/updater as rejected alternatives, and assigns `FF-EXCL` at [ADR-0006:186](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:186). The architecture repeats that there is no Electron, desktop bootstrap, or updater surface at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:220](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:220) and [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:3608](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:3608).

Electron’s own documentation confirms that retaining it creates a distinct lifecycle:

- Electron ships Chromium and Node.js, grants powerful filesystem/shell capabilities, and requires timely framework updates: [Electron security guidance, accessed 2026-07-30](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/security).
- Electron supports only its latest three stable major releases and follows an approximately eight-week release cadence: [Electron release timelines, accessed 2026-07-30](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/electron-timelines).
- Desktop updates require platform packaging and update metadata: [Electron update documentation, accessed 2026-07-30](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/updates).

See **Inference I-017**.

### What it costs

- No native tray, window-management, installer, auto-updater, or OS-specific desktop integration.
- No Electron-packaged offline desktop product.
- Users must use the loopback web interface, CLI, or TUI.
- Any existing desktop-only workflows must be retired or expressed through the retained interfaces.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

- **AT-017-01 — source exclusion:** repository scans must find no Electron bootstrap, preload script, renderer main process, desktop updater, or desktop-only route.
- **AT-017-02 — dependency exclusion:** lockfiles, build manifests, packages, SBOM, and runtime dependency graphs must contain no Electron or desktop-updater dependency.
- **AT-017-03 — build exclusion:** no build profile or feature flag may produce a desktop bundle; attempted activation must fail.
- **AT-017-04 — network/runtime exclusion:** runtime tests must show no desktop updater endpoint, download, or desktop-only listener.
- **AT-017-05 — retained-path parity:** local web and TUI operations must call the same public context APIs and authority kernel used by other retained surfaces.

These instantiate the existing `FF-EXCL` requirements recorded at [ADR-0006:276](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:276).

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-017` is substantively settled. Its null/default-BLOCK state conflicts with `DEC-RANEX-020` only at the catalog/procedure level.

- ADR-0003 accepts the full architecture as the normative destination at [ADR-0003:21](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:21).
- ADR-0006 fixes desktop exclusion and says changing a fixed decision requires a superseding accepted ADR at [ADR-0006:304](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:304).
- ADR-0007 keeps delivery applications free of authority and business logic at [ADR-0007:33](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:33); it does not preserve an excluded desktop application.
- ADR-0012 cannot waive the unresolved catalog binding merely because web/TUI paths work at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-058` through `-065` are kernel identity, persistence, enforcement, dependency, and test obligations; none selects a delivery surface: [ADR-0013:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [ADR-0013:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3 governs journal/current-state agreement and has no basis for retaining Electron at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you intend to supersede `DEC-RANEX-020` and accept permanent ownership of a separate desktop security, packaging, and updater lifecycle?**

---

## 4. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-019` — Governed Execution consistency context

### Recommendation

**Keep execution, gate binding, grant/permit handling, and effect intent/reconciliation as separate submodules of one strong-consistency Governed Execution bounded context, sharing one persistence authority and transaction coordinator.**

They may have separate domain files and types, but not independently persistent authority contexts. Assurance and Policy remain external contexts; Governed Execution binds immutable results from them rather than absorbing their authoring responsibilities.

### Why, from evidence

The owner row asks exactly this at [ADR-0013:1039](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:1039). The architecture already describes Governed Execution as one bounded consistency cell with four subdomains and one invariant/SQLite transaction—not four independent authorities—at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:409](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:409). ADR-0003 similarly assigns run state, gate binding, permit, and effect intent to the same authority at [ADR-0003:41](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:41).

Prior art supports drawing the strong boundary around invariants that must change together:

- Microsoft’s tactical DDD guidance, updated 2026-02-26, says aggregates protect invariants and form transactional consistency boundaries: [Tactical DDD design](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/microservices/model/tactical-domain-driven-design).
- Fowler’s dated bounded-context guidance, published 2014-01-15, confines a model’s meaning and relationships within an explicit boundary: [Bounded Context](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/BoundedContext.html).
- Cross-boundary eventual consistency is appropriate where independent aggregates do not need one invariant-preserving commit; that is why Assurance and Work Management remain separate, not why permit and effect intent should be split.

See **Inference I-019**.

### What it costs

- The four submodules cannot be independently deployed, stored, or scaled.
- Changes to their shared invariant require coordination.
- SQLite’s single-writer behavior can become a throughput constraint.
- The Governed Execution context is larger and requires strict internal dependency discipline.
- Independent persistence technologies for permit or effect intent are unavailable without superseding the consistency guarantee.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

- **AT-019-01 — one transaction coordinator:** all state-changing paths for execution, gate binding, permit, and effect intent must enter the same Governed Execution unit of work.
- **AT-019-02 — fault matrix:** terminate or fail after each write boundary. State, gate binding, permit consumption, journal, and effect intent must be all old or all new.
- **AT-019-03 — permit CAS:** concurrent consumption attempts must produce exactly one successful transition.
- **AT-019-04 — fail-closed invariant:** no effect intent may commit unless the same transaction proves a fresh compatible gate and consumes the required permit.
- **AT-019-05 — persistence ownership:** static and runtime tests must find exactly one owner for these tables and reject independent repositories that bypass the Governed Execution unit of work.
- **AT-019-06 — external ownership:** Governed Execution must be unable to author Assurance evidence/GateEvaluations or Policy HumanDecisions.

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** independently persistent subcontexts would conflict directly with accepted architecture.

- ADR-0003 identifies one authority transaction and rejects component-local gate/permit enforcement at [ADR-0003:89](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:89).
- ADR-0006’s `DEC-RANEX-010` assigns the four responsibilities to Governed Execution at [ADR-0006:106](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:106).
- ADR-0007 calls Governed Execution the specifically documented authority cell and prohibits silent cross-context transaction coordination at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155) and [ADR-0007:183](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:183).
- ADR-0012 leaves implementation-start blocked until the exact owner choice is accepted; production evidence cannot compensate at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-059` through `-063` align the aggregate, current row, atomic journal/outbox, optional event sourcing, and fail-closed enforcement inside this cell; `-058`, `-064`, and `-065` constrain its identities, dependencies, and tests: [ADR-0013:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [ADR-0013:752](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:752), [ADR-0013:772](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:772), [ADR-0013:792](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:792), [ADR-0013:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3’s current-row/journal agreement depends on those writes being produced by the same authority unit of work at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you accept the existing one-cell Governed Execution boundary, or do you intend to supersede the accepted ADRs and accept distributed consistency between execution, gate, permit, and effect intent?**

---

## 5. `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-020` — Execution event sourcing

### Recommendation

**Do not activate event sourcing for the Execution aggregate now.**

Retain the accepted hybrid:

- relational current state/version as the operational read source;
- append-only ordered journal as the replay/audit oracle;
- atomic journal, current-state, and outbox writes;
- mandatory snapshot/journal agreement checks.

The journal remains required, but it is not yet the sole authoritative write model.

### Why, from evidence

The row makes activation conditional and default-BLOCK at [ADR-0013:1051](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:1051). Promotion `-062` permits event sourcing only inside Execution and only if replay and migration tests justify it at [ADR-0013:782](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:782). ADR-0006 fixes a selective replay journal while explicitly rejecting whole-product event sourcing at [ADR-0006:122](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:122). Section 8.3 still names the current row as the operational read source and the journal as the replay/audit oracle at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

The rewritten tests are materially stronger:

- They replay persisted journal bytes and compare the result with the current snapshot at [test_journal_replay.py:1](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/replay/governed_execution/test_journal_replay.py:1) and [test_journal_replay.py:104](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/replay/governed_execution/test_journal_replay.py:104).
- They reject corrupted current events and unknown event types at [test_journal_replay.py:117](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/replay/governed_execution/test_journal_replay.py:117) and [test_journal_replay.py:142](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/replay/governed_execution/test_journal_replay.py:142).
- The resilience test starts a child process, sends `SIGKILL`, opens a fresh connection, runs integrity checks, and verifies old-or-new state plus replay agreement at [test_execution_crash_recovery.py:127](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/resilience/test_execution_crash_recovery.py:127).

A focused read-only run of the journal replay, real-process crash, and migration files passed **7/7**.

That is enough evidence for the accepted hybrid’s replay/crash controls, but not yet enough to make the event stream the sole source of truth:

- The migration test proves only idempotent creation of schema version 1 and its three tables at [test_execution_store_migration.py:11](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/migration/governed_execution/test_execution_store_migration.py:11).
- The decoder accepts exactly schema version 1 and the current event types at [execution_store.py:147](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/src/ranex_kernel/governed_execution/execution_store.py:147). No released-history upcaster chain is exercised.
- The crash wrapper executes the wrapped SQL statement before sending `SIGKILL` at [test_execution_crash_recovery.py:86](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/.claude/worktrees/kernel-tracer/tests/resilience/test_execution_crash_recovery.py:86).

Established event-sourcing systems demand more:

- Temporal Python SDK 1.30.0, released 2026-07-02, recommends replaying exported historical workflow histories and treating nondeterminism as failure: [Temporal SDK 1.30.0 workflow replay](https://github.com/temporalio/sdk-python/tree/1.30.0#workflow-replay).
- Axon Framework 4.13 requires event revisions and ordered upcaster chains because stored events must remain readable after model evolution: [Axon 4.13 event versioning](https://docs.axoniq.io/axon-framework-reference/4.13/events/event-versioning/).
- Microsoft’s Event Sourcing pattern, updated 2026-03-28, identifies schema evolution, replay compatibility, snapshots, migration, and operational complexity as explicit costs and recommends applying event sourcing selectively: [Event Sourcing pattern](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/event-sourcing).
- SQLite’s published testing methodology uses separate processes and VFS-level fault simulation to test interrupted writes and old-or-new durability; the cited page describes the 3.42.0 test posture dated 2023-05-16: [How SQLite Is Tested](https://sqlite.org/testing.html).

The supplied prior-art report identifies the same four journal/snapshot production controls—recovery authority, exact journal position, atomic updates, and verify/rebuild—at [priorart-B.md:6879](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/reviews/artifacts/2026-07-30/agent-reports/priorart-B.md:6879). The adversarial audit records those controls and warns that atomicity alone does not establish semantic integrity at [kernel-tracer-adversarial-audit.md:161](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/reviews/2026-07-30-kernel-tracer-adversarial-audit.md:161).

See **Inference I-020** and **Inference I-020-CRASH**.

### What it costs

- The relational state schema still requires migrations.
- The product must maintain and continuously verify two agreeing representations: current state and journal.
- It cannot claim that deleting every current-state row and replaying events is the complete production recovery contract.
- It loses some benefits of an event-store-only model, including arbitrary historical read-model construction and a single append-only persistence abstraction.
- A later activation will require a separate compatibility qualification rather than being obtained automatically from today’s journal.

### Predeclared acceptance tests

For the recommended **not-activated** decision:

- **AT-020-01 — explicit mode:** the accepted register must say `EVENT_SOURCING_NOT_ACTIVATED`; startup and authority transitions must require the relational current row.
- **AT-020-02 — dual agreement:** every load must verify current version, journal head, event count, and digest. Mismatch, truncation, unknown type, or digest corruption must fail closed.
- **AT-020-03 — atomic hybrid:** injected exceptions and real process termination across state, journal, and outbox writes must yield only the complete old or complete new state.
- **AT-020-04 — scope guard:** static tests must reject event-sourced repositories or event-store authority in any other context and reject treating the Execution journal as sole authority while activation is false.
- **AT-020-05 — rebuild control:** discarding a non-authoritative snapshot/projection and replaying persisted journal bytes must reproduce the expected state, while disagreement with the operational current row must be reported as corruption—not silently preferred.

Any future proposal to activate event sourcing must additionally pass, before acceptance:

- **AT-020-F01 — released-history corpus:** raw journal fixtures from every released event revision replay under the proposed code.
- **AT-020-F02 — upcaster chain:** every old revision has an ordered, deterministic migration/upcaster path, including explicit rejection of unsupported histories.
- **AT-020-F03 — behavioral equivalence:** replay proves state plus deterministic commands/effect intents/outbox consequences, not merely a digest generated by the same implementation.
- **AT-020-F04 — snapshot independence:** snapshots can be discarded and rebuilt from journal bytes, with exact event-position metadata.
- **AT-020-F05 — upgrade/rollback:** old-code/new-code, new-code/old-data, interrupted migration, and rollback matrices preserve readable history.
- **AT-020-F06 — durability faults:** VFS-level or equivalent tests cover interrupted commit, I/O errors, truncation, torn writes, and power-loss behavior.
- **AT-020-F07 — test sensitivity:** mutations to event field mapping, ordering, revision, digest, reducer behavior, or emitted intents must cause the qualification suite to fail.

### Conflicts

**Conflict finding:** “do not activate now” has no substantive conflict with the accepted architecture. Activating now would conflict with the conditional qualification requirement because migration/evolution evidence is incomplete.

- ADR-0003 permits the target architecture but does not declare the event stream the sole source of truth at [ADR-0003:21](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0003-accept-target-architecture-and-authority-kernel.md:21).
- ADR-0006 fixes a selective Governed Execution replay journal and says the product is not wholly event sourced at [ADR-0006:122](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0006-register-fixed-decisions-and-fitness-crosswalk.md:122).
- ADR-0007 requires Governed Execution to own its aggregate, tables, migrations, contracts, events, and outbox; either persistence style must preserve that ownership at [ADR-0007:155](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0007-establish-modular-ddd-repository-organization.md:155).
- ADR-0012 means passing the current replay/crash tests does not by itself declare implementation-start or production readiness at [ADR-0012:28](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0012-separate-implementation-start-and-production-readiness.md:28).
- Promotions `-058`, `-059`, `-060`, `-061`, `-063`, `-064`, and `-065` remain mandatory regardless of activation; `-062` is the conditional activation gate: [ADR-0013:742](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:742), [ADR-0013:752](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:752), [ADR-0013:762](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:762), [ADR-0013:772](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:772), [ADR-0013:782](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:782), [ADR-0013:792](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:792), [ADR-0013:802](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:802), [ADR-0013:812](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/decisions/ADR-0013-promote-hermes-research-obligations.md:812).
- §8.3’s dual current-row/journal contract is satisfied by non-activation and would need explicit supersession if an event store became sole authority at [HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470](/home/soultransit/devtony/ranex/docs/architecture/HERMES_GROUND_ZERO_FULL_SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md:470).

### What genuinely remains the owner’s call

**Do you accept “event sourcing not activated” for implementation start, or do you want to fund and wait for the full historical-evolution and durability qualification above before deciding?**

## Inferences

- **INFERENCE I-003:** The smallest boundary satisfying all accepted invariants is the Governed Execution authority tuple only. Adding Assurance or Work Management writes would enlarge the transaction beyond the accepted consistency boundary without evidence that their canonical state must commit atomically.
- **INFERENCE I-013:** Because canonical WorkItemStatus already has one owner and §8.3 requires derived projections to be rebuildable, adapting selected Kanban tables as authority would create an unnecessary split source of truth.
- **INFERENCE I-017:** `HERMES-OWNER-DECISION-017` is not an open product-design decision. It is an unresolved catalog binding to an already accepted exclusion.
- **INFERENCE I-019:** Execution, gate binding, permit consumption, and effect intent belong in one consistency cell because failure to change any one atomically can authorize an effect that the other records do not justify.
- **INFERENCE I-020:** The current tests justify the hybrid journal/current-state contract, but do not yet justify making the journal the sole authoritative write model.
- **INFERENCE I-020-CRASH:** The child-process test proves abrupt process death at selected SQL-call boundaries. Because the wrapper calls the wrapped SQL statement before killing the process, its `COMMIT` case does not prove interruption inside SQLite’s low-level commit or power-loss path.
- **INFERENCE I-MINIMAL:** None of the five recommendations requires a new bounded context, artifact type, or dependency. Four require binding existing accepted decisions; the fifth records non-activation under an already declared conditional gate.

## Unverified

- **UNVERIFIED:** interrupted low-level SQLite commit, torn-write, filesystem I/O-error, and power-loss behavior. The current crash harness does not provide VFS-level fault injection.
- **UNVERIFIED:** replay of histories written by a previously released event schema. The examined migration test covers schema version 1 creation, not event-revision migration or upcasters.
- **UNVERIFIED:** deterministic replay equivalence for emitted commands, effect intents, or outbox records. The current journal test proves reconstructed state/digest agreement.
- **UNVERIFIED:** the complete `feature/kernel-tracer` test suite. I ran only the three focused replay, crash-recovery, and migration files; they passed 7/7.
- **UNVERIFIED:** implementation-start or production-readiness status. The focused test result cannot establish either ADR-0012 tier.
